## Implementation Notes

- Data layer (decision of 2026-11-11): Polars is the one tabular data layer. From task 1.14 on, tabular data crosses crate boundaries as Polars frames with the schemas in the design's "Data frames" section; tasks written before that decision that speak of records or lists (1.7, 1.9, 1.10, 1.12, 2.7, 3.7) take and return those frames. Numeric code gets slices, arrays or sparse rows from the frames in row order and stays out of Polars' thread pool.
- Memory-aware batching (decision of 2026-11-11): requirement 12, task 1.15. Every operation that loads a dataset plans its batch size from the free memory first; operations that need all rows at once (baseline fit, calibration, shift test, pairwise assessments) call the whole-dataset check before loading. Later tasks (1.7, 1.12, 2.6, 2.7, 3.x) use it; encoding for the NPU runs in images of 64 rows (task 2.6) that are fed from these data batches.
- Conformal layer (decision of 2026-11-11): the `conformal-prediction` crate is adopted and wrapped in `l2d-decide` (task 1.13, requirement 11). Superseded on 2026-11-12: the owner had the crate replaced by own code (task 1.18); the tool no longer depends on it.

- 1.1: Build output relies on the host's `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`; `l2d-core/build.rs` only warns if output lands in the repo. Run `cargo fmt --all` before each commit.
- 1.1: `Placement` and `PlacementSource` live in `l2d-core` (the core `RunRecord` needs them); `l2d-encoder` re-exports them and must not redefine them.
- 1.1: `Label` serializes in lower case (`"good"`, `"bad"`, `"unknown"`); keep the CLI JSON consistent with that.
- 1.1: `l2d-train` is a workspace member outside `default-members`; `l2d-cli` and `l2d-eval` do not depend on it.
- 1.1: Store location is undecided in the design (`stores/<name>.db` at the technology stack, `stores/<name>/` in the file plan). Task 1.8 uses `stores/<name>.db` and adds the path helper to `ProjectPaths`.
- 1.2: Unwired commands exit 4 (`Refused`, "not implemented yet"); with `--json` the error envelope `{"error":{"kind","message","exit_code"}}` goes to standard output and the one-line diagnostic to standard error. Stubs declare no options yet.
- 1.2: Fixture `tests/fixtures/mini-labelled.csv` (header `text,label,group`): 200 data rows = 199 well-formed (115 good, 84 bad) + 1 malformed at data row 121 (two fields only, group missing); 169 distinct texts (96 good-only, 72 bad-only, 1 conflicting); 27 texts repeat; 8 group keys `METHOD /path`, each with 12 good and 9 bad distinct texts. Exact counts are pinned by `crates/l2d-cli/tests/fixture.rs`.
- 1.2: The malformed row is malformed only by field count. A reader must treat a wrong field count as a skipped record (expected: 200 read, 1 skipped at position 121, 169 distinct, 1 unknown label); a lenient CSV reader would wrongly accept it.
- 1.2: One group key holds only 21 to 22 distinct texts, below the 30 minimum of the shift test; fixture shift tests must compare good against bad, not group against group.
- 1.3: Input code is in `l2d-core`: `input.rs` (`InputAdapter`, `Dedup`, `read_source`, `content_sha256`, `strip_bom`, `split_lines`) and `adapters/{lines,labelled}.rs`. New adapters reuse `Dedup` and `read_source`; they do not reimplement deduplication.
- 1.3: Position is the 1-based record index: line number for lines and JSONL, data-row index without the header for CSV. CSV blank lines take no position, so `records` counts CSV data rows, not file lines; `l2d ingest` must not present it as a line count.
- 1.3: Labels: `good`/`bad` case-insensitive; empty, `unknown`, null or absent means unlabelled and never conflicts; any other value skips the record. On duplicate text the first group key is kept. Any read failure maps to `Missing`.
- 1.3: `LabelledAdapter::new` defaults the label column to `Some("label")`; unlabelled CSV/JSONL needs `label_column = None` set by the caller (1.11).
- 1.3: A CSV data row starting with an unbalanced quote absorbs the following rows into one skipped record (standard CSV behaviour). Adapters for real datasets must check the total row count against the dataset's known size.
- 1.3: Open follow-ups, not blocking: no test pins that the content hash covers raw bytes including a byte-order mark; input tests leave scratch directories in the system temp folder.
- 1.7: `l2d_baseline::scorer::BaselineScorer`: `fit(&LogsFrame, &[InputSummary], &BaselineSettings)`, `fit_with(.., &MemoryBudget)`, `ratio`, `score(&LogsFrame)` and `score_texts` (Scores frame: `text`, `scorer` = "baseline", `ratio` Float32, `abstained` false, `no_features`), `save`, `load`, `load_under`, `was_fitted_on(hash)`, `has_known_fitting_inputs()`, `fit_report()`, `gradient_converged()`. Settings: buckets, ngram_max, routing, l2, max_iter (1,000), tolerance (1e-3), all in the revision.
- 1.7: For 1.13: refuse calibration when `was_fitted_on(input hash)` is true, and when `has_known_fitting_inputs()` is false (unknown provenance). Fitting inputs are merged on re-save of the same revision; they are outside the revision, so a hand-edited list is not detected; two processes saving the same model at the same moment can lose one added entry (no lock, accepted for a single-analyst tool).
- 1.7: For 1.10 and 1.11: rows flagged `no_features` all tie at one constant ratio and carry no evidence; triage must decide how to place them (in strip mode 1,634 of 20,825 CSIC fitting rows have no features). Pass the budget used for reading to `fit_with`, so the logs frame counts against the limit. Use `load_under` in commands. A project root written with `..` is refused on save; commands pass a normalized root. A fit in which every row is featureless is not refused.
- 1.7: For 1.12: a whole-CSIC fit takes 5 to 15 s in release and about 96 s in debug; run the evaluation in release. Sanity run with three path groups held out (4,783 rows): ROC AUC strip 0.989, keep 0.996, isolate 0.998. No held-out payload occurs in the fitting rows, but 28 % of held-out bad rows contain a parameter value seen only in bad fitting rows (CSIC reuses a small attack dictionary). Held-out good rows average 0.65 to 0.68, so a fixed 0.5 threshold does not transfer across endpoints; calibration per group matters. 1,604 of 1,632 groups are bad-only.
- 1.7: On-request release tests: `cargo test --release -p l2d-baseline --test scorer_csic -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads=1`. Open follow-ups: no failure-injection test for the atomic save steps; the memory estimate has a thin margin (heap peak 0.95 of the estimate on CSIC keep mode).
- 1.15: `l2d_core::memory`: `MemoryBudget::{observe, observe_with, plan, next_batch_rows, require_whole, require_held, hold, usable_bytes, left_bytes}`, `Batcher`, `BatchCost { expansion, row_bytes, batch_bytes }`, `BatchPlan` (Display, `RunRecord::record_batch_plan` under `settings["batch_plan"]`), `BatchChange { cause, available_bytes, .. }`, `parse_memory_size`, `PROCESS_BASELINE_BYTES` (32 MiB). Free memory via `windows-sys` `GlobalMemoryStatusEx`; reading injectable.
- 1.15: Adapter contract: required `read_from(source, &mut MemoryBudget) -> (LogsFrame, InputSummary, BatchPlan)`; provided `read_with(path, budget)` and `read(path)`. `Csic2010Adapter::read_counted_with`. `Source` reads 64 KiB pieces and hashes incrementally; `read_source`, `strip_bom`, `split_lines` no longer exist. Batches under 4 MiB are parsed by Polars on one thread; `Dedup::push` copies text and group strings out of the parse buffers.
- 1.15: Memory limit is kept in reality (reviewer measured peak working set at most 0.78 of the limit over 12 shapes, completed or refused). Operations that hold memory must declare a `BatchCost` and count what they keep through `hold`; operations needing all rows call `require_whole` first. Constants (parse 6.0 x bytes + 500 per row + 4 MiB per batch; held 200 per row + 2.6 x estimated size; baseline 32 MiB) are calibrated on this host with Polars 0.52.0: re-run `cargo test --release -p l2d-core --test memory_limit -- --ignored --nocapture` (about 2.5 min) on any Polars change and at feature validation.
- 1.15: The accounting is cautious: reads can be refused that would have fitted (2,000,000 distinct 120-byte lines peak at about 830 MiB but need a limit of about 1.2 GB). 1,024-row batches are slow at scale (about 47 to 60 s against 3 s for 2,000,000 distinct lines). Follow-up: a deduplication that does not copy its state.
- 1.15: For 1.11: no command prints the batch plan yet; `--memory-limit` at or below 32 MiB is only rejected when a budget is observed, so wired commands must observe the budget before doing anything else; a limit between 32 and about 36 MiB gives a misleading refusal ("record larger than 1 B") and should say the limit leaves nothing for a batch. `--memory-limit` units are multiples of 1,024.
- 1.15: `crates/l2d-core/tests/no_network_crates.rs` fails if `Cargo.lock` names a network client crate. `crates/l2d-core/examples/read_batched.rs` reads a file with a given limit and batch size for memory measurements.
- 1.13: `l2d_decide::conformal::ConformalCalibrator`: `fit(&DataFrame, ScorerKind, scorer_revision, &CalibrationSettings)`, `fit_with(.., &MemoryBudget)`, `fit_baseline(&BaselineScorer, &LogsFrame, &[InputSummary], &CalibrationSettings, &MemoryBudget)`, `predict(ratio, group, error_rate) -> Calibrated`, `require_error_rate`, `require_scorer`, `smallest_supported`, `strata`, `good`, `bad`, `save`, `load`, `load_under`; `require_held_out(scorer, inputs)`, `points_frame`, `points_schema`, `estimated_bytes(points, group_bytes)`, `DEFAULT_ERROR_RATE` (0.05), `DEFAULT_MIN_PER_GROUP` (100), `EXCHANGEABILITY_NOTE` (the sentence of requirement 11.9 for 1.11). `l2d_decide::ScorerKind` (`baseline`, `neighbour`) is defined at the crate root; 1.10 uses it. `ProjectPaths::{calibrations_dir, calibration_dir}` were added to `l2d-core`.
- 1.13: `conformal-prediction` is pinned `=2.0.1`. The crate covers: keeping the calibration scores of a list and the p-value `(count of scores >= candidate + 1) / (n + 1)` with ties counted (`ConformalPredictor::calibrate` and `predict`); a unit test compares it with a direct count. The wrapper adds: the nonconformity of a ratio for a label (the crate's measures are for regression; its `NonconformityMeasure` hook passes the wrapper's value through), one list per label and per group, the choice of list, the label set at an error rate chosen per call (the crate fixes one level per predictor, so predictors are created at the smallest positive level and return every p-value), the refusal rule, the check against the fitting inputs, the memory check, saving and loading. The crate's `cpd`, `pcp` and `streaming` modules are not used; they are the only places that draw random numbers.
- 1.13: Dependencies: `Cargo.lock` grows by `conformal-prediction`, `lean-agentic` 0.1.0 and a second `ndarray` (0.17.2), to 199 packages. `rand` 0.8.8 with `getrandom` 0.2 was in the lock for the tests of `l2d-baseline` only and is now linked into the tool, unused. `lean-agentic` has no dependencies and no file, process or network access, but its arena, symbol table and environment are created for every list. No network crate; the guard test passes. Results are identical on repeat within and across processes. The owner has not reviewed these crates.
- 1.13: Deviations from the design contract: `fit` takes the calibration points frame, not `&[CalibrationPoint]` (data layer decision); `Calibrated` has `good_stratum` and `bad_stratum` in place of one `stratum`, because a group can have its own list for one label and the pooled list for the other (requirement 11.4 is per label); `CalibrationError` has more variants (`UnknownFittingInputs`, `UnknownCalibrationInputs`, `InvalidErrorRate`, `InvalidRatio`, `InvalidPoints`, `InvalidSettings`, `OtherScorer`, `Memory`, `Scorer`, `Internal`). The design text should be brought in line.
- 1.13: Rules to know. An error rate below `1 / (n + 1)` of the shortest list in use (pooled or a group's) is refused for every log, with the lengths of the shortest good and bad lists; a calibration without good or without bad logs is refused at fit. P-values and error rates are compared as 32-bit floats, and `Calibrated` reports those values. Rows labelled `unknown` are ignored. The pooled list of a label holds the logs of all groups. Saved as `calibration.json` and `scores.bin` (sorted nonconformity lists as little-endian doubles); the revision covers scorer kind and revision, `min_per_group` and the lists, and does not depend on the order of the calibration logs. Calibration inputs are not stored with the calibration.
- 1.13: For 1.10, 1.11, 1.12: the provenance check compares content hashes of whole inputs. Folds cut from one file all carry that file's hash, so `fit_baseline` refuses them; the evaluation harness keeps its parts disjoint itself and calls `fit_with` with the points. Call `require_scorer` before judging ratios and `require_error_rate` once before a batch. A decision scans the lists it uses (the crate has no sorted lookup), so its time grows with the number of calibration logs: 4,783 decisions against 5,254 calibration logs took about 0.01 s in release; large triage runs against large calibrations were not measured. A scorer's abstention has no ratio and must not be passed to `predict`.
- 1.13: Measured on CSIC 2010 at error rate 0.05 (release, three mixed path groups held out, 4,783 logs of which 4,453 are one endpoint, `/tienda1/publico/anadir.jsp`): the target is missed for good logs on held-out endpoints. Calibrated on other logs of the endpoints the scorer was fitted on: good logs wrongly ruled out 79.1 % (keep) and 56.7 % (strip), bad logs 0.0 %. Calibrated on two endpoints kept from the scorer: good 32.0 % (keep) and 31.9 % (strip), bad 0.0 %. On logs of the seen endpoints, which are exchangeable with the calibration logs, the rate is reached: good 5.2 % and 5.1 %, bad 4.5 % and 4.6 %. Cause: the scorer's ratios shift on unseen endpoints (good logs average 0.39 on calibration and 0.69 held out), which is the exchangeability condition failing; no setting of the calibration layer repairs it. From the same run (confirmed by the reviewer): on held-out endpoints no log receives the set "good" in any of the four arrangements, so calibration scores nothing down there; a higher error rate makes the good-log miss worse (70 % to 99.6 % at 0.1, 99.6 % to 100 % at 0.2); the failure is on the safe side, bad logs being missed 0.0 % at 0.05 and at most 0.5 % at 0.2. The pooled list also holds the logs of groups that have their own list, so a log of a small or unseen group is judged against a mixture dominated by the large groups. Command: `cargo test --release -p l2d-decide --test conformal_csic -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads=1` (about 8 s).
- 1.13: Memory: `fit_with` asks `require_whole` for `1 MiB + 200 bytes per point + 2 bytes per byte of group key`; the heap peak was at most 69 % of that in the first runs and 73 to 80 % in the runs made for task 1.18 on the same code (it varies by run, the peak being Polars' group-by), over seven shapes (the worst is a group per point) in `tests/conformal_csic.rs`, which asserts it on every run; the points frame itself is the caller's and is not in the estimate. `save` and `load` take no budget. A loaded calibration is not transient: it keeps about 16 bytes per stored value for its lifetime, and while loading the peak is roughly 24 to 40 bytes per value of the list under construction (reviewer's reading of `List::new`, not measured). Tasks 1.10 and 1.11 must count a loaded calibration against the memory budget (requirement 12.9). Open follow-ups: no failure-injection test for the atomic save; the group-key check on load does not verify that a group list is a subset of the pooled list. For 1.11 (provenance gaps): the same logs in a file with different bytes (reordered, a subset, re-exported) pass the whole-input hash check; nothing binds the `inputs` argument of `fit_baseline` to its `logs`; `require_held_out` takes a `BaselineScorer` only, so the neighbour scorer needs an equivalent. Test weaknesses noted by the reviewer: the memory-refusal test in `tests/conformal.rs` does not assert the estimate or the limit it names; the abstained-row filter in `fit_baseline` is never exercised; the synthetic tolerance of 0.03 at 0.05 is loose.
- 1.18: The `conformal-prediction` crate is gone (owner decision of 2026-11-12, which reverses the decision of 2026-11-11 above); the 1.13 notes that describe the crate are history. `conformal.rs` now computes the p-value itself: each list is kept once, sorted ascending (the form it is saved and hashed in), the number of values at least the new log's is the list length less `partition_point(value < candidate)` (binary search; ties count, nothing is random), and the p-value is `(count as f64 + 1.0) / (n as f64 + 1.0)` rounded to `f32`, the crate's arithmetic in the crate's order. The public contract is unchanged: same types, signatures, error variants and messages; only the doc text of `CalibrationError::Internal` changed (it is now Polars failing, or lists that do not have the shape of a calibration). `predict` can no longer return `Internal`. Revisions and saved files are unchanged.
- 1.18: Dependencies: `Cargo.lock` goes from 206 to 203 packages; `conformal-prediction` 2.0.1, `lean-agentic` 0.1.0 and `ndarray` 0.17.2 left, nothing else changed. `rand` 0.8.8 (with `rand_chacha` 0.3.1, `rand_core` 0.6.4) stays in the lock file through `linfa`, a test-only dependency of `l2d-baseline`, and is no longer linked into the tool (`cargo tree -i rand@0.8.8 -e all`). `getrandom` 0.2.17 is still linked into the tool through Polars, as before 1.13. The tool still links `rand` 0.9.5 and `ndarray` 0.16.1 through Polars and the regression, as before 1.13.
- 1.18: Proof of equality: `crates/l2d-decide/tests/fixtures/conformal-1.13/` holds files written by the crate-based code before it was changed: a saved calibration (`saved/crate-2.0.1-2a1be4d97ed8/`, 180 calibration logs, six lists: both pooled lists, a group with a list for each label, a group with a list for good only, a group with a list for bad only, ties, both zeros, the smallest positive ratio, the largest below 1, and 1), its revision, and `decisions.tsv` with 250 ratio and group pairs at six error rates (p-values as `f32` bit patterns, lists, label sets) and 36 refusals (error rate too small, not an error rate, not a ratio). `tests/conformal_fixture.rs` loads the saved calibration, fits the same points again in both orders, and requires the same revision, bit-identical p-values, identical sets, lists and refusal texts, and byte-identical files on saving. The manifest names the tool version (`0.1.0`), so the byte comparison of `calibration.json` puts the present version in that one place. The fixture directory is stored without line-ending conversion (`tests/fixtures/.gitattributes`; the host has `core.autocrlf` on). To regenerate: the reference is the crate-based code, so check out the last commit whose `Cargo.lock` names `conformal-prediction`, copy `tests/conformal_fixture.rs` into it, set `L2D_CONFORMAL_FIXTURE_OUT` to an empty directory, run `cargo test -p l2d-decide --test conformal_fixture -- --ignored regenerate`, and replace the fixture directory with what was written. On the present code the same command only reproduces the committed files.
- 1.18: Measured (release). CSIC evaluation, same command as in the 1.13 note: every printed figure is the same before and after (good wrongly ruled out 79.05 % keep and 56.67 % strip on the fitted endpoints, 32.02 % and 31.90 % with two endpoints kept from the scorer, bad 0.00 %; control 5.21 % and 5.06 % good, 4.52 % and 4.62 % bad); the only lines that differ apart from timings are three `heap_peak` values of the calibration (0.86, 0.97, 0.82 MiB before; 0.84, 0.73, 0.86 MiB after), which vary from run to run. Speed of a decision (`cargo test --release -p l2d-decide --test conformal_fixture -- --ignored decision_speed --nocapture`, 20,000 decisions, no groups): against 5,000 calibration logs 0.91 microseconds before and 0.05 after; against 200,000 calibration logs 32.8 microseconds before and 0.08 after. A decision no longer grows with the number of calibration logs in any way that matters, which settles the open point of the 1.13 note for large triage runs.
- 1.18: Memory: `estimated_bytes` is unchanged (`1 MiB + 200 bytes per point + 2 bytes per byte of group key`) and so are the heap assertions. The heap peak of a calibration as a share of the estimate over the seven shapes of `tests/conformal_csic.rs`: 27, 50, 77, 44, 57, 22, 35 % before; 28, 49, 73, 44, 58, 22, 26 % after, and 27, 51, 67, 44, 56, 22, 22 % on a repeat. The peak is the counting in Polars, which runs on several threads and varies by a few points from run to run, so it hardly moved; the worst shape is still a group per point. What did change is what a calibration keeps: 8 bytes per stored value (one sorted list) where the 1.13 note says about 16, and no second copy while a list is built. The kept figure follows from the representation; it was not measured separately.
- 1.10: `l2d_decide::triage` (new `triage.rs`) and `l2d_decide::scorer` (new `scorer.rs`). `triage(&LogsFrame, &mut [&mut dyn Scorer], &TriageSettings) -> TriageResult` observes the host; `triage_with(.., &mut MemoryBudget)` takes the caller's budget (commands use this one, with the budget the logs were read under). `TriageSettings { primary: ScorerKind, review_budget: Option<u64>, calibration: Option<TriageCalibration { calibrator, error_rate }> }`. `TriageResult`: `frame()`, `into_frame()`, `review()`, `scored_down()`, `abstained()`, `list(TriageList)`, `primary()`, `scorers()`, `review_budget()`, `counts()` (`TriageCounts { logs, review, scored_down_by_calibration, scored_down_by_budget, scorer_abstained, uncertain, unlike_calibration }`), `calibration()` (`CalibrationUse { revision, error_rate, note, held_bytes, good: ListUse { group, pooled }, bad, set_good, set_bad, set_both, set_neither }`), `plan()` (the `BatchPlan`, for requirement 12.3), `held_bytes()`. `TriageList`, `AbstainReason`, `ScoredDownBy` with `as_str`; the small structs are `Serialize` for `--json`, the frame is not. `TriageError` (`InvalidScorers`, `Calibration`, `Memory`, `Scorer`, `Scores`, `Internal`) converts to `L2dError` (exit 2, the calibration's own, 4, the scorer's own, 5, 5).
- 1.10: The triage frame: one row per log in the order of the logs frame; the three lists are views of it. Columns: the five of the logs frame (shared with the caller's frame, not copied), then `scorer` String (the primary's name, requirement 6.6), `ratio` Float32 (null where the scorer abstained), `abstained` Boolean, `list` String (`review`, `scored_down`, `abstained`; never null), `rank` UInt32 (place in the review ranking from 1, before the budget cuts it; null for a log that is not ranked), `scored_down_by` String (`calibration`, `budget_only`; null unless scored down), `abstain_reason` String (`scorer_abstained`, `uncertain`, `unlike_calibration`; null unless abstained), `label_set` String (`good`, `bad`, `both`, `neither`), `p_good`, `p_bad` Float32, `good_stratum`, `bad_stratum` String (`group`: the list of the row's own group; `pooled`); these five are present always and null without a calibration and where the scorer abstained. Then `ratio_<kind>` Float32 for every scorer beside the primary (null where it abstained) and the primary scorer's evidence columns (the baseline's `no_features`); a triage of no logs has no evidence columns. Why one frame with a list column and not three: "every log is in exactly one list" is then the shape of the data (one value of `list` per row), and evaluation (1.12) joins it to the labels by row. The lists in reading order: review by rank; scored down by rank for the budget's, then the calibration's by ratio, highest first; abstained in the order of the logs frame.
- 1.10: Rules. Tie rule: equal ratios keep the order of the logs frame, the earlier row first (the two zeros are one ratio); the same rule cuts a tie at the budget. The budget counts logs (rows, distinct texts), not occurrences; 0 sends nothing to review; without a budget every ranked log is in review and nothing is scored down by the budget. Without a calibration every log with a ratio is ranked. With one: set bad is ranked, set good is scored down by calibration (rank null), both is abstained as `uncertain`, neither as `unlike_calibration`; the budget applies to the ranked logs only, so a log scored down by the budget has the set bad and one scored down by calibration the set good. A log the scorer abstained on gets no label set and is abstained as `scorer_abstained`, with or without a calibration. Logs of endpoints the calibration does not cover are placed like any other (they use the pooled lists, as logs without a group do); no ratio is normalised per endpoint. Decision on the 1.7 question: the baseline's featureless logs are placed like any other log; they all have one ratio, so the tie rule orders them by the logs frame, and `no_features` in the frame lets 1.11 mark them. Refused before a log is scored: no scorer, two of one kind, a primary that is not among them, a calibration for another scorer or revision than the primary (`require_scorer`), an error rate it does not support, a memory limit that does not leave triage's share or the smallest batch.
- 1.10: The `Scorer` contract (for 2.8 and 2.10): `kind()`, `revision()` (a calibration is tied to it), `cost(&LogsFrame) -> ScoringCost { fixed_bytes, row_bytes, evidence_row_bytes, evidence_columns }` and `score(&mut self, &LogsFrame) -> DataFrame`, the Scores frame for the rows given, in their order: `text` (equal to the logs frame's), `scorer` (the kind's name on every row), `ratio` Float32 (null where abstained, else a number in 0 to 1), `abstained` Boolean, then evidence columns that keep names, types and order from batch to batch, do not take the name of a triage column, are String, Boolean or numeric (anything else, a list per log for instance, is refused as `TriageError::Scores`) and are no more than `evidence_columns` states. `check_scores` holds a frame to this; triage and `fit_scorer` call it on every batch. `impl Scorer for BaselineScorer` is in `l2d-decide`; `BaselineScorer::scoring_bytes(longest_text_bytes)` was added to `l2d-baseline` for its cost. `score` gets a batch and may be called many times; the neighbour scorer encodes and searches inside it. Nothing in triage matches on `ScorerKind`, so `Linear` only needs its name in `as_str` and `from_name` and its number in the calibration revision (`scorer_code` in `conformal.rs`).
- 1.10: Memory (requirement 12). Triage scores in batches from `MemoryBudget::plan` and `next_batch_rows` (free memory read before each batch; the plan with its changes is in the result) at the largest `row_bytes` of the scorers per row, and the result is the same for every batch size (tested with batches of 1 to 100,000 rows, and on CSIC with 1,024). Beside the batches it holds, on top of what the caller's budget holds and until it returns: 256 bytes per log (`RESULT_ROW_BYTES`: what it keeps per log and the triage frame), 16 per log and further scorer, three times the primary's `evidence_row_bytes` per log, 48 KiB per evidence column it states (`EVIDENCE_COLUMN_BYTES`), every scorer's `fixed_bytes` (baseline: 40 bytes x `ngram_max` x the longest text), and the calibration's `held_bytes()`. The caller loads the calibration and must not also count it. The budget comes back holding exactly what it held (`MemoryBudget::held_bytes()` was added to `l2d-core` for that; `l2d-store` still rebuilds the figure as usable less left in `add_with` and the searches, which loses an over-held figure: a follow-up there).
- 1.10: Evidence and the batch size (review round 1, the blocking finding). Kept as stacked Polars frames, a String evidence column held the 8 KiB block of every batch alive, also after the pieces were merged: with 20,000 logs and batches of 2 rows the heap was 13.8 times what triage had asked (87.8 MB against 6.3 MB; 4.4 times with batches of 7). Now flags and numbers are kept in at most 128 pieces that are merged as they accumulate (fixed-width pieces merge into one new block and free the old ones), and words are copied out of each batch into one text per column, with where each value ends and whether it is null, from which the column is built once after the last batch. Counted: three times `evidence_row_bytes` per log (words take up to three times their bytes while the text grows and while the column is built) and 48 KiB per column. Measured after the fix (debug, default run, `evidence_in_words_stays_within_what_triage_asked_at_every_batch_size`, 20,000 logs, heap peak over what was asked): 13-byte words 0.47 in one batch and 0.63 at batches of 1, 2, 3, 7, 16 and 64, 0.62 at 1,024; 200-byte words 0.42 and 0.64 at every one of those sizes, 0.62 at 1,024; two columns of 40 bytes, one of them null on two rows of three, 0.33 and 0.48 to 0.49. The peak is the same number of bytes at every batch size. The String columns triage writes itself (`scorer`, `list`, the reasons, `label_set`, the strata) are each built once after the last batch and do not accumulate; a run in which every log has the 16-byte reason `scorer_abstained` peaks at 0.52 and 0.70 of what was asked. Not covered: the scores frame of a batch itself is the scorer's (`fixed_bytes` and `row_bytes`): a String column in it costs at least 8 KiB per batch whatever the rows, which a scorer must put in `fixed_bytes` (the test scorer states 64 KiB).
- 1.10: What a calibration keeps: `ConformalCalibrator::held_bytes()` = 8 bytes per value of every list (a log of a group with its own list is in two lists), the struct, about 56 bytes per list, and per group list its name twice plus 96 bytes (`GROUP_INDEX_ENTRY_BYTES`) for its index entry. Measured on the heap of a loaded calibration (`a_calibration_keeps_what_it_says_it_keeps`, default run): the heap is 1.000 of the figure for two lists of 20,000 values (320,216 of 320,360 bytes), 0.960 for 400 values, 0.985 for 300 groups with names of 200 bytes, 0.900 for 2,000 groups of 20 logs (the index entry is counted at its largest); never above it. A fitted calibration keeps 372 bytes more than a loaded one in that test (Polars' threads, not the calibration). Loading takes no budget and peaks at 16 bytes per value without groups and 30 to 33 with many groups (the file and the manifest beside the lists): 1.11 should call `require_whole` with about four times the size of `scores.bin` before `load`, or accept that the load is uncounted.
- 1.10: Measured (release, `cargo test --release -p l2d-decide --test triage_memory -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads=1`, about 9 s): CSIC's 25,608 distinct logs, baseline scorer with default settings fitted on half of them (12,772, 3.6 s) and calibrated on a quarter (6,460: 2,441 good, 4,019 bad, 10 lists of groups, 101,436 bytes kept), split by a hash of the text; all logs are then triaged, the fitting and calibration logs among them, so this measures triage and is not an evaluation. Without a calibration: 0.63 to 0.73 s; heap peak 3.99 MiB of 8.01 MiB asked (0.50) in one batch and 4.00 of 6.51 (0.61) in batches of 1,024; no budget: 25,608 in review; budget 500: 500 in review, 25,108 scored down by the budget. With the calibration at 0.05: 0.69 to 0.80 s; heap peak 4.28 MiB of 8.01 (0.53), 4.29 of 6.51 (0.66) in batches of 1,024; sets: 12,620 bad (review), 7,364 good (scored down by calibration), 5,244 both (uncertain), 380 neither (unlike the calibration logs); with budget 500: 500 in review, 12,120 scored down by the budget, the rest unchanged; 23,391 logs were judged on their group's own lists and 2,217 on the pooled lists, the same for both labels. The frames of the batched runs equal those of the unbatched ones. The result kept is 3.3 MiB (about 135 bytes per log).
- 1.10: The three calibration tests of the 1.13 review. (1) `a_calibration_above_the_memory_limit_is_refused_with_estimate_and_limit` now asserts the estimate (25,048,576 bytes), the limit (33 MiB) and what is left (1 MiB) in the message, and that a limit leaving exactly the estimate is enough while one byte less is refused naming both. (2) The filter is now `conformal::calibration_points(scores, logs)`, and `fit_baseline` and the new `ConformalCalibrator::fit_scorer(&mut dyn Scorer, logs, settings, budget)` share one path after the provenance check; `logs_the_scorer_abstained_on_are_not_calibration_logs` sends a scorer that abstains on 60 of 240 logs down it and requires the calibration of the other 180, and `a_scorer_that_abstains_on_every_log_leaves_nothing_to_calibrate_on` the refusal. The path now also runs `check_scores`. (3) The synthetic tolerance is three standard deviations of calibration and test draws together, `3 sqrt(e (1 - e) (1/2,000 + 1/20,000))`: 0.0153 at 0.05 (was 0.03), 0.0211 at 0.1, 0.0281 at 0.2; measured achieved rates 0.0460 and 0.0521 at 0.05, 0.1029 and 0.1048 at 0.1, 0.2036 and 0.1981 at 0.2 (good, bad).
- 1.10: Deviations from the task and design text. (a) A frame, not `TriageResult { review, scored_down, abstained: Vec<ScoredLog> }` (data-layer decision); `ScoredLog`, `Decision` and `Evidence` are not defined: a decision is a row of the Scores frame and evidence is the scorer's columns. (b) `Scorer::score` takes a logs frame and returns the Scores frame, not `&[&str]` and `Vec<Decision>`; the trait has `revision` and `cost` in addition. (c) `triage` takes `&mut [&mut dyn Scorer]` and a settings struct, not `Box<dyn Scorer>` and positional arguments; every operation has a `_with` form for the caller's budget, as elsewhere. (d) `AbstainReason` and `ScoredDownBy` are columns of the frame, and `calibrated: Option<Calibrated>` is the five calibration columns; a stratum is `group` or `pooled`, the group being the row's own. (e) `ConformalCalibrator` gained `fit_scorer`, `held_bytes` and the free function `calibration_points`. (f) Requirement 6.9 holds by construction (triage has no store and takes the logs by shared reference); the test compares the logs frame before and after, and there is no test against a store file because triage cannot reach one. design.md lines to bring in line (numbering of 2026-11-12): 308, 309, 310 and 364 (traceability: `TriageResult.review`, `.scored_down`, `ScoredLog.by`, `AbstainReason`), 839 to 867 (the contract block), 924 to 939 (add `fit_scorer`, `held_bytes`, `calibration_points`), 941 to 945, 1076 (`l2d triage` lacks the calibration and the error rate among its options), 1102 to 1110 (add the triage frame to "Data frames").
- 1.10: For 1.11. Memory while printing: what triage held is given back when it returns, but the result is still there, and the list views `review()`, `scored_down()` and `abstained()` are new frames that nothing counts: together they cost 190 to 206 bytes per log (the reviewer's measurement), about a second copy of the triage frame. The command must itself hold `RESULT_ROW_BYTES` per log for the result plus the views it makes, for as long as it prints, and must count the calibration once (triage holds it only while it runs; the command holds it before and after, not during). A triage of no logs has no evidence columns, so the columns of the JSON output differ for an empty input; the command should say so or add the scorer's columns empty. Print `CalibrationUse::note` whenever `calibration()` is some (requirement 11.9), with the error rate and the list use per label; print the plan (12.3); mark `no_features` rows. Triage needs the logs frame in memory: a store's rows go through `LogsFrame` first (the stored rows frame has `source` and `truncated` beside these columns). For 1.12: fit per fold with `fit_with` or `fit_scorer`, triage the test side, and read `label` and `label_set` from the one frame; `rank` gives "ranked within the review budget" for the single-label groups. For 2.8: implement `Scorer`; `require_held_out` still knows the baseline only, so the neighbour scorer needs its own check before `fit_scorer`. Evidence does not come through in any shape: it must be String, Boolean or numeric columns, as many as `evidence_columns` states, with `evidence_row_bytes` covering them (16 bytes plus its bytes for a String value). The counts of requirement 6.7 fit as numbers; the nearest logs of requirement 6.2 (a list per log) do not fit as a list column: 2.8 writes them as columns per neighbour place or as one String per log, or keeps the hits frame beside the triage frame, joined by row. For 2.8 and 3.7: only the primary scorer's evidence is kept; a scorer run beside the primary contributes `ratio_<kind>` alone, so a neighbour scorer that is not the primary loses its evidence for requirements 6.2 and 6.7. Either make it the primary, or 3.7 extends triage to keep the evidence of every scorer under a prefix (the accounting is per scorer already: `evidence_row_bytes` and `evidence_columns` would be counted for each). For 3.7: `triage_with` accepts several scorers; the primary ranks and the others appear as `ratio_<kind>`. Not done or not verified: triage with a real second scorer (only a scripted one); a budget in occurrences; the cost figures of `Scorer::cost` for anything but the baseline; the baseline's `fixed_bytes` for a very long text (the CSIC maximum is 891 bytes); `load` under a budget.
- 1.11: Wired: `l2d ingest`, `l2d baseline fit`, `l2d baseline score`, `l2d calibrate`, `l2d triage` (code in `crates/l2d-cli/src/cmd/{common,ingest,baseline,calibrate,triage}.rs`; `common.rs` holds what they share: `Context`, the budget and its report, `InputArgs` and `read_input`, `find_model`, `load_scorer`, `load_calibration`). Global options, accepted before or after the command: `--json`, `--project <DIR>` (default `.`; made absolute, so models are read and written only below `<project>/models/`), `--batch-rows <N>`, `--memory-limit <SIZE>`, `--memory-share <SHARE>`, `--reserve-share <SHARE>`. Input options on all five (`InputArgs`): `--input <PATH>` (the path as given, else the same relative path below `<project>/data/`; a path with `..` is not looked up there), `--adapter auto|lines|csv|jsonl|csic2010` (auto: `.csv`, `.jsonl`, `.ndjson` as labelled data, anything else as lines; `csic2010` without `--input` reads `data/csic-2010/csic_database.csv`), `--text-column`, `--label-column`, `--no-labels`, `--group-column` (csv and jsonl only; with another adapter they are refused; no group column unless named). Per command: `ingest --limit N`; `baseline fit --name NAME [--routing keep|strip|isolate --buckets N --ngram-max N --l2 X --max-iter N --tolerance X]`; `baseline score --baseline NAME [--limit N]`; `calibrate --baseline NAME --name NAME [--min-per-group N (100) --error-rate RATE (0.05)]`; `triage --baseline NAME [--calibration NAME --error-rate RATE (0.05) --budget N --primary baseline --limit N]`. A saved model is named by its name or its directory name `<name>-<rev12>`; several revisions under one name are an analyst error that lists them. Options that are required (`--input`, `--name`, `--baseline`) are checked by the command, not by clap, so the message names the option.
- 1.11: Exit codes: 0; 2 analyst error (usage, option values, shares out of range, a typed share that cannot be honoured, shares that add up to more than 1, a share together with `--memory-limit`, a name that is not a name, a column the input lacks, a directory as `--input`, `--error-rate` without `--calibration`, a typed memory limit or share that leaves nothing for a batch); 3 missing (input, baseline scorer, calibration, project directory; the message names the item and `expected at`); 4 refused (too few labels, an input the scorer was fitted on, too few calibration logs for the error rate, a calibration for another scorer, every memory refusal, `--store`, `--primary neighbour`, the stubs); 5 runtime (I/O, Polars, writing the output). A failure prints one line on standard error and, with `--json`, the error envelope on standard output; nothing is saved. A consumer must check the exit code: `baseline score` and `triage` write rows as they go, so a run that fails after output began (the free memory fell before a later batch of `baseline score`; the output could not be written) leaves a cut-off document on standard output, followed after a line feed by the envelope on the last line of its own; the last line of standard output is always one whole JSON document, the result or the envelope, unless standard output itself was closed (then exit 5 and the line on standard error are all there is; nothing panics).
- 1.11: JSON output is one object on one line, written piece by piece (`output::JsonOut`). Common objects: `input` = `{source_path, adapter, records, distinct_texts, content_sha256, label_conflicts, skipped_count, skipped: [{position, reason}], labels: {good, bad, unknown}, group_keys}` (`records` are records, for CSV data rows, never lines); `memory` = `{free_bytes, limit_bytes, reserve_bytes, limit_share (null with --memory-limit), reserve_share, limit_set_by_analyst, operations: [{operation, rows, batches, whole_dataset_bytes}]}` where `operation` is `read`, `fit`, `score`, `held_out_check`, `calibrate` or `triage`, `rows` is what the step went through (records for `read`, logs otherwise), `batches` is the serialized `BatchPlan` of a step that works in batches (free memory, limit, reserve, bytes per row, first batch, every change, smallest and largest batch size chosen: requirements 12.3 and 12.8; these sizes are upper bounds on a batch, so a 64-row input reports 65,536 there and 64 in `rows`; the text output words it as "64 logs in batches of at most 65536 rows") and `whole_dataset_bytes` the estimate of a step that needs all rows at once; `scorer` = `{kind, name, revision, directory, settings: {buckets, ngram_max, routing, l2, max_iter, tolerance}, fitted_text_hashes (null for a scorer saved before 1.11), fitted_text_hashes_complete}`. Per command: `ingest` = `{command, input, memory}`; `baseline fit` = `{command, scorer, fit: {termination, iterations, objective, gradient_norm, gradient_converged, rows, good, bad, unknown_ignored, no_feature_rows, stored_entries, estimated_bytes}, input, memory}`; `baseline score` = `{command, scorer, input, scores: [{row, text, label, group, first_position, occurrences, scorer, ratio, abstained, no_features}], scored, rows_omitted, no_features, memory}`; `calibrate` = `{command, calibration: {name, revision, directory, scorer: {kind, revision}, min_per_group, good, bad, smallest_supported_error_rate, strata: [{label, stratum: pooled|group, group, logs}], held_bytes}, held_out: {check: per_log|per_log_partial|whole_file, logs, dropped, kept, note}, error_rate, note, scorer, input, memory}`; `triage` = `{command, primary, scorers: [scorer + primary], review_budget, calibration: null | {name, revision, error_rate, note, lists: {good: {group, pooled}, bad: {group, pooled}}, sets: {good, bad, both, neither}}, counts: {logs, review, scored_down, scored_down_by_calibration, scored_down_by_budget, abstained, scorer_abstained, uncertain, unlike_calibration, no_features}, evidence_columns, input, review: [row], scored_down: [row], abstained: [row], rows_omitted: {review, scored_down, abstained}, memory}`. A triage row always has the same 20 keys: `row` (index in input order), the five logs columns, `scorer`, `ratio`, `abstained`, `list`, `rank`, `scored_down_by`, `abstain_reason`, `label_set`, `p_good`, `p_bad`, `good_stratum`, `bad_stratum` (null where a column does not apply), `ratios` (object: ratio per further scorer by kind) and `evidence` (object: the primary scorer's evidence columns, for the baseline `no_features`). Decision for the empty input (1.10 note): evidence is a nested object and `evidence_columns` names its keys, so no row can differ in shape; for an input without logs the three lists are `[]` and `evidence_columns` is `[]`. Ratios and p-values are printed as the shortest decimal of the `f32`. The text output shows at most `--limit` rows per list (20); JSON has every row unless `--limit` is given, and says how many were left out. Log text in the text output is cut at 120 characters and control characters are shown escaped (log text is data and must not reach the terminal as control sequences).
- 1.11: Memory options (owner decision): `--memory-share` (share of the free memory used as the limit, default 0.5) and `--reserve-share` (share kept as the reserve, default 0.25) are global like `--memory-limit`, so they are on every command, stubs included. Values outside (0, 1), not numbers, `NaN` and `inf` are usage errors (exit 2). `--memory-share` together with `--memory-limit` is exit 2. "A reserve that leaves no usable memory" is implemented as: the free memory less the reserve is not above the 32 MiB the tool keeps for itself (`common::check_reserve`); exit 2 when `--reserve-share` was typed, exit 4 with the default share (then the host is short). Shares that add up to more than 1 (`Context::shares`; the tool would fall below its reserve and halve its batches before it has used its limit, so the limit could never be reached): when one share is typed and the default of the other does not fit beside it, the other is lowered to 1 minus the typed one and the output says so (`--memory-share 0.8` alone runs with a reserve share of 0.2 and `reserve_share_adjusted: true`; `--reserve-share 0.6` alone runs with a limit share of 0.4 and `limit_share_adjusted: true`; the memory line of the text output says "lowered from the default ..."); when both are typed and add up to more than 1 it is exit 2 with the largest value of each that fits beside the other; with `--memory-limit` the limit is not a share and nothing is adjusted. The check that the reserve leaves usable memory runs on the adjusted values. A typed `--memory-share` that gives a limit at or below the 32 MiB the tool keeps is exit 2 as well; with the default share the same condition is exit 4. `l2d-core`'s `MemorySettings::validate` still admits a limit share of 1 and a reserve share of 0 for library callers; the stricter range is the command line's. Every wired command observes the budget before anything else (`Context::budget`) and refuses a limit that leaves less than the 4 MiB of a parse batch plus 256 KiB for its rows, with "leaves nothing for a batch" (exit 2 for the analyst's limit), which replaces the "record larger than 1 B" refusal of the 1.15 note; so the smallest usable `--memory-limit` is 36.25 MiB.
- 1.11: Held-out check per log (owner decision). `l2d-baseline`: a fit keeps `text_hash(text)` = the first 16 bytes of the SHA-256 of the text's bytes (rule name `sha256-128`) for every row it was fitted on (labelled good or bad; rows labelled unknown are not fitted on and stay usable for calibration). New API: `text_hash`, `BaselineScorer::{has_fitted_text_hashes, fitted_text_hashes, was_fitted_on_text -> Option<bool>, held_bytes, load_bytes(dir)}`, constants `FITTED_TEXTS_FILE`, `TEXT_HASH_BYTES`, `TEXT_HASH_NAME`; `validate_name` is public. Saved form: a third file `fitted-texts.bin` (the hashes, 16 bytes each, sorted ascending, each once) and in `baseline.json` the entry `fitted_texts: {file, hash, count, sha256}`; `format` is now 2. The revision does NOT change: it still covers the settings and the model only (domain `l2d-baseline-scorer/1`), the hashes are outside it like the fitting inputs, so a scorer has the same revision with and without hashes and existing calibrations stay valid. Load reads format 1 (no entry, no file: `has_fitted_text_hashes()` false) and format 2 (entry optional); it refuses a hash file whose length, order, count or SHA-256 does not match the manifest, a file name other than `fitted-texts.bin` (never opened), another hash rule, format 1 with the entry, and any other format. Saving over the same revision merges like the fitting inputs: a directory without hashes gains those of the scorer being saved, two sets become their union (hash file replaced first, manifest second, each through a temporary file; a crash between the two after a changed union leaves a directory that no longer loads: accepted, refit). The entry carries `complete` (absent means true): a set is complete while it covers every fitting input the scorer names; hashes merged into a directory whose earlier inputs came without hashes are marked incomplete, unless the new fit was fitted on every input the directory names (same content hash, same texts) and it names one; `BaselineScorer::fitted_text_hashes_complete()` says which, and an incomplete set stays incomplete. A loaded format-1 scorer saved again stays without hashes. Fixture: `crates/l2d-baseline/tests/fixtures/scorer-1.7/` is a project root written by the code at commit `de2666a` before the change (512 buckets, fitted on the mini fixture, with `revision.txt` and the `f32` bit patterns of 171 ratios); `tests/scorer_fixture.rs` loads it, compares revision and ratios bit for bit and holds the regeneration test. Memory: 16 bytes per fitted row were added to the fit's estimate (`rows * 16`; four existing assertions in `tests/scorer.rs` were updated for the third file, format 2 and this term), `held_bytes()` states what a scorer keeps (weights 8 bytes per column, hashes 16 per text; 0.7 MiB for the default 65,536 buckets with CSIC's 11,451 fitted texts), `load_bytes` what a load takes (the weights once, the hash file once, 128 KiB; see the review-round line below).
- 1.11: `l2d-decide`: `conformal::held_out_logs(scorer, logs, inputs, budget) -> (LogsFrame, HeldOut { check: PerLog | WholeFile, logs, dropped, kept })` runs `require_held_out` unchanged (an identical input is still refused as a whole; so is a scorer without fitting inputs), then leaves out every log whose text the scorer was fitted on, whatever its label, after asking the budget for 96 bytes per log (`HELD_OUT_ROW_BYTES`; the kept frame shares the texts). A scorer without hashes gets its logs back with `WholeFile` and `HeldOut::note()` = `WHOLE_FILE_NOTE`; a scorer whose hashes are incomplete gets `PerLogPartial` and `PARTIAL_NOTE` (what it knows is left out; logs of the earlier inputs are caught only as an identical file). `ConformalCalibrator::fit_baseline` now goes through it, so library callers get the check per log too (its existing tests pass unchanged). `l2d calibrate` calls `held_out_logs`, drops the frame it read, calibrates with `fit_scorer`, then `require_error_rate(--error-rate)`, then saves; "refuses when too few remain for the error rate" is that last check and the fit's own refusal when no good or no bad log remains, and the message then starts with how many logs were left out. Nothing is saved on a refusal. For 2.8: `held_out_logs` and `require_held_out` still take a `BaselineScorer`; the neighbour scorer needs its own check before `fit_scorer` (for a store: no calibration log whose text is a stored labelled row).
- 1.11: `l2d triage`: reads logs from a file only. `--store` is declared on `triage` and `calibrate` and refused with exit 4 and the reason (no encoder before 2.8 and 2.9), as is `--primary neighbour`; 2.9 replaces these refusals. `--baseline` is required for now. Printing: `TriageResult::rows(list) -> &[IdxSize]` was added to `l2d-decide`; the command walks the triage frame through these row numbers and writes each row as it goes, so no list view (`review()`, `scored_down()`, `abstained()`) is made and the output takes no second copy. Memory order in the command: budget observed; model directories resolved (a missing model fails before the input is read); input read (the budget then holds the frame); scorer loaded after `require_whole(load_bytes)` and its `held_bytes()` added to what is held; calibration loaded after `require_whole(4 x scores.bin + 8 x calibration.json)` and NOT held; `triage_with` (holds result and calibration while it runs); afterwards the command holds `TriageResult::held_bytes()` (the result at `RESULT_ROW_BYTES` per log, scorer costs, and the calibration, once) and asks for 256 KiB plus 8 times the longest text for printing. `baseline score` scores batches that are views of the logs frame (planned with the scorer's `Scorer::cost`) and prints each batch as it is scored. The JSON output names the scorer on every row and in `scorers`; the text output names it in the head only, and its rows show rank, ratio, label set, the two p-values, the lists used, the reason and the text, not label, group, occurrences or position (JSON has all of them). The output prints `EXCHANGEABILITY_NOTE` with the error rate whenever a calibration is used (11.9), the label sets, and per label how many logs were judged on their group's own list against the pooled list; rows the baseline has no features for are marked `[no features]` and counted.
- 1.11: Measured on CSIC 2010 (release, 2026-11-13, an on-request measurement, not an evaluation: the file was cut by row, two rows in four to fit, one to calibrate, one to triage, and CSIC repeats texts across the parts, so the triage part contains fitted texts). `--memory-limit 1GB`; seconds are wall time of the whole process, peaks are the working set sampled every 20 ms. Ingest of the whole file: 61,065 records, 25,608 distinct, 1,632 group keys, 0.36 s, 74 MiB. Fit on 30,533 records (11,451 distinct: 2,824 good, 8,627 bad): 2.1 s, 118 iterations, estimate 78.6 MiB, 77.5 MiB. Score of 8,644 logs: 0.29 s, 27 MiB, 3.3 MB of JSON. Calibrate on 7,478 distinct logs: the check per log left out 914, calibrated on 2,845 good and 3,719 bad, 10 lists, 0.23 s, 28 MiB. Triage of 8,644 logs at error rate 0.05: 0.37 s, 28 MiB, 5.1 MB of JSON; sets 1,156 good (scored down by calibration), 1,680 bad (review), 5,775 both, 33 neither; good p-values from a group's own list for 4,079 logs and the pooled list for 4,565, bad 7,420 and 1,224; with `--budget 500`: 500 review, 1,180 scored down by the budget. With `--batch-rows 1024` the three lists are identical and the peak is 20 MiB. Under `--memory-limit 160MB` everything completes with the same peaks (fit 77 MiB); under `100MB` the fit is refused before loading with estimate and limit, and the other commands complete.
- 1.11: Dependencies: none new. `l2d-cli` now depends on `l2d-baseline` and `serde`, `l2d-baseline` on `sha2` (all in the workspace already); `Cargo.lock` gains three edges and no package. Tests: `crates/l2d-cli/tests/commands.rs` runs the built binary against the fixture in a temporary project (13 tests: text and JSON output, exit codes, messages, the model directory as the only place written to); `tests/cli.rs` keeps the skeleton tests for the nine operations that are still stubs.
- 1.11: For 1.12: the evaluation must not go through `l2d calibrate` (folds of one file share its hash and its texts); it keeps its folds disjoint and calls `fit_with` or `fit_scorer`. Reuse `common::{Context, read_input, InputReport, MemoryReport, Operation}` and `JsonOut` for `l2d eval`. For 1.16: `read_input` returns one logs frame; zip input needs the batch-wise form of requirement 13 and its own options beside `InputArgs`. For 2.9: `--store` on `triage` and `calibrate` is the place to wire; `find_model` works for encoders too; the store commands are still stubs that take no options. For 4.1 (feature validation): the commands hold the scorer and the result in the budget, but three things are stated and not measured against a heap counter in this task: the command's own figure for printing (256 KiB + 8 x longest text), `InputReport` (64 bytes per row while labels and group keys are counted), and `BaselineScorer::load_bytes`; the working-set peaks above are far below the limits, which says the totals hold on CSIC and nothing about inputs with very long texts or millions of rows.
- 1.11: Deviations from the task and design text. (a) `--store` and `--primary neighbour` are refused, not implemented (the task's item 6 allows it). (b) The option names `--memory-share` and `--reserve-share` are this task's choice. (c) `l2d ingest` takes the column options and `--no-labels` beside `--input` and `--adapter`; `calibrate` takes `--error-rate`; `triage` and `baseline score` take `--limit`; every command takes `--project`. (d) The revision of a scorer does not change with the hashes (the task asks to record how it changes: it does not, by design, see above). design.md lines to bring in line (numbering at commit `9ced82b`, checked after the first review): 490 (`ProjectPaths`: the root comes from `--project`), 531 to 534 (the shares are options; the range (0, 1); a sum of at most 1; the reserve rule), 585 and 586 (three files, format 2, `fitted_texts` with `complete`, the merge of hashes, the load that takes the weights once), 607 to 619 (add `has_fitted_text_hashes`, `fitted_text_hashes`, `fitted_text_hashes_complete`, `was_fitted_on_text`, `held_bytes`, `load_bytes`, `text_hash`), 948 and 949 with 954 (`held_out_logs`, `HeldOut`, `fit_baseline` leaves fitted logs out), 1095 (`l2d ingest`: the column options, output is the `input` and `memory` objects), 1096 (`baseline fit`: the settings options; `score`: `--baseline`, `--limit`), 1099 (`l2d triage`: `--input` in place of `--store` for now, `--limit`), 1100 (`l2d calibrate`: `--error-rate`, the held-out report; `--store` refused for now), 1106 (add the global options `--project`, `--memory-share`, `--reserve-share`, and that a failed run's standard output is not a valid document), and the "Data frames" table needs no change.
- 1.11: Limits of the held-out check per log. The text is hashed as the adapter read it, before any routing rule, and compared as bytes: that is right under keep, strip and isolate alike, and it means that a text differing in one byte (a trailing space, another case, another percent-encoding of the same request) is another text, by design. Under strip a calibration log with the same payload on another path is not left out, although the model sees the two alike. A text that occurred with both labels is unlabelled (`unknown`): it is not fitted on and not hashed, so it may be a calibration log if it is labelled there. The check knows the baseline scorer only.
- 1.11 (review round 1, 2026-11-13): three blocking findings fixed, with tests that failed against the code as it was. (1) Load: `BaselineScorer::load` held the weights about three times (the file, a second buffer to hash the revision, the numbers) while `load_bytes` stated twice; counted on a 4 MiB weights file: heap peak 12.58 MB against 8.40 MB stated. Now the weights are read in 64 KiB pieces straight into their final form and the revision is hashed over the pieces (`RevisionHasher`, `read_weights`; `save` and the fit's revision go over the weights the same way), and `load_bytes` is the weights once, the hash file once, eight times `baseline.json` and 128 KiB: heap peak 4.20 MB against 4.34 MB stated (`crates/l2d-baseline/tests/scorer_memory.rs`, default run). Binary with a 32 MiB weights file (`--buckets 2097152 --routing isolate`), `baseline score` and `triage` at `--memory-limit 101MB` and `72MB`: heap peak 32.2 to 32.3 MiB, working set 31 to 43 MiB, private bytes 36 MiB (the reviewer measured 106 MiB working set and 131 MiB private at 101MB before); at `66MB` both are refused before loading with a heap of 1 MiB. (2) Fit: the memory estimate hashed its sample rows before the budget was asked; with one long text the fit refused only after hashing it (counted: 67.1 MB of heap before the refusal for a 2 MiB text; the reviewer measured 277 MiB working set for an 8 MiB text). `fit_with` now asks the budget, before any row is hashed, for every term of the estimate but the entries, with the longest labelled text found by a scan that allocates nothing: the same refusals take 546 and 554 bytes of heap; the fit that fits stays at 0.27 of its estimate. Binary with one 8 MiB text at `--memory-limit 120MB` and `200MB`: `baseline fit`, `baseline score`, `calibrate` and `triage` are all refused with a heap peak of 64.2 MiB, which is the peak of reading that input alone (`ingest`: 64.2 MiB) and within the limit less the tool's 32 MiB; working set 54 to 56 MiB, private bytes 67 to 68 MiB. With `2GB` they complete at a heap peak of 265 MiB against an estimate of 984 MiB. (3) A closed output pipe with `--json` panicked in `println!`; `report_error` now writes with handled results, and the test closes the pipe after 200 bytes for `baseline score` and `triage` in both forms: exit 5, one line. Also: typed shares that cannot be honoured or add up to more than 1 are exit 2; a directory as `--input` is exit 2; `shown_text` escapes the invisible format characters (bidirectional overrides, embeddings and isolates, zero-width characters, the byte order mark, separators, tags); merged hashes carry `complete` and the held-out report says `per_log_partial`; the text output words the batch size as an upper bound. For measurements the binary has a cargo feature `heap-report` (off by default, `crates/l2d-cli/src/heap.rs`): a counting allocator and, with `L2D_HEAP_REPORT` set, one more line `heap peak: <bytes> bytes` on standard error; the heap figures of the binary above come from it.
- 1.11 (after the re-review, 2026-11-13): `baseline fit` reports the scorer as it is saved, read back after `save`, so after a merge into a directory of the earlier form it says `fitted_text_hashes_complete: false` and "text hashes saved: N (incomplete: ...)", and `calibrate` then reports `per_log_partial` (end-to-end test with the fixture directory of the earlier form and a refit on another file; that test relies on a refit giving the fixture's revision on this host). The `memory` object has `limit_share_adjusted` and `reserve_share_adjusted`. Checks: `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features` does not build at this HEAD because `l2d-encoder` has two features that exclude each other (another task removes that); the feature of this task was checked per crate with `cargo clippy -p l2d-cli --all-targets --features heap-report -- -D warnings`. Not tested end to end: a failure after JSON output has begun (only the envelope on a line of its own is unit-tested), and a scorer without hashes saved over a directory that has them (covered in `l2d-baseline` tests for the unchanged case only, not for the case that adds an input and marks the set incomplete). `shown_text` still lets variation selectors, private-use characters and U+3164 (Hangul filler) through and lets combining marks stack: cosmetic, nothing that reorders or hides text of other lines. For 4.1: the reader's margin for one huge record is thin, the re-review measured its heap peak at 0.97 of what the read asked for (task 1.15's accounting, not changed here).
- 1.10 (correction of 2026-11-13, found in the review of 1.11; the 1.10 note above is left as written): the figures "asked" in the measured line of 1.10 (8.01 MiB in one batch, 6.51 MiB in batches of 1,024) do not include the baseline's `fixed_bytes` (hashing the longest text), which triage has held since the review rounds of 1.10. The same command now prints asked 8.11 MiB (heap peak 4.00 MiB without and 4.28 MiB with the calibration, 0.49 and 0.53 of asked) and 6.61 MiB in batches of 1,024 (0.61 and 0.65); the heap peaks and the counts are unchanged.
- 1.12: GATE PASSED on 2026-11-13, narrowly and unevenly; implemented and measured, not yet reviewed (`docs/2026-11-13-baseline-gate.md`). Two definitions of the gate figure exist and which one applies is the owner's decision; in strip mode the verdict is the same under both. The approved design (Evaluator, "over the pooled test predictions") defines all held-out predictions as one ranking: 0.9140 stripped (pass), 0.8966 kept (below the bar), 0.9059 isolate. The document leads with the mean over the folds of the per-fold cross-endpoint ROC AUC (the held-out logs of the 8 URL paths with both labels), the definition the science gate document of 2026-11-12 used; that choice is the implementer's and is recorded nowhere before the run. Mean over the folds, five folds by URL path, default settings. Stripped: 0.9188 (folds 0.9749, 0.8201, 0.8191, 0.9912, 0.9887; standard deviation 0.0908; average precision 0.9446). Kept: 0.9182 (lowest fold 0.8060). Isolate: 0.9219. The two folds below 0.90 hold out `registro.jsp` and `editar.jsp`, the twin endpoints. Other ways of pooling, stripped: mean over the five large endpoints 0.9185, over all eight 0.9468, all folds as one ranking 0.9140 (kept: 0.8966, below the bar). Overall ROC AUC with the single-label paths, mean over the folds: 0.9262.

A second rule that reads the fitting side only, the highest inner ROC AUC, picks 0.1 or 0.01 in every fold; its choice per fold was computed in the first run, its summary row was added to the report after the results had been seen: stripped 0.9530 (a), 0.9550 (b), 0.9456 (c), 0.9530 (d); kept 0.9587, 0.9609, 0.9489, 0.9589. That lead holds per fold only: with all folds as one ranking the inner-ROC-AUC rule, stripped, is below the default (0.9093 against 0.9140 in a, 0.9122 against 0.9181 in b, 0.9071 against 0.9149 in d). All 1,020 fits ended as converged with the gradient criterion met, none at the cap of 1,000 iterations; mean iterations 643 at 0.01, 347 at 0.1, 147 at 1, 57 at 10, 24 at 100, the most 856 (strip, 0.01). Most of the gain of a lighter penalty is transfer between the twins: +0.083 on a twin fold when the other twin is fitted, +0.012 (0.7617 to 0.7737) when both are held out; no penalty lifts the twin fold above 0.774. The default was not changed. The evidence supports 0.1 if the owner accepts inner ROC AUC as the criterion (triage ranks by ratio); it does not under the log-loss criterion the evaluation was written with. Owner decision.

In each fold the first inner fold of the fitting side is the calibration part (whole URL paths), the calibrated scorer is the one fitted on the fitting side less that part, and it judges the held-out logs at error rate 0.05. The rate is missed for both labels on held-out endpoints. Stripped, default penalty, all folds together: (a) good logs wrongly ruled out 16.7 %, bad 11.7 %, label sets good 32.9 %, bad 56.8 %, both 10.3 %, neither 0.0 %; (b) 15.2 % and 11.3 %; (c) 34.9 % and 4.0 %; (d) 14.3 % and 13.4 %. Per group in (a): `pagar` 0.0 % and 2.0 %, `autenticar` 1.6 % and 2.3 %, `anadir` 64.6 % and 0.2 %, `registro` 10.4 % and 34.2 %, `editar` 13.1 % and 28.4 %; the 20 good logs of single-label paths 100 %, their 1,617 bad logs 0.0 %. With both twins unseen (c): 82.9 % and 84.8 % of their good logs. Unlike the 1.13 run, bad logs are missed too (the twins' bad logs are called good).

Share of them within a review budget of 10 % of a fold's held-out logs, default penalty: (a) 19.8 % stripped, 15.8 % kept; (b) 3.7 % and 10.2 %; (c) 0.0 % and 0.9 %. Within a budget of as many logs as the fold holds bad logs: 98.2 % to 100 %. Recall of all bad logs at the 10 % budget 0.149 to 0.161 (the most a tenth can hold is 0.16), at the bad-count budget 0.90 to 0.94. A tie at the budget is shared between the tied logs, which is not triage's tie rule (frame order).
- 1.12: How it is run: `cargo run --release -j 4 -p l2d-eval --example baseline_gate -- --workers 6` from the repository root (options `--data`, `--out`, `--workers`, `--memory-limit`). It writes `reports/<date>-csic-2010-baseline.json` (every figure, the settings, the dataset hash, the held-out paths of every fold), `.md` (the same as tables) and `.run.json` (times, cost, memory). The first two repeat byte for byte: two runs gave SHA-256 `a6f7ec601d754cbd671d2b610e0e049eb7c4447dde31f29d8a0d7bb772d69f8e` for the JSON. The reviewer's re-run (4 workers, `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\review-1.12\rerun\`) gave the same two hashes. Cost is a range over the three run records on disk, because the host was loaded by other work in all of them; no quiet-machine figure exists. 1,020 fits: 1,820 s and 1,697 s on 6 workers, 1,769 s on 4. One thread, default penalty, 20,486 fitting logs, in the order first record / `reports\rerun-check` / reviewer: fit stripped 2.87 / 4.31 / 4.10 s, kept 4.74 / 7.21 / 5.80 s, isolate 5.98 / 7.66 / 8.82 s; scoring stripped 210,570 / 147,380 / 167,170 logs/s, kept 112,017 / 84,708 / 63,442, isolate 109,756 / 88,858 / 66,805; first ratio 0.02 to 0.05 ms (first record). Process CPU time (the energy proxy of 9.4) was not measured: the workspace's `windows-sys` entry has no feature for it. Memory: limit 5,854 MiB (half of the free memory), each worker a share of 967 MiB against which its fits are checked, heap peak 1,072 MiB with 6 workers (18 % of the limit; 766 MiB with 4 workers in the reviewer's run) by a counting allocator.
- 1.12: Code, `crates/l2d-eval` (dependencies added: `serde_json`, `sha2`, both in the workspace already; `Cargo.lock` gains two lines under `l2d-eval` and no package). `split`: `units(groups, together) -> Vec<Unit>`, `Folds::{balanced, dealt, leave_one_out, held_out, fold_of, mask, membership_sha256}`; no label, text or random number is read. `metrics`: `roc_auc`, `average_precision`, `budget_shares`, `recall_at_budget`, `log_loss_sum`, `spread`; ties are handled as ties and the ranking figures do not depend on the order of the logs (`log_loss_sum` and `spread` add in the order given). `evaluate`: `EvalLogs`, `EvalSettings`, `summarize` (one held-out side: overall, cross-endpoint, per endpoint, single-label, budgets), `evaluate_fold`, `evaluate` and `evaluate_with_progress` (worker threads, each with a memory budget of its own), `rule_summary`, `calibration_summary`, `predictions_frame` (the design's Predictions frame). `report`: `Report`, `report_json`, `report_markdown`, `write_report`, `date_of`. Tests: `tests/split.rs` (14), `tests/metrics.rs` (12), `tests/evaluate.rs` (19, with the mini fixture end to end and the report written twice into a temporary directory).
- 1.12: Leakage, how it is enforced. `evaluate_fold` refuses a mask that puts one group key on both sides. The choice of the penalty (`choose_penalty`) and the calibration get a `Side`, a copy of the fitting-side rows, and nothing else. `score` asks the scorer `was_fitted_on_text` for every log before it is scored (the per-log check of 1.11) and stops the run on a yes. Held-out labels are read by `summarize` and by the coverage count only. Tests: every held-out label flipped and every held-out text changed leave the choice, every fit and every ratio bit-equal; a held-out log whose text was fitted on stops the evaluation. Which paths hold both labels is read from all labels: it selects the logs of a metric and, in arrangements (b), (c) and (d), how folds are dealt, and reaches no fit. Arrangement (a) reads no label.
- 1.12: For 3.7. Reuse `split` and `metrics` as they are; `summarize`, `rule_summary` and `calibration_summary` work on held-out ratios whatever produced them. `evaluate_fold` is written for `BaselineScorer` (it calls `fit_with` and `was_fitted_on_text`): a second scorer needs a fit-and-score trait in its place and its own held-out check. `ScorerKind` has no `linear` yet. Still to add for 3.7: abstention rate and the figures on non-abstained logs (the baseline never abstains), CPU seconds, the comparison of two scorers, and the `l2d eval` command (3.9), which can call `evaluate` and `write_report`. The report states three ways of pooling; the design names one.
- 1.12: Deviations and design lines that no longer match. (a) Design, Evaluator: "Metrics per scorer over the pooled test predictions". The gate figure here is the mean over the folds of the per-fold figure, as in the science gate; the pooled figure and the per-endpoint figures are reported beside it. (b) Design: `ScorerResult`, `CostMeasure` and `EvaluationReport` do not exist as structs; the report is `report::Report` written as JSON with another layout, and has no `cpu_seconds`, `abstention_rate` or `roc_auc_non_abstained`. (c) Design: `reports/<date>-<dataset>-<encoder>.json`; without an encoder the files are `<date>-csic-2010-baseline.json`, `.md` and `.run.json`. (d) Design traceability, 9.2: `grouped_folds` is `split::Folds::balanced` (and `dealt`, `leave_one_out`). (e) Design: the trigger `l2d eval` is not wired (task 3.9); the evaluation is an example binary of `l2d-eval`. (f) Predictions frame: `label_set` comes from the calibrated scorer of the fold, which is not the scorer of the `ratio` column. (g) Task text "report ... per group": per group is given for the 8 paths with both labels; the 1,624 single-label paths are reported as one group. (h) Recall at the budget shares a tie at the budget; triage breaks it by frame order. (i) `isolate` has no choice of the penalty and no calibration. (j) The evaluation runs on worker threads; the cost figures are measured on one thread before they start.

In strip mode identical payloads under different paths on the two sides of a split are not detected or counted by the held-out text check (it compares whole texts); how many there are was not measured. Other cuts of the inner folds and of the calibration part were not tried. The workspace had 498 passing and 22 ignored tests before this task (not 450 and 21), measured with `--exclude l2d-eval`.
