- 2.3: Review round 1 (2026-11-12), what changed; where this differs from the notes above, this note holds. (1) The rule of the check is now: a file is used without being hashed only when the record `provisioned.json` says it was hashed with the modification time it has now. So in a directory WITHOUT a record the fast check hashes every file (0.84 s) and, when all are right, writes the record itself: `format` 1, `release`, `onnxruntime`, `"written_by": "check"`, `verified_unix_s`, and `files` with `name`, `bytes`, `sha256`, `modified_unix_ns`; no `source` and no `installed_from`, because the check does not know where the files came from. The provisioning step's record now carries `"written_by": "provisioning"` (a record without the key counts as provisioning's). Both kinds are read by the same code and invalidated the same way: another release or ONNX Runtime version is refused with both versions, another `format` is refused naming it, a file whose modification time differs or that the record does not name is hashed. If the record cannot be written, the check still passes, every start hashes, and `RuntimeFiles::record` is `RecordState::NotWritten { reason }`. `RuntimeFiles` has `record: RecordState` (`Provisioning`, `Check`, `Written`, `NotWritten`, with `describe()`) in place of `provisioned: bool`, and `library`, the manifest's first file, which `onnxruntime_dll()` joins to the directory. A folder in the place of a required file is refused as "not a file". The case this closes: AMD's NuGet package has the five names and sizes with other content; a directory filled from it by hand passed the old fast check and is now refused at its first use (test with a same-size replacement and no record). Still not found by the fast check: a modification time set back to the recorded one, and a record written by hand; `Full` finds both. A record is not refreshed: a file that was only touched is hashed at every start until the record is removed or the directory provisioned again. Measured on scratch copies (713.6 MB, warm file cache, debug build with optimized `sha2`): no record, first start 0.84 s (hash and write); with a record 0.33 to 2.2 ms (the first call of a process is the slowest); full 0.84 to 0.89 s. The main checkout's `runtime\ryzenai\` has no record: its first use by the tool, by the gate test or by the on-request timing test will hash and WRITE `provisioned.json` there. This task did not run anything against it after the change.
- 2.3: Review round 1, the device query. `NpuStatus::matched()` gives `Some(NpuMatch::KnownDevice)` or `Some(NpuMatch::AmdVendorOnly)`, `taken()` the device; the summary line of `log_lines()` reads "NPU driver version: ..." only for a known device id and "driver version of the AMD device taken as the NPU (its id is not a known NPU device id): ..." otherwise. For 2.6: the run record must carry `matched()` next to `driver_version()`, because with `AmdVendorOnly` the version is that of an AMD device that is not known to be an NPU; `driver_version()` alone does not say so. A device whose description or driver version cannot be read (no driver, a failed property call, a node that cannot be located) is listed with `unknown` and the query goes on (`ComputeAccelerator::from_properties`); a value longer than the first buffer is read with a second call of the size the first one asked for. `driver_version()` is `None` for `unknown`, so a present NPU can have no driver version to record. An error of `query()` is now only a failed listing of the class.
- 2.3: Review round 1, file versions. `file_version` asks for the file's own resource (`GetFileVersionInfoExW` with `FILE_VER_GET_NEUTRAL`) and requires the signature `0xFEEF04BD`. A default-run Windows test reads the version of a copy of `C:\Windows\System32\version.dll` and requires it in the refusal for a wrong file, for a file of the release's own version, and for a stray DLL. Seen on the way: for the file in `System32` itself Windows reported 6.2.26100.1150 to the test program and 10.0.26100.1150 for its copy (a program without a declared Windows version is told an older one for system files); the runtime files are not system files, so this does not touch the check.
- 2.3: Review round 1, the runtime directory, for 2.6 and 2.9. Precedence: a directory given by an explicit option of a command, then `L2D_RUNTIME_DIR`, then `runtime/ryzenai/`; in code `paths.runtime_files_dir(option.or(runtime_dir_from_environment()).as_deref())`. A relative value, from either, is relative to the current directory of the process, not to the project root, and is used as given. Before `ort::init_from` the path of `onnxruntime_dll()` must be made absolute and plain: `canonicalize`, then strip the `\\?\` prefix, as the gate test does; only the plain drive form was proven with the provider (2.2), and `RuntimeFiles` returns the directory as it was given. Not handled anywhere yet: a UNC path, where the prefix is `\\?\UNC\`.
- 2.3: Review round 1, provisioning. `provision.py` ends a manifest that lacks a key or has a value of the wrong kind with "provision: refused: ... lacks the key 'x'" and exit code 1, and a failed copy or write with "provision: failed: ..." and exit code 2, no traceback; a half-copied file does not keep its name and no record is written for an unfinished run. Python tests: 20. Added to the steps for finishing part D, after step (b): open `...\ryzenai-from-lock\provisioned.json` and require `"written_by": "provisioning"` and a `source.installed_from` that is the pinned URL, not null (null would mean the script did not find uv's `direct_url.json`, and the default route would then have refused, so a null there is a defect to look into). And for step (e): if the main checkout's directory has meanwhile got a record written by the check, the provisioning step replaces it with its own, which adds the source.
- 2.3: Record refresh (owner decision, 2026-11-12; replaces "a record is not refreshed" above). A file whose modification time differs from the record, or that the record does not name, is hashed; when every file of the directory is right, the check rewrites the record with the new time of those files and adds `"reverified": { "by": "check", "unix_s": <time>, "files": [<names>] }`. Everything else stays as it was read: `written_by`, and for a provisioning record `source` and `provisioned_utc`, so the record still says who verified the directory first and where the files came from. The start after it hashes nothing. `RuntimeFiles::refresh` is `RecordRefresh::NotNeeded`, `Refreshed { files }` or `Failed { files, reason }` (the check passes, the files are hashed at every start, `describe()` says so). A directory that is refused keeps its record byte for byte, also when another touched file of it was right. For 2.9: print `record.describe()` and `refresh.describe()`.
- 2.3: Trial of AMD Ryzen AI 1.8.0 from the owner's local ZIP (2026-11-12). RESULT: the hardware gate FAILS on 1.8.0, the branch stays on 1.9.0, and by the coordinator's rule (stop at the first step that fails) no ZIP route was added to `provision.py`. What was done and found. (1) The ZIP `C:\Users\mariu\Downloads\ryzen_ai_nuget_1.8.0 (1).zip` was read in place, after the method of the sibling project (outer ZIP and nested package opened by fixed names, entries read by fixed names, written under fixed names, a `source.json` with source and hashes): 272,356,050 bytes, SHA-256 `055f1d428948329a8f4d156a69e2f70ae17cd8a6bb1935399bef6c6e423d96b4`; it holds `RyzenAI_Deployment.1.8.0.nupkg` (274,829,591 bytes, SHA-256 `46d66c12080422004ee7418da5ec2ab2e52e5f69160957f7b177cbd0ff4bc20b`, nuspec id `RyzenAI_Deployment`, version `1.8.0`) and two managed packages. Under `runtimes/win-x64/native/` are 15 files; the five names of 1.9.0 report ONNX Runtime 1.27.0 and provider 1.8.0 and sum to 302,169,488 bytes. (2) `ort` =2.0.0-rc.15 has the feature `api-27`; built with it, `ort` loads the 1.27.0 DLL ("at API level 27"). (3) With exactly the five files the process dies with an access violation (`0xC0000005`) while the provider is registered, as 1.9.0 does without `dyn_dispatch_core.dll`. With `aiecompiler_client.dll` (149,486,160 bytes) added, six files, 451,655,648 bytes, the provider registers and compiles; in 1.8.0 the compiler is that separate file (in 1.9.0 the provider DLL is 518 MB and there is none). `vaiml.dll`, `vaip_config.json` and the other eight files are not needed, and adding all DLLs of the package changes nothing in the figures. (4) Gate on 1.8.0, six files, the same fixture, test and options as for 1.9.0, reduced `PATH`, cache `...\gate-2.3-runtime\cache-1.8.0\gate-2.2-1.8.0-6901c42a8d11` (the release is now part of the gate's cache key), four runs, all alike: 36 of 38 nodes on the NPU and 2 on the CPU (as 1.9.0); executed nodes by provider `VitisAIExecutionProvider`: 3 (as 1.9.0); ready with compilation after 3.6 to 3.8 s, from the cache after 0.23 to 0.30 s; 28,000 to 29,300 logs/s (1.9.0 in this session: 24,300 to 24,400; 2.2 measured 24,200 to 36,400); NPU output bit-identical on repeat and from the cache; driver 32.0.20102.4480. But the lowest cosine between NPU and CPU output over the 32 rows is 0.9777 in every run, below the 0.99 the test requires (requirement 4.6); 1.9.0 gives 0.9958. So the test fails on 1.8.0 at that assertion and on nothing else. Not looked into: why the two releases quantize the same 8-bit model differently, whether a provider option or `vaip_config.json` passed as `config_file` changes it, and what a trained model gives (the fixture has random weights; 0.9958 on 1.9.0 is itself a small margin). The trial directories are under `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\gate-2.3-runtime\` (`ryzenai-1.8.0`, `ryzenai-1.8.0-extras`, `ryzenai-1.8.0-plus-aiecompiler`, `ryzenai-1.8.0-all-extras`, `gate-1.8.0-*.txt` with the full output); nothing of the ZIP is in the repository.
- 2.3: The built release is now one explicit choice, added for the trial and kept. `crates/l2d-encoder/Cargo.toml`: `[features] default = ["ryzenai-1-9"]`, `ryzenai-1-9 = ["ort/api-30"]`, `ryzenai-1-8 = ["ort/api-27"]`; the workspace manifest no longer sets an API level for `ort`. The feature selects the compiled-in manifest: `runtime/ryzenai-1.9.0.manifest.json` (the former `runtime/ryzenai.manifest.json`, renamed) or `runtime/ryzenai-1.8.0.manifest.json` (new, six files, source kind `nuget`). Both features, or neither, do not compile (tried). The manifest's `source` has a `kind` (`wheel` or `nuget`), `package`, `version`, `directory`, and the rest by kind (`index` and `wheel`, or `archive`, `nupkg` and `nupkg_sha256`); Rust keeps the rest as a map (`ManifestSource::detail`). Held together by tests: the feature, the compiled-in manifest, `ort::MINOR_VERSION` and the line "The tool is built for **AMD Ryzen AI ...**" of `runtime/README.md`; both manifests and the two tables of the README; a directory with the other release's files, or with a record of the other release, is refused and both releases are named. `provision.py` reads the built release from that `default` line and takes its manifest; a manifest whose source is not a wheel is refused by name. To build for 1.8.0: change `default` to `["ryzenai-1-8"]` and the README line (the test names it); the README test is the only one that then fails until the line is changed, and the gate fails on the cosine. For later crates that depend on `l2d-encoder` (2.9): do not switch its default features off, or name the release feature explicitly; a workspace build with both features enabled anywhere does not compile, which is the intent.
- 2.3: Where this leaves provisioning. Built release: 1.9.0, files from AMD's wheel `onnxruntime-vitisai` 1.30.0. The index route is unchanged and still outstanding (no `uv.lock`, the default route has not run; steps above). There is no provisioning route that works today without the index: the owner's ZIP is release 1.8.0, which fails the gate; AMD's 1.9.0 NuGet package (`https://download.amd.com/opendownload/RyzenAI/1.9.0/RyzenAI_Deployment.1.9.0.nupkg`, 289,269,284 bytes, reachable on 2026-11-12) has the five names with the sizes of the 1.9.0 wheel but other content (CRC-32), and was not downloaded or tried: it is the candidate for a NuGet route on the proven release, and would need its own manifest and a gate run, like 1.8.0 got here. The local copy of the wheel itself is in pip's cache (note above). The main checkout's `runtime\ryzenai\` (1.9.0 files, no record) needs nothing done by hand after the merge: its first use hashes the five files and writes `provisioned.json` (`"written_by": "check"`); a later provisioning run replaces that record with one that names the source.
- 2.13: Implemented on 2026-11-12. The runtime can be provisioned without AMD's index, for release 1.8.0, from AMD's NuGet ZIP. Command (development step; `uv` only supplies a Python 3.12, no environment is made, nothing is downloaded): `uv run --no-project --python 3.12 python tools/runtime/provision.py --release 1.8.0 --nuget-zip "<path>\ryzen_ai_nuget_1.8.0.zip" [--target <directory>]` (default target `runtime/ryzenai/` of the checkout; `--release` picks `runtime/ryzenai-<release>.manifest.json`, `--manifest` names a file instead, without either the manifest of the built release is taken). Which route runs is decided by the manifest's `source.kind`: `nuget` needs `--nuget-zip`, `wheel` must not have it; each route refuses the other kind and says which option is missing or too much. The ZIP route (`open_nuget`, `verify_nuget`, `extract_member` in `provision.py`), after the method of the sibling project: the ZIP is read where it lies; the package is opened by the name in the manifest (`nupkg`), its SHA-256 is compared with the manifest's `nupkg_sha256`, and the id and version in `<package>.nuspec` must be the manifest's `package` and `version`; each file is read as `<directory>/<name>` built from the manifest, whose names must be plain file names. No name found in the archive is used to read or to write, so a member whose path climbs out or is absolute is never looked at (tested with such members in the package and in the ZIP). Every file is hashed against the manifest before anything is written; then the same as the wheel route: a target with a DLL the manifest does not name is refused, each file goes through `.<name>.partial`, is hashed again and renamed, a file that is already right is left alone, and `provisioned.json` is written last. Refusals name both sides (ids, versions, hashes, the packages the ZIP holds) and change nothing; a failed copy ends with one line and exit code 2 and leaves no half file and no record, and the next run finishes the work. Python tests: `tools/runtime/test_provision_nuget.py` (18, on a synthetic ZIP with a nested package built in a temporary directory) and `test_provision.py` (22); 40 in all.
- 2.13: What the record of the ZIP route holds: `format` 1, `release`, `onnxruntime`, `"written_by": "provisioning"`, `provisioned_utc`, `source` = `{ "kind": "nuget", "package", "version", "directory", "archive": { "name", "path", "bytes", "sha256" }, "nupkg": { "name", "bytes", "sha256" } }` and `files` with `name`, `bytes`, `sha256`, `modified_unix_ns`. There is no `installed_from` (that is the wheel route's; its `source` now also carries `"kind": "wheel"`). The record's temporary file is `.provisioned.json.<pid>.partial` on both sides now.
- 2.13: Run for real on the reference host: from `C:\Users\mariu\Downloads\ryzen_ai_nuget_1.8.0 (1).zip` (272,356,050 bytes, SHA-256 `055f1d428948329a8f4d156a69e2f70ae17cd8a6bb1935399bef6c6e423d96b4`, unchanged afterwards) into `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\gate-2.3-runtime\ryzenai-from-zip\`: six files, 451,655,648 bytes, 9 s; a second run copies nothing; the six SHA-256 values equal the 1.8.0 manifest and the manual extraction of the 2.3 trial. The tool built for 1.8.0 (`--no-default-features --features ryzenai-1-8` on `l2d-encoder`): the full check passes (0.43 s, 1 GB/s; fast check with the record 0.3 to 1.0 ms; all six file versions as in the manifest). The hardware gate of that build loads the directory at API level 27, compiles (ready after 4.6 s, from the cache 0.29 s), places 36 of 38 nodes on the NPU and 2 on the CPU, executes 3 nodes by `VitisAIExecutionProvider`, runs 26,655 logs/s, and fails at one assertion only: "the NPU output is too far from the CPU output of the same model (lowest cosine 0.9776721743514212, required 0.99)". That is the expected result (2.3 trial); nothing else differs from 1.9.0. The default build (1.9.0) refuses the same directory and names both releases. The task's "done when" is met in the sense given by the coordinator: the directory passes the full check and the 1.8.0 build of the gate loads it and runs the model on the NPU; the gate test as a whole does not pass on 1.8.0.
- 2.13: The record is bound to the manifest (2.3 review). In the fast check a file is trusted by its modification time only if the record has it with the size and SHA-256 of the manifest the build carries; a record without sizes and hashes, or with those of another source of the same release (the wheel and the NuGet package hold different builds of the same sizes), makes nothing trusted: the files are hashed, refused if they are the other source's, and otherwise the record is brought up to the manifest's values (`reverified`). Test: a directory whose files and record agree with each other in every respect but belong to another source is refused, naming found and expected hash of each file, and its record is left as it was.
- 2.13: Smaller follow-ups. (a) A record that cannot be read (not JSON, empty, not the shape of a record, no `format`, a folder in its place) is treated as no record: every file is hashed, and when all are right a new record replaces it; `RuntimeFiles::record` is then `RecordState::Replaced { reason }` and `describe()` says so. Chosen over a refusal with advice because the record only saves hashing: the hashes decide, a correct directory should not stop the tool, and the repair the advice would give ("remove the file") is what the check now does itself, after it has verified the files. With a wrong file the directory is refused and the unreadable record stays. A record of another `format` is still refused (it can be read, a later tool may have written it, and this build must not replace it); the format is looked at before the shape. (b) Temporary record files (`.provisioned.json.*.partial`) older than a minute are removed after a successful record write; a younger one may be another process's write under way and is left, as are the provisioning step's `.<file>.partial`. (c) The gate test takes the library from `RuntimeFiles::onnxruntime_dll()` and no longer joins a file name. (d) A directory of the other release without a record: the refusal now names both releases; `RuntimeManifest::known()` holds every tracked manifest (both are compiled in, a few kilobytes), and when most files of another known release are there with that release's sizes the reason says so. (e) `runtime/README.md` states once that `--all-features` and `--no-default-features` without a release feature do not compile, and why. (f) The comment in `runtime.rs` about the API level names both features. (g) The on-request timing test no longer assumes five files.
- 2.13: Outstanding. (1) The index route for release 1.9.0, unchanged: no `tools/runtime/uv.lock`, the default route has not run (steps in the 2.3 notes). (2) The release decision waits for task 2.12: the built release stays 1.9.0 (default feature `ryzenai-1-9`), 1.8.0 is the feature switch `ryzenai-1-8`; today the release that can be provisioned without the index (1.8.0) is not the release the tool is built for, so a fresh host still cannot get the built release's files except by hand. If 2.12 decides for 1.8.0: change `default` in `crates/l2d-encoder/Cargo.toml` and the "built for" line of `runtime/README.md` (a test names it), provision the main checkout with the command above (its `runtime\ryzenai\` holds the five 1.9.0 files, which the ZIP route refuses as DLLs of another release only if they are not in the manifest: the five names are, so they are replaced, and the sixth is added), and rerun the gate. If it decides for 1.9.0 and the index stays closed: a `nuget` manifest for `RyzenAI_Deployment.1.9.0.nupkg` (not downloaded, not tried) and a gate run on its files would let the ZIP route serve 1.9.0 unchanged. (3) Not done here: the main checkout's `runtime\ryzenai\` was not touched.
- 2.14: Implemented on 2026-11-12. Release 1.9.0, the built release, can now be provisioned without AMD's index, from AMD's NuGet package. Command (development step; `uv` only supplies a Python 3.12, no environment, nothing downloaded): `uv run --no-project --python 3.12 python tools/runtime/provision.py --nupkg "<path>\RyzenAI_Deployment.1.9.0.nupkg" [--target <directory>]`. Without `--release` the built release is meant; `--nupkg` (a package file) or `--nuget-zip` (a ZIP that holds one) selects the manifest of the kind `nuget`, neither selects the wheel's. Run for real from `C:\Users\mariu\Downloads\RyzenAI_Deployment.1.9.0.nupkg` (289,269,284 bytes, SHA-256 `b96db85a612f8ac5579a6684ef8f254b27c6e181df3ab6d86bc98f2c97ffc3e9`, unchanged afterwards) into `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\gate-2.3-runtime\ryzenai-1.9.0-nuget\`: five files, 713,594,768 bytes, 11 s; a second run copies nothing; the five SHA-256 values equal `runtime/ryzenai-1.9.0.nuget.manifest.json`. The record's `source` for a package file: `{ "kind": "nuget", "package", "version", "directory", "nupkg": { "name", "bytes", "sha256", "file", "path" } }` (`file` and `path` are the file that was given; from a ZIP there is `archive` with name, path, bytes, SHA-256 instead).
- 2.14: The manifests. A manifest is for one release from one source, and the source is in its file name: `runtime/ryzenai-1.9.0.wheel.manifest.json` (was `ryzenai-1.9.0.manifest.json`), `runtime/ryzenai-1.9.0.nuget.manifest.json` (new) and `runtime/ryzenai-1.8.0.nuget.manifest.json` (was `ryzenai-1.8.0.manifest.json`). NuGet manifests carry `nupkg`, `nupkg_bytes` (the package's size, a string like the other source details) and `nupkg_sha256`, and `archive` only where AMD publishes the package inside a ZIP (1.8.0). In Rust `RuntimeManifest::built()` gives the manifests of the built release (1.9.0: wheel, then NuGet; 1.8.0: NuGet), `expected()` the first of them, `known()` all three. `RuntimeFiles::check(dir, depth)` now accepts a directory that matches either manifest of the built release with all its files and reports which in `RuntimeFiles::source` (`wheel` or `nuget`); `check_among(manifests, dir, depth)` is that for given manifests, `check_against` still checks one. How: the manifest whose hashes the record holds is tried first, so a start with a record hashes nothing; otherwise each manifest is tried in order with one shared cache of hashes, so every file is read at most once (a NuGet directory without a record: five hashes, not ten). When none matches, the error is that of the first one tried, and if the hashes show files of two sources the refusal says so: "the directory mixes files of 2 sources of the release: <names> from the source `wheel` (...); <names> from the source `nuget` (...). All files must come from one source". The record of the check carries `"matched_source"`. A record stays bound to the manifest it was verified against: with the other source's manifest its hashes make nothing trusted, every file is hashed; if the files are then right for that other source (the directory was re-filled from it), the record is not patched but replaced by a record of the check (`RecordRefresh::Rewritten`), because its `source` would no longer be true. What the binding cannot see is unchanged from 2.3: files swapped for the other source's with the modification times set back to the recorded ones are trusted by the fast check through the old record; the full check finds it. Tests: either source passes and is reported, with and without a record; a mix is refused and named; the record binding in both directions; `the_built_release_is_one_choice...` now also requires the sources of a release to agree in names, sizes and file versions; the README has one table per manifest, held by a test.
- 2.14: Are the NuGet files the wheel's builds signed separately? Yes, all five. Method of the sibling project (`native_build_sha256`): each file with the checksum field of the PE header zeroed, the security directory entry zeroed and the Authenticode certificate table cut off, then SHA-256. Result per file, package against the wheel's file in the scratch directory: `onnxruntime.dll`, `onnxruntime_providers_shared.dll`, `onnxruntime_providers_vitisai.dll`, `onnxruntime_vitisai_ep.dll`, `dyn_dispatch_core.dll`: identical apart from signature and checksum. In each pair the link time stamp is the same, the certificate table lies at the same offset with the same size (10,320 bytes), the first differing byte is the checksum field, and 569 to 5,758 bytes differ in all, the rest of them inside the certificate table. The tool does not use this; it compares whole files with the manifest it matched. (Done once with a script outside the repository; not kept as a test because it needs both packages.)
- 2.14: The gate on both sources, default build, reduced `PATH`, each directory twice, separate caches: identical. Wheel directory and NuGet directory alike: 36 of 38 nodes on the NPU, 2 on the CPU; executed nodes by `VitisAIExecutionProvider`: 3; lowest cosine against the CPU provider 0.9958; ready with compilation 4.4 to 4.9 s, from the cache 0.44 to 0.53 s; 22,500 to 24,400 logs/s (wheel 23,147 and 23,314, NuGet 22,528 and 24,429: no difference beyond the run-to-run spread). Bit-identity across the sources: the NPU output of the first inference (4,096 values) has SHA-256 `ae62b15d1484d2314cc36cb24b023d625230e7868777939b6ce9147b992a694a` in all four runs, and the CPU output `dffc147e0e91698d75411f2fbebc19dac8cee15be48f1cba72bdadbb6d555a57` in all four; the dumped files are byte for byte equal. For this the gate test got `L2D_GATE_DUMP_OUTPUT=<path prefix>`: it writes `<prefix>.npu.f32` and `<prefix>.cpu.f32` (little-endian 32-bit floats) and always prints the two SHA-256 values; without the variable nothing is written. The task's "done when" is met: the package-provisioned directory passes the full check and the gate with the default build, and the wheel-provisioned directory still does.
- 2.14: Advisory findings of the 2.13 review, closed. (a) File names: `is_plain_file_name` in `runtime.rs` and in `provision.py` refuse a name with any of `< > : " / \ | ? *` or a control character, a trailing dot or space, `.`/`..`, and the device names `CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`-`COM9`, `LPT1`-`LPT9` with or without extensions; the manifest parser and the script use it for file names, the script also for the package and archive names of a manifest. `second.dll:stream` and `CON.dll` are refused before anything is read. (b) The package is not read into memory: its size is compared with the manifest's `nupkg_bytes` first (the file's size, or the size the ZIP states for the member), it is hashed as a stream, and from a ZIP it is copied to a temporary file with the copy stopped at the manifest's size; the nested archive is opened from the file. (c) Refused as target: the directory the package or ZIP lies in, and any directory inside the repository other than `runtime/ryzenai/` (the wheel route too). (d) `read_receipt`: only content that is not a record (not UTF-8, not JSON, not the shape, no `format`) counts as unreadable and is replaced; a record that cannot be opened (in use without sharing, access denied, a folder) gives `RecordState::Unavailable`: every file is hashed for that start and the record is neither replaced nor refreshed. Tested with a file held open without sharing. (e) A `BadZipFile` while placing ends with "provision: failed: the archive could not be read while placing: ..." and exit code 2.
- 2.14: How to provision the main checkout (after review; not done here, its `runtime\ryzenai\` was not touched): in the main checkout, `uv run --no-project --python 3.12 python tools/runtime/provision.py --nupkg "C:\Users\mariu\Downloads\RyzenAI_Deployment.1.9.0.nupkg"`. The default target is `runtime/ryzenai/` of that checkout. Its five files are the wheel's (same names, so no stray DLL): the script replaces each (they differ by hash) and writes `provisioned.json` with the package as the source; expected output five lines "copied" and "5 files, 713594768 bytes". Nothing else may use the directory meanwhile (a DLL that is loaded cannot be replaced; the script then ends with exit code 2, files already replaced are whole, and a rerun finishes). Afterwards `cargo test -p l2d-encoder --test runtime_files -- --ignored --nocapture` must report the source `nuget`, and the gate must pass. Until then the directory is accepted as the source `wheel`.

- 2.1: Gate result as the implementer reported it; read it with the review note at the end of the 2.1 lines, which takes back the lead over the baseline (`docs/2026-11-12-science-gate-encoder.md`, scripts in `tools/science/`, development only). On five folds grouped by URL path, three seeds, input stripped of method and path, 8-bit model: linear head on the vectors 0.959 cross-endpoint ROC AUC (seeds 0.967, 0.960, 0.949; average precision 0.978) against 0.923 for the baseline on the same folds (Python re-implementation; it reproduces the 1.7 figures 0.989 and 0.996); neighbour ratio with k = 11 only 0.893 (seeds 0.927, 0.895, 0.859; 0.55 to 0.87 on `/tienda1/publico/anadir.jsp`). On the 1.7 split the head loses to the baseline (0.962 against 0.989) and the neighbour ratio fails (0.694). So the linear head (2.10) is the use that passes; the neighbour scorer (2.8) at the design's defaults does not, and k = 51 gave 0.921 as a sensitivity figure only. The comparison must be repeated against the output of 1.12.
- 2.1: For 4.4 and any trainer: the design's objective (cross-entropy on a classifier after the raw 128-value output) fails. It puts the signal into the vector's length (lengths 0.9 to 2,565, 99.99 % of the variance in one direction), which L2 normalization removes and 8-bit quantization destroys (float against 8-bit cosine 0.66, neighbour ratio 0.54). What worked (`bce-norm-unit`): the classifier layer reads 16 x the L2-normalized vector, and the loss adds mean((length - 1)^2) on the raw vector; then float and 8-bit agree (cosine 0.9999, head ROC AUC within 0.0006). Supervised contrastive loss failed across endpoints in one run. Settings used: AdamW, learning rate 0.001 one-cycle with 10 % warm-up, weight decay 0.01, batch 64, 8 epochs, best epoch chosen by the neighbour-ratio ROC AUC on a validation part of the fitting side cut by path (chosen epochs 2 to 8); CPU, about 20 s per epoch on 4 threads on a quiet machine (16,000 texts). The same seed gives byte-identical model files.
- 2.1: For 2.4 to 2.6, the model's interface. Graph input `x` float32 `[32, 64, 1, 256]`, output `e` float32 `[32, 128]`, opset 17; the 8-bit QDQ model has 5 Conv (kernel `[1, 5]`, pads `[0, 2, 0, 2]`, strides 1, 2, 2, 2, 1), GlobalAveragePool, Flatten, Gemm, 9 QuantizeLinear, 21 DequantizeLinear and no Relu node (ONNX Runtime's quantizer folds Relu into the uint8 range), identical node for node to the spike model `enc_b32_int8.onnx`; quantized with `quantize_static` of ONNX Runtime 1.31.1 (QDQ, uint8 activations, int8 symmetric weights, per tensor, min/max over 512 fitting-side texts). 976,000 parameters in the graph plus the 256 x 64 byte table. Byte-to-input mapping for `ByteCodec`: the UTF-8 bytes of the text, the first 256, padded with byte 0; `x[n, c, 0, t] = table[byte[n, t]][c]`; the table file is 256 rows of 64 little-endian float32 in byte order (65,536 bytes) and row 0 is all zeros, so padding is zero input (a text holding a NUL byte reads as padding there; the store refuses such texts anyway). The last batch is padded with all-zero inputs. The vector is L2-normalized on the CPU after the graph; raw lengths are 0.8 to 1.4.
- 2.1: Input text. The gate model was trained on the payload part only (`split_routing(text).payload`, as the baseline's strip mode), not on the whole text the design's `ByteCodec::encode_text(text)` takes. With the whole text the encoder is level with the baseline (head 0.928 against 0.923, seed 0) and worse on the long-form endpoints, because 256 bytes cut 91 % of their texts (45 % stripped; over all texts 38 % kept, 19 % stripped). 2.5 must either strip the routing part for texts in the CSIC layout, with the mode recorded in the encoder identity, or the input length must grow, which changes the model the hardware gate ran. Stripped, 1,634 CSIC texts have an empty input and share one vector.
- 2.1: Artifacts in the main checkout, `models/science-2.1/` (not tracked): `split17-f0-s0-strip-bce-norm-unit.int8.onnx` (SHA-256 `45f1810b…4f43a`), `.f32.onnx` (`df1d385c…a150d`), `.table.bin` (`f90c5f68…f58e`), `record.json`. That model was fitted without the three endpoints of the 1.7 split and scores 0.938 (head) and 0.592 (neighbours) on them; it is for wiring 2.2 to 2.7, not a model trained on all data. All 55 encoder runs and 6 baseline fits are in `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\science-2.1\runs\`.
- 2.1: Not verified: no run on the NPU (to check with the `gate-2.2` test: same node placement as the spike, no rejected scale, NPU against CPU cosine at least 0.99); the Rust baseline was not run; kept input and the design's objective have one seed; five endpoints of real size carry the fold means; the length penalty was added after the earlier variants' held-out results were known. Design lines that no longer match: Trainer "Objective: binary cross-entropy on a classifier layer placed after the 128-dimension output"; Trainer `TrainSettings` (no weight decay, schedule or grouped validation; `holdout_fraction` must cut by group); `ByteCodec::encode_text(text)` taking the whole text and no stated padding byte; `OnnxWriter` "Conv, Relu, GlobalAveragePool, Flatten and Gemm" (true of the float model only); NeighbourScorer defaults `k` 11 and `reach` 0.5 (the reach never acted: no log abstained); requirement 3.1's "baseline threshold" has no number until 1.12.
- 2.1: Review (independent, 2026-11-12). Every reported figure reproduces from the 61 run records, no run is missing, a rerun gave byte-identical models, and no leakage was found (training, epoch choice, quantization sample, neighbour store and head see fitting-side rows only; held-out and fitting sides share no path and no text). Two findings the write-up did not have change the conclusion. (1) Twin endpoints: `registro.jsp` and `editar.jsp` have the same 13-parameter form; in folds 1 and 2 one is held out while the other is fitted, and those are the two folds where the encoder beats the baseline. With both held out together (one seed) the head scores 0.639, the neighbour ratio 0.504 and the baseline 0.762. Those two folds show transfer from a same-form endpoint, not an unseen one. (2) The baseline's 0.83 on those folds belongs to its default penalty: at L2 0.1 the baseline averages 0.955 over the five folds (0.980, 0.908, 0.905, 0.992, 0.990), level with the head's 0.959; that value was picked with held-out figures in view, so it is a sensitivity figure, not a setting. Attack values reused across endpoints: 26 %, 67 %, 64 %, 15 %, 26 % of the held-out bad logs per fold carry a parameter value seen only in bad fitting rows. Judgement: the task's own rule (at least 0.90 on grouped folds) is met by the linear head, robustly (every objective variant and every seed mean clears it); it is not met by the neighbour scorer at k = 11; "matches or exceeds the baseline" (requirement 3.1) is not established: a tie at best, a loss on `anadir` (0.945 against 0.988), on the 1.7 split, and when the form family is unseen. Conditions before an encoder package is frozen: repeat against task 1.12 with the baseline's penalty settled; add a fold that holds `registro` and `editar` out together, three seeds; run the hardware check on this model (task 2.12). For 2.10: the head is worth building as "about level with the baseline". For 2.8: k = 11 fails, k = 51 was not chosen in advance, and a reach of 0.5 means nothing for unit-length vectors. Owner decisions carried by this gate: the training objective; payload-only input (it is specific to the CSIC layout: a log without `?` or a line feed becomes an empty input) and its place in the encoder identity; padding with byte 0; PyTorch for the spike where the design names Burn; the baseline's default L2. The task box stays unticked until the owner has taken the gate decision.
- 2.1: Owner, 2026-11-13: the gate is decided on measured metrics, not on the estimates above. That means the baseline as the Rust tool measures it in task 1.12 (with the fold that holds both twin endpoints out), and the trained model run on the NPU in task 2.12: quality of the vectors that come off the NPU and throughput as measured. Until both exist the gate stays open; tasks 2.5 and 2.4 go ahead, because they do not depend on it. The AMD release is 1.9.0.
- 2.1: Owner, 2026-11-13 evening, on the reviewed figures of 1.12 and 2.12: the gate is passed, with the encoder as the vector source. The encoder does not clearly beat the baseline (vectors off the NPU: linear head 0.958 mean over folds; baseline 0.923 at the default penalty and about 0.955 at a lighter one on the same folds; both twin endpoints unseen: baseline 0.76, head 0.64), so the baseline stays the first scorer. The encoder goes ahead because search, the linear head and the Venn-style decision part need vectors, and the NPU delivers them right and at about 16,600 logs/s. 2.6 starts. NPU input stays float; 8-bit input (27 % to 48 % more throughput, bit-identical vectors) is a recorded option that needs the revision's domain string to change.
- 2.5: `l2d_encoder::codec` (re-exported from the crate root): `InputArch { input_bytes, channels, batch }` (`InputArch::V1` = 256, 64, 32; `validate()`, `dims()` = `[batch, channels, 1, input_bytes]`, `row_values()`, `batch_values()`, `batch_bytes()`, `table_file_bytes()`; serde, JSON `{"input_bytes":256,"channels":64,"batch":32}`, unknown fields refused), `ByteTable::{from_le_bytes, read}(…, &arch)`, `InputMode::{Whole, Payload}` (`as_str`, `parse`, `select`; serde as `"whole"` / `"payload"`), `ByteCodec::new(arch, table, mode)`, `TextInput { part_bytes, read_bytes, nul_bytes, truncated, empty }`, `InputBatch`. No new dependency. For 2.4: `InputArch` is the part of the architecture description the codec needs; put it into `encoder.json` as it is, or build it from the fields of `EncoderArch`, and open the table with `ByteTable::read(dir/table.bin, &arch)`. The table is refused (`Refused`, naming the reason) when its size is not 256 x channels x 4 bytes, when row 0 holds anything but positive zeros (negative zero included, because a padded row of a batch is positive zeros), or when a value is not finite; a missing file is `Missing`.
- 2.5: Input mode, for 2.4 and the owner. There is no default: `ByteCodec::new` takes the mode, and `InputMode::parse` knows `whole` and `payload` only (not the baseline's `keep`, `strip`, `isolate`). `whole` reads every byte of the text (`keep` of `l2dsci.byte_ids`); `payload` reads `split_routing(text).payload` (`strip`), so a text without `?` and without line feed is an empty input: every ordinary log line outside the CSIC layout. The mode must be in `encoder.json` and in the bytes the revision is hashed over (as the name from `InputMode::as_str`), next to `InputArch`: the same model file fed in the other mode gives other vectors. The published gate model is a `payload` model. Which mode the tool ships is still the owner's decision (2.1 open points); the codec only makes the choice explicit.
- 2.5: Truncation rule (requirement 1.5): `truncated` is true when the part that is encoded (after the input mode) has more than `input_bytes` UTF-8 bytes. Bytes, not characters; in `payload` mode the routing part does not count. 256 bytes: not truncated; 257: truncated. The cut is at the byte: when it falls inside a multi-byte character the bytes before the cut stay in the input and the rest is dropped, with no backing up to a character boundary, because the model was trained on raw bytes cut at 256 (`raw[:256]` in `l2dsci.byte_ids`). On CSIC (25,608 texts): 9,681 truncated in `whole` (37.8 %), 4,821 in `payload` (18.8 %), the shares of the 2.1 note.
- 2.5: Empty input, for 2.6, 2.7 and the reports: `TextInput::empty` is true when no byte other than padding reaches the model (the part is empty, or every byte read is NUL). The input is then all zeros and all such texts share one vector; count them as the baseline counts `no_features`. On CSIC in `payload` mode: 1,634 texts (6.4 %), the number of the 2.1 note; none in `whole`. A NUL byte is not refused by the codec: it has the padding id, so the model sees padding at its position (a trailing NUL is the same input as the shorter text); `TextInput::nul_bytes` counts those among the bytes read.
- 2.5: Batches, for 2.6 and 2.12. `codec.new_batch()` allocates the one buffer; `codec.fill(&mut batch, &mut texts_iter)` starts it anew, takes up to `batch` texts and returns the row count (0 = no text left); `codec.push(&mut batch, text)` adds one (`None` when full). `batch.values()` is always the whole `[32, 64, 1, 256]` float32 input as one contiguous slice in row-major order, `batch.dims()` its shape, `batch.rows()` the texts in it and `batch.texts()` their flags in order; rows from `rows()` on are all-zero inputs (the padding of the last batch), and the caller drops the model's output for them. The slice can be lent to the runtime as the input tensor without a copy (`TensorRef::from_array_view((dims, values))`, as the gate test does); its address is stable for the life of the batch. "Bound once" holds for the address only: `fill` needs `&mut InputBatch`, which the borrow checker refuses while a tensor that borrows the values is alive. Recommended for 2.6: build the `TensorRef` anew from `batch.values()` for each run and drop it before the next `fill` (no copy, a few words of bookkeeping per batch, no `unsafe`); binding a raw pointer once needs `unsafe` and should be taken only if 2.12 measures a gain. No allocation per text or per batch: `tests/byte_codec_speed.rs` counts allocations and finds none in 32 batches. `fill` zeroes only rows that held a text in the previous batch and hold none now.
- 2.5: Memory, for 2.6 and 2.7 under `MemoryBudget`: a codec holds the table, `ByteCodec::held_bytes()` = 65,536 bytes for v1; a batch buffer holds `InputBatch::held_bytes()` = 2,097,152 bytes of values (`InputArch::batch_bytes()`) plus the flags of 32 texts (32 bytes each, 1,024 bytes). Neither depends on the number or the length of the texts; the codec reads a text in place and keeps nothing of it.
- 2.5: Throughput, measured (release, one thread, reference host, `csic_throughput_of_text_to_model_input_on_one_thread` in `tests/byte_codec_speed.rs`, on request with `$env:L2D_CSIC_CSV` and `cargo test --release -p l2d-encoder --test byte_codec_speed -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads=1`): text to the float batch on CSIC's 25,608 texts runs at 209,000 to 277,000 logs per second in `whole` mode (3.6 to 4.8 microseconds per log) and 268,000 to 331,000 in `payload` mode (median of 15 passes; the range of three runs, other work on the machine); the worst case, every text filling all 256 bytes, 154,000 to 177,000. That is 5 to 11 times the 30,000 logs per second of the NPU, so the codec takes roughly a tenth to a fifth of one core's time next to the NPU and is not the bottleneck; the flags alone run at 5 to 10 million texts per second. The cost is the 64 KiB of float values written per text (13 to 20 GiB/s). The test fails in release when the median falls below 30,000. What would cut the bytes written by up to four (16 KiB against 64 KiB per text), if 2.12 wants more: write the quantized uint8 input directly. The time saved is not shown: the worst case is 16,384 table lookups per text either way, and only padding-heavy texts gain the full factor; to be measured in task 2.12. The first node of the published 8-bit model (`x_QuantizeLinear`) quantizes `x` with scale 0.030868560075759888 (float32 bits `0x3CFCE010`) and zero point 134 (uint8); a table of 256 x 64 uint8 values `clamp(round_half_even(v / scale) + 134, 0, 255)` and a padding value of 134 would give the same tensor that node produces, provided the division is done in float32 and rounded half to even as ONNX Runtime does (derived from the node's parameters, not tested against the runtime). That changes the graph input (the model interface the hardware gate ran) and is not done here. A side finding that belongs to the trainer and task 2.12, not to the codec: the table's values span -4.326 to 4.086, the input range of that node is -4.136 to 3.735 (calibrated on 512 texts), so the 8-bit model clips the largest table values. The review counted them: 4 of 16,384 table values saturate (1 below, 3 above), in the rows of bytes 0x05, 0x06, 0x4B and 0x8D, with a largest error of 0.35 against a half step of 0.015; calibrating over the table rows as well would remove it.
- 2.5: Bit identity with the Python mapping, and how to regenerate. `tools/science/codec_fixture.py` writes `crates/l2d-encoder/tests/fixtures/byte-codec-expected.json` with the science project's own functions (`byte_ids`, `table_input`, the batch padding of `onnx_vectors`) and the published table: 41 texts (ASCII, CSIC rows, 255/256/257/300/5,000 bytes whole and as payload, two-, three- and four-byte characters ending at and cut by the boundary, NUL bytes, no delimiter, empty), for each mode the 256 byte ids, the truncation flag and the SHA-256 of the float input, and the SHA-256 of each batch of 32. `tests/byte_codec.rs` compares ids, flags and hashes for all 82 cases and both batches per mode, and also computes each input from the table file by the formula, without the codec. The table fixture `science-2.1.table.bin` is a copy of `models/science-2.1/split17-f0-s0-strip-bce-norm-unit.table.bin` (65,536 bytes, SHA-256 `f90c5f68…f58e`, asserted). Regenerate from `tools\science`: `uv run python codec_fixture.py D:\Source\log2decision\models\science-2.1 ..\..\crates\l2d-encoder\tests\fixtures`; a rerun gave the identical file.
- 2.5: Not done, not verified: no model was run (the CPU-provider comparison was dropped on the owner's direction of 2026-11-13; that the NPU gives sensible vectors for these inputs is 2.12); the codec is not connected to a package (2.4), a session (2.6) or the store (2.7); the throughput figures are from one host with other work running; texts above 1 MiB were not timed. Design lines that no longer match: `ByteCodec::encode_text(text: &str) -> ([u8; 256], bool)` (the codec is a value built from architecture, table and mode; `byte_ids` gives the ids, `encode_text` and the batch calls give the float input); `Encoded { vector, truncated }` has no place for the empty-input flag; `EncoderArch` in the design holds the input length and table width that `InputArch` now carries for the codec; the package file list has no field for the input mode.
- 2.5: Validation of the architecture description (review pass). `InputArch::validate` bounds each field (`input_bytes` 1 to 65,536, `channels` and `batch` 1 to 4,096) and the batch as a whole: `batch_bytes()` may be at most `MAX_BATCH_BYTES` = 256 MiB (268,435,456 bytes), 128 times the 2 MiB of v1, so task 2.12 can try v1-shaped batches up to 4,096 texts; without it the three maxima together allowed a 4 TiB buffer and `new_batch` would have aborted. Every path that takes an `InputArch` validates it: `ByteTable::from_le_bytes`, `ByteTable::read`, `ByteCodec::new`, and deserialization (serde `try_from`, so a description read from `encoder.json` is valid or an error; 2.4 need not call `validate()` itself). Unknown fields are refused, which rules out `#[serde(flatten)]` of `InputArch` into a larger architecture struct: 2.4 nests it as a field or builds it from the fields.
- 2.5: For the owner, requirement 1.5 in `payload` mode: a text longer than 256 bytes whose payload fits is not marked truncated, although its routing part is not read at all. This matches the trained mapping (`l2dsci.byte_ids` with `strip`); the requirement or the design could say "the part the encoder reads" to make that explicit.
- 2.5: Further design lines that no longer match: design.md names the file `bytes.rs` (the code is `crates/l2d-encoder/src/codec.rs`); "the session pads the last batch to 32" (the padding now lives in `InputBatch`: rows from `rows()` on are zero inputs; the session only drops their outputs); `encoder.json` needs the input mode, and the revision must cover it.
- 2.4: `l2d_encoder::package` and `l2d_encoder::onnx` (re-exported from the crate root): `EncoderArch { input: InputArch, dimension: u16 }` (`EncoderArch::V1` = `InputArch::V1` and 128; `validate()`, `model_input_shape()` = `[batch, channels, 1, input_bytes]`, `model_output_shape()` = `[batch, dimension]`; serde with validation, unknown fields refused), `PackageParts<'a> { name, arch, input_mode, model_int8: &[u8], table: &[u8], training_record: &[u8], model_f32: Option<&[u8]> }`, `EncoderPackage::{revision_of(&arch, mode, table, model_int8) -> String, write(&paths, &parts), open(&paths, dir), open_with(&paths, dir, &MemoryBudget), open_bytes(dir), identity(), dir(), arch(), input_mode(), model(), codec(), into_codec(), files(), model_path(), read_model(), cache_key(), held_bytes()}`, `PackageFile { name, bytes, sha256 }`, `ModelInterface { input, output: TensorSpec { name, element_type, shape }, input_quantization, output_quantization: QuantSpec { tensor, element_type, scale: f32, zero_point: i32 } }` with `ModelInterface::from_onnx(&[u8])`. Constants: `ENCODER_JSON`, `MODEL_INT8_FILE`, `TABLE_FILE`, `TRAINING_RECORD_FILE`, `MODEL_F32_FILE`, `FORMAT` (1), `KIND` (`l2d-encoder`), `REVISION_DOMAIN`, `REVISION_CHARS` (40), `MAX_NAME_BYTES` (64), `MAX_DIMENSION` (4,096). No new dependency; `Cargo.lock` is unchanged.
- 2.4: Layout. `models/encoders/<name>-<rev12>/` holds exactly `encoder.json`, `model.int8.onnx`, `table.bin`, `training-record.json` and, when the maker has one, `model.f32.onnx`. The float model is optional and is not in the revision: the design lists it, the task's layout does not, and since the encoder is NPU only nothing in the tool runs it; it is kept as the source of the 8-bit model and only hashed. Anything else in the directory (a stray file, a sub-directory) is a refusal that names it, so nothing may write into a package directory; the provider's cache stays in `runtime/cache/`. The name rule is the baseline scorer's (1 to 64 letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, not starting with a dot).
- 2.4: `encoder.json`, format 1, as written (indented JSON, keys in this order, one line feed at the end; every object refuses unknown fields; every key is required except `files."model.f32.onnx"`): `{"format": 1, "kind": "l2d-encoder", "identity": {"name", "revision" (40 lower-case hex), "dimension"}, "arch": {"input": {"input_bytes", "channels", "batch"}, "dimension"}, "input_mode": "whole" | "payload", "model": {"input": {"name", "element_type", "shape"}, "output": {"name", "element_type", "shape"}}, "quantization": {"input": {"tensor", "element_type", "scale", "zero_point"}, "output": {...}}, "files": {"model.int8.onnx": {"bytes", "sha256"}, "table.bin": {...}, "training-record.json": {...}, "model.f32.onnx": {...}}}`. `arch.input` is `InputArch` nested as it is. `model` and `quantization` are not typed in by the maker: they are read from `model.int8.onnx` when the package is written and again when it is opened, and `encoder.json` must say what the model says. `quantization.input.tensor` is the output of the `QuantizeLinear` node that reads the graph input, `quantization.output.tensor` the input of the `DequantizeLinear` node that produces the graph output; with scale `s` and zero point `z` the graph computes `q = saturate(round_half_even(x / s) + z)` and `e = (q - z) * s`. A package without `input_mode` is refused (`missing field`), as is an unknown mode, an unknown field anywhere, and an architecture that does not validate.
- 2.4: The revision, exactly. The first 40 characters of the lower-case hexadecimal SHA-256 over, in this order: (1) the 22 bytes `l2d-encoder-package/1` and a line feed; (2) `input_bytes`, `channels`, `batch` and the output dimension, each as a 32-bit unsigned integer, little-endian; (3) the input mode's name (`whole` or `payload`): its length as a 64-bit unsigned integer, little-endian, then its ASCII bytes; (4) `table.bin`: its length the same way, then its bytes; (5) `model.int8.onnx`: its length the same way, then its bytes. Not hashed: the name, the training record, the float model, the times of the files, and what `encoder.json` says about the model (that is a function of the model file, which is hashed). The test `the_revision_is_the_documented_hash...` recomputes it by hand in this order; `every_ingredient_changes_the_revision` flips one bit of the model (first, middle, last byte) and of the table, changes each of the four architecture fields, exchanges two of them, changes the mode, and moves a byte across the boundary of table and model. Consequence: the same model, table, architecture and mode under another training record have the same revision and, under the same name, the same directory; writing them is refused (the existing package is left unchanged) and needs another name.
- 2.4: `EncoderPackage::open(&paths, dir)` verifies in this order and never writes: the place (below); the directory exists (`Missing`, item `encoder package`, with the directory); the memory estimate against the budget; `encoder.json` (at most 1 MiB, JSON, format and kind, then the strict parse); the name and revision are well formed; the directory name equals `<name>-<rev12>`; identity and architecture agree on the dimension; the directory holds exactly the listed files, each a plain file of the recorded size (a missing one is `Missing` with its path); each file's SHA-256 (the table and the model are read once and used from the bytes that were hashed; the others are hashed through a 1 MiB buffer); the revision recomputed from those bytes, the architecture and the mode equals the stored one (both are named); the model's interface, read from the model, fits the architecture (32-bit float, the two shapes); `encoder.json` is byte for byte what the tool writes for its content with the model's own facts in it; the table loads through `ByteTable::from_le_bytes` and the codec through `ByteCodec::new`. So a changed byte in any file is a refusal that names the file, a changed value in `encoder.json` is caught by the check it belongs to, and a byte that changes no value (white space) by the last one. What this cannot see: someone who replaces the training record or the float model and the hash recorded for it; neither is in the revision.
- 2.4: Only under `models/` (requirement 10.3). A package directory must be directly below `ProjectPaths::encoders_dir()`: `ProjectPaths::is_under_models` (lexical, no `..` step, as the baseline scorer and the calibration use it) and the parent must be that directory; neither `models/`, `models/encoders/` nor the package directory may be a link (symbolic link or junction, `symlink_metadata`); and, once it exists, the canonical path of the directory must still be that child of the canonical `models/encoders/`. This is stricter than the baseline and the calibration, which check the path only. A place outside is refused as outside whether or not anything is there. `write` checks the links before it creates `models/encoders/` and again after, so nothing is written through a link. There is no `open` without `ProjectPaths` (the design's `open(dir)`).
- 2.4: Writing. `EncoderPackage::write(&paths, &parts)` validates everything before it touches the disk (name, architecture and a training record that is not JSON are `InvalidInput`; a table that is not the architecture's, a model that is not an 8-bit model of the architecture's shapes, and the place are `Refused`), then writes into `models/encoders/.<name>-<rev12>.writing-<process id>-<count>/` with `create_new`, each file synced, in the order model, table, training record, float model, `encoder.json` last, renames the directory into place and returns the package opened from disk. When the directory exists already: the same package (all recorded sizes and hashes equal) is returned and nothing is written; anything else is `Refused`, "left unchanged". A failure on the way removes the temporary directory; a rename that fails because another writer got there first falls back to the same comparison. A process that dies leaves its temporary directory: it does not open (no `encoder.json` until the last step, and then its name is not `<name>-<rev12>`), it is not in the way of a later write, and nothing removes it (it could belong to a live process): `l2d` or the analyst may delete `models/encoders/.*.writing-*` when no training runs. On Windows a directory cannot be synced with the standard library; the files are synced, the rename is left to the file system (on Unix both directories are synced).
- 2.4: The model reader (`onnx.rs`). Not an ONNX library: it walks the protobuf wire format of the file for `ModelProto.graph`, the nodes' operator and tensor names, the initializers' type, dimensions and first value, and the graph inputs and outputs, borrowing from the bytes (weights are not copied). It requires the 8-bit QDQ form: one graph input that is not an initializer and one output, both with fixed shapes; the input read by exactly one node, a `QuantizeLinear`; the output produced by a `DequantizeLinear`; scale (32-bit float, positive, finite) and zero point (uint8 or int8) as scalar initializers stored in the file, in `raw_data` or in the typed fields, packed or not. Anything else is refused with the reason, so a float model in the place of `model.int8.onnx` is refused ("QuantizeLinear"). `prost` (named in the design) is not used and not needed for reading.
- 2.4: How to make a package from the science gate's files (the on-request test `the_science_gate_model_packages_and_opens` does it in a temporary project): `EncoderPackage::write(&paths, &PackageParts { name: "csic-science-2.1", arch: EncoderArch::V1, input_mode: InputMode::Payload, model_int8: <bytes of split17-f0-s0-strip-bce-norm-unit.int8.onnx>, table: <bytes of ...table.bin>, training_record: <bytes of record.json>, model_f32: Some(<bytes of ...f32.onnx>) })`. Result: identity `csic-science-2.1-099570ec739c (128 dimensions)`, revision `099570ec739ce4d0548c2d15629e857cf516664f`, directory `models/encoders/csic-science-2.1-099570ec739c`; read from the model: input `x` float32 `[32, 64, 1, 256]`, quantized as `x_QuantizeLinear_Output` uint8 with scale 0.03086856 and zero point 134; output `e` float32 `[32, 128]`, dequantized from `e_QuantizeLinear_Output` uint8 with scale 0.002527482 and zero point 133. The mode is `payload` because that model was fitted on the payload part (2.1, 2.5); the same files packaged as `whole` are another revision. Nothing was written under the real `models/`; the package for 2.12 has still to be made there. The tracked fixture `spike-enc-b32-int8.onnx` packages the same way (a test does it with the science table).
- 2.4: Memory. An open package holds the codec with the byte table (`ByteCodec::held_bytes()`, 65,536 bytes for v1) and under 1 KiB of names, shapes, hashes and the path: `EncoderPackage::held_bytes()`, 66,264 bytes for the science package. It does not hold the model and does not grow with it (a test with a 4 MiB model). While it is opened the model is in memory once (hashed and read for its interface, then dropped), the table twice, and the other files go through a 1 MiB buffer: `EncoderPackage::open_bytes(dir)` = model + 2 x table + 3 x `encoder.json` + 1 MiB + 64 KiB from the file sizes, 2,238,954 bytes for the science package. `open` checks that figure against a budget observed with the default settings, `open_with` against the caller's (`require_whole`, so the refusal reads "needs the whole dataset in memory at once"); the final verification inside `write` is not checked, because the caller holds the same bytes already. The estimate is from the code, not from a measured working set.
- 2.4: For 2.6. Open with `EncoderPackage::open_with(&paths, &paths.encoder_dir(name, revision), &budget)`. Take the tensor names and shapes from `package.model()` (no guessing: `x`/`e` are the science model's names, not constants), the batch from `package.arch().input.batch`, the codec from `package.codec()` (or `into_codec()`), the identity for the run record and the store from `package.identity()`. The model: `package.model_path()` is the verified file, but it can change between the open and the session; `package.read_model()` reads it again and hands the bytes out only when size and SHA-256 are those the package was opened with, so a session created from memory runs what was verified (the caller then holds the model bytes, 989,261 for the science model, and should count them). Cache key for the provider: `package.cache_key()` is the revision, 40 characters, and is only the package's part of the key (see the review round 1 note "For 2.6 and 2.7, the cache key"). The package says nothing about a CPU run.
- 2.4: For 2.7: `EncoderIdentity` comes from `package.identity()`; its `revision` covers the input mode, so vectors made in `whole` and in `payload` mode from the same model never share a store. For 2.12: the input's and output's scale and zero point are in `package.model()` and in `encoder.json`; a model changed for the measurements (another batch size, quantized input fed directly) is another model file and so another revision and package, and a model whose input is not float32 or not read by one `QuantizeLinear` is refused by the package as it stands (`EncoderArch::misfit` and `onnx.rs` are the places to widen). For 4.4: `write` takes the finished files as bytes, not `Weights` and `CalibrationRanges`; the writer and the quantizer produce the bytes and call it. Interrupted training (3.7): until `write` returns nothing of the new package is visible, and an existing package is never written into.
- 2.4: Tests. `crates/l2d-encoder/tests/encoder_package.rs` (32 tests and one on request) with `tests/common/mod.rs`, which writes small model files by hand in the protobuf wire format (input `[2, 3, 1, 8]`, output `[2, 4]`); two unit tests in `package.rs` for interrupted writes, which need the private observer of a write: a failure at each of the six steps (after the temporary directory is made, after each file, before the rename) leaves no entry in `models/encoders/` and an existing package unchanged in names, sizes and times; a panic at each step, which unwinds past the clean-up as a kill would stop it, leaves exactly the temporary directory and the next write succeeds beside it. Links are made as symbolic links, or as junctions (`mklink /J`) where Windows asks for a privilege. No model is run anywhere.
- 2.4: Deviations and design lines that no longer match. (a) Design `EncoderPackage::write(paths, name, arch, weights, ranges, record)`: it is `write(paths, &PackageParts)` with finished bytes; `Weights`, `LayerWeights` and `CalibrationRanges` are not defined here (4.4). (b) Design `open(dir: &Path)`: it is `open(paths, dir)`; there is no open that skips the place check. (c) Design "`encoder.json` (identity, architecture, the input mode, quantization ranges)" and Physical Data Model "identity, architecture description, calibration ranges, quantization scheme, file hashes": no calibration ranges are stored (they are the trainer's input to the quantizer); stored are the input's and output's scale and zero point, read from the model, the model's tensor names, types and shapes, and file sizes with hashes. (d) Design layout lists `model.f32.onnx` without qualification: it is optional. (e) Design "Revision: SHA-256 over `model.int8.onnx`, `table.bin` and the architecture description": the order and encoding are as above, and the input mode is in it. (f) Design `EncoderArch` "layer definitions, input length, dimension" (file `arch.rs`): `EncoderArch` is input shape and dimension, in `package.rs`; layer definitions belong to the trainer. (g) Design names `prost` (P1) for this component: not used. (h) Design, EncoderSession: "cache key equal to the encoder revision" holds (`cache_key()`); `EncoderSession::open(pkg, paths, allow_cpu)` and the title "or on the CPU when allowed" are older than the NPU-only decision. (i) Task text "Requirements: 3.2, 3.6": by their text the requirements meant are 3.3 (identity) and 3.7 (interrupted training); the design's traceability table has the same older numbers.
- 2.4: Not verified. No model was run, so nothing shows that the provider accepts a session from `read_model()` bytes or the revision as a cache key; a kill of a real process during a write was simulated in-process only (the panic), not with a second process; a power loss between the rename and the journal's commit on NTFS is not covered by a directory sync; the link refusals were tested with whichever of symbolic link or junction the host allowed, not both; `open_bytes` is an estimate from the code, not a measured peak; the model reader was tried on the science model, the spike fixture and hand-written files, not on a model from another exporter; a scale or zero point stored outside the model file is refused by the code, and no test covers it.
- 2.4, review round 1 (2026-11-13, a third commit; where it differs from the 2.4 notes above, this holds). `EncoderPackage::write(&paths, &parts) -> Result<WrittenPackage, L2dError>`, `WrittenPackage { package: EncoderPackage, outcome: WriteOutcome }`, `WriteOutcome::{Written, Unchanged, ExistsWithOtherRecord { differing: Vec<&'static str> }}`. `Written`: nothing was in the place. `Unchanged`: the very same package was there, nothing was written. `ExistsWithOtherRecord`: the same encoder (same name and revision) was there with another `training-record.json` or `model.f32.onnx` (or with or without a float model); nothing is written or overwritten, the package returned is the one that was there, `differing` names the files, and the caller (4.4, the train command) says that the new record was not stored. A training run that ends in an encoder that exists already therefore succeeds. Refused, and left unchanged, is only what is no such package: a damaged one, something else in the place. Eight writers at once (test): one `Written`, seven `Unchanged` for the same record, seven `ExistsWithOtherRecord` for eight records, the stored record the winner's, no temporary directory left.
- 2.4, review round 1, the scope of the revision. The `/1` of `REVISION_DOMAIN` stands for everything in code that shapes a vector and is not among the hashed bytes: the padding byte 0, the truncation rule, a NUL byte read as padding, the tensor layout `x[n, c, 0, t] = table[byte[n, t]][c]`, and the L2 normalisation after the graph (2.6). A change to any of them must raise the number, which changes every revision. Feeding quantized uint8 input directly (2.12) would add the tool's rounding rule to that list. Stated in the module documentation of `package.rs`.
- 2.4, review round 1, smaller changes. (A2) A temporary name that is taken (a leftover of a process that had the same process id) is passed over for the next count, 64 tries at most, then a failure that names the leftovers to remove; the baseline scorer and the calibration use the same naming scheme without the retry and were not changed. (A3) `encoder_json_is_this_file_byte_for_byte` holds the file of the small synthetic package as a literal, so a change in `serde_json`'s indentation or float printing fails a test before it makes packages unopenable. (A4) A directory in the place of `encoder.json` is `Refused` ("is not a plain file"), checked before the read. (A6) A model file with a second `graph` field is refused. (A7) A float model, when given to `write`, must have the 8-bit model's input and output (names, float32, shapes), or the write is refused naming `model.f32.onnx`; on open the float model is hashed only. (A8) Unit tests for the resolved-path comparison alone (a link to a directory outside, a link to a sibling, `models/` reached through a link, the other spelling) and a test of the 1 MiB ceiling of `encoder.json`. (A10) The mismatch between `encoder.json` and the model is worded ("states the quantized input as `x_q` uint8 with scale 0.015625 and zero point 127, but in model.int8.onnx it is ..."). (A11) On a file system that merges upper and lower case, `TINY` beside `tiny` with the same revision is refused saying that the directory belongs to the encoder of the other spelling and that the new name must differ by more than case.
- 2.4, review round 1, hostile model files (A5). The reader no longer keeps nodes or initializers: it walks the graph three times (inputs and outputs; nodes, one at a time; initializers, one at a time) and keeps at most 256 graph inputs and 256 outputs of at most 8 dimensions (more is refused), names up to 256 bytes (longer is refused), and lists at most three names in a refusal, each clipped to 64 characters. Measured with a counting allocator (`tests/onnx_reader_memory.rs`, a test binary of its own): 641 bytes of heap for the small model, the same 641 bytes for a 5.6 MB file of 400,005 nodes, a refusal of 231 bytes when all of them read the input, and 63,551 bytes at the ceiling of 255 listed inputs of 8 dimensions. `OPEN_FIXED_BYTES` is now 256 KiB and its documentation says what it covers; `open_bytes` for the science package is 2,435,562 bytes (was 2,238,954), `held_bytes` about 66 KiB as before.
- 2.4, review round 1, for 2.6 and 2.7, the cache key. The provider's cache key must not be the bare revision: the provider compiles per runtime release, and the review of 2.3 found that the vectors differ between releases. `package.cache_key()` is the package's part. The session builds the key from it, the runtime release (or the provider DLL's hash from the manifest) and a digest of the provider options that change compilation, for example `runtime/cache/<rev12>-<release>-<options digest>`. The runtime release that produced the vectors belongs in the run record and, for 2.7, beside the encoder identity in the store, so that vectors from two releases are never mixed. This is flagged for the owner; the store was not changed.
- 2.4, review round 1, for 2.12. Another batch size needs no change in `onnx.rs` or in the format: format 1 stays, `arch.input.batch` and the model's shapes change, and so does the revision; a symbolic batch dimension is refused (an encoder model has a fixed shape). Quantized uint8 input fed directly needs three changes: `onnx.rs` (`ModelInterface::read`, where the input must be read by one `QuantizeLinear`) to accept an input that is uint8 and is read by a `DequantizeLinear` or by no quantize node; `EncoderArch::misfit` in `package.rs` to allow a uint8 input; and the codec to produce quantized bytes with a stated rounding rule, which then belongs to the revision's scope (above). The format 1 schema can already express it (`model.input.element_type`).
- 2.4, review round 1, the on-request test. `the_science_gate_model_packages_and_opens` takes the directory from the environment variable `L2D_SCIENCE_DIR` only and prints `SKIPPED` with what to set when it is not set. On the reference host: `$env:L2D_SCIENCE_DIR = "D:\Source\log2decision\models\science-2.1"; cargo test -p l2d-encoder --test encoder_package -- --ignored --nocapture`. Tests now: 41 in `encoder_package.rs` and one on request, 4 unit tests in `package.rs`, 1 in `onnx_reader_memory.rs`.
- 2.4, review round 1, further design lines that no longer match (design.md, EncoderSession): line 665 (Intent "or on the CPU when allowed"), 669 ("naming every finding and both releases": both only with a record), 671 (the release feature choice, `ryzenai-1-8`, `--all-features` does not compile), 674 (release 1.8.0 provisioned through the ZIP route, six files), 676 ("All manifests are compiled into the tool; ... the others only word a refusal"), 677 ("cache key equal to the encoder revision": the revision is the package's part of the key), 682 (the CPU path), 702 (`EncoderSession::open(pkg, paths, allow_cpu)`). The workspace `Cargo.toml` comment at the `ort` entry was corrected in this commit.
- 2.4, review round 2 (2026-11-13, a fourth commit). (1) The reader's pass over the initializers looks each name up in a set of the listed graph inputs, so it is linear: a 5 MB file of 1,000,255 initializers with 256 listed inputs takes 0.9 s in a debug build (test bound 3 s; with one comparison per initializer and input it was 4.5 s on the reviewer's file). Heap at the ceiling of 255 listed inputs is now 85,104 bytes with the two sets; 724 bytes for the small model and for the 400,005-node file. (2) A scale or zero point of the input or output that is defined twice is refused, naming it (the last one used to win); other initializers may repeat. The empty name has one meaning in every pass: a graph input or output without a name is refused, and an initializer without a name is passed over. (3) `existing` compares the whole 40-character revision and the name of the package in the place with the one to be written before anything else; a difference (two encoders that share the first twelve characters) is a refusal that names both revisions. (4) A float model must be one: a file whose input is read by a `QuantizeLinear` or whose output is produced by a `DequantizeLinear` is refused as `model.f32.onnx` ("this is a quantized model"), so the 8-bit model given twice is refused. The science gate's float model is accepted; the package's identity and revision are unchanged. Tests now: 42 in `encoder_package.rs` and one on request, 5 unit tests in `package.rs`, 1 in `onnx_reader_memory.rs`.
- 2.4, review round 1, not done or not verified: the float model is not parsed on open (a package made by hand with a hash-consistent bogus float model opens); the eight-writer test ran on NTFS only; the case-merging refusal was exercised because this host's file system merges case, the other branch (a file system that tells them apart) did not run here.
- 2.4, clean-up (2026-11-13, a commit of its own after the task's): the build switch for AMD Ryzen AI 1.8.0 is removed, as the owner decided ("always forward"; the tool stays on 1.9.0). Removed: the feature `ryzenai-1-8` (`ort/api-27`) of `l2d-encoder` and the compile error for two release features; the tracked manifest `runtime/ryzenai-1.8.0.nuget.manifest.json` and its compiled-in copy; `RuntimeManifest::known()` and the note that named another release from its manifest (`other_release_note`); in `runtime/README.md` the section and table of 1.8.0, the sentences about `--all-features` and the two features, and the ZIP section's 1.8.0 command; in the tests the 1.8.0 rows and branches (`runtime_files.rs`, `test_provision.py`, `test_provision_nuget.py`). Where this replaces older notes: the 2.3 note "The built release is now one explicit choice", the 2.13 notes about the 1.8.0 build and the 2.14 note's `known()` describe what is gone; the measurements of the 1.8.0 trial in those notes stand as history.
- 2.4, clean-up, what is left. One release feature, `ryzenai-1-9` = `["ort/api-30"]`, on by default; `l2d-encoder` without it does not compile and the error says so; `--all-features` compiles (`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features` is clean). `RuntimeManifest::built()` is the wheel manifest and the NuGet manifest of 1.9.0, `expected()` the first; a test requires that exactly these two manifests are tracked. `provision.py` still reads the release from the `default` line of `crates/l2d-encoder/Cargo.toml`.
- 2.4, clean-up, a runtime directory of another release is still refused, by the same checks; what the message names changed. With a record of another release (`provisioned.json` written by provisioning or by a check built for it): both releases, as before ("was verified as AMD Ryzen AI 1.8.0 (ONNX Runtime 1.27.0) according to its record, this build of l2d expects AMD Ryzen AI 1.9.0 (ONNX Runtime 1.30.0)"). Without a record it can no longer say "N of the M files of AMD Ryzen AI 1.8.0 are there": it names the built release and each file that is not that release's with the size found and expected (or the hash, where the size is the same), the file version a real DLL reports ("it reports file version 1.8.0, the release has 1.9.0: a file of another release"), and each DLL the release does not have ("`aiecompiler_client.dll` is not part of the release"). The test `a_directory_of_another_release_is_refused` holds this with the six names and sizes of 1.8.0 written into the test.
