Covered: the whole route on small ZIPs made by the tests (placing, the record naming the ZIP, refusals of another id, version, hash or size, paths that point elsewhere, an interrupted copy), and the command line against the tracked 1.9.0 manifest up to the refusal of a ZIP that does not hold AMD's package; `--release 1.8.0` now ends with the missing manifest's name. Not covered: a run on a ZIP that holds AMD's real 1.9.0 package (AMD publishes it bare; the bare route ran for real in 2.14). 51 Python tests pass.
- 2.4, clean-up, how release 1.8.0 (or a later release) would be brought back. The commits that added it: `ae55790` (the release as one feature choice, the 1.8.0 manifest, the README section) and `e3413ef` (the NuGet ZIP route and the binding of the record to the manifest; that route stays). Steps: restore `runtime/ryzenai-1.8.0.nuget.manifest.json` from the parent of the clean-up commit (`git show <clean-up>^:runtime/ryzenai-1.8.0.nuget.manifest.json`); add the feature `ryzenai-1-8 = ["ort/api-27"]`; in `runtime.rs` compile the manifest in, choose `BUILT_MANIFESTS` by feature and refuse both features together; put the README table back (a test requires a table per tracked manifest) and widen the two tests that pin the tracked manifests to 1.9.0. Reverting the clean-up commit does all of it. A release newer than 1.9.0 needs the same plus an `ort` whose API level matches its ONNX Runtime.
- 2.4, clean-up, not touched because outside the encoder crate, `runtime/` and `tools/runtime/`, and now out of date: the comment in the workspace `Cargo.toml` at the `ort` entry ("feature `ryzenai-1-9` sets `ort/api-30`, `ryzenai-1-8` sets `ort/api-27`"); `.kiro/handover.md`, "Things that will bite", "Release switch" (there is no 1.8 switch and `--all-features` compiles); design.md, EncoderSession: "a cargo feature of `l2d-encoder` chooses the release (... `ryzenai-1-8` with `ort/api-27` for trials); exactly one must be enabled, so `--all-features` does not compile", "release 1.8.0 is provisioned this way and needs six files", and "All manifests are compiled into the tool; the enabled feature selects those of one release, the others only word a refusal".
- 2.12: Measured on 2026-11-13 on the reference host with the trained model of 2.1 (`docs/2026-11-13-npu-throughput-and-row-correctness.md` has the tables). RESULT, rows: in the exported batch layout `[32, 64, 1, 256]` only the first text of a batch is computed right on the NPU under Ryzen AI 1.9.0. Row 0 is bit-identical to the text encoded alone (batch-of-one model); all other rows are wrong (identical rows in: up to 87 to 132 output steps apart, cosine down to -0.30; different texts: squared L2 between a row's unit vector and the text's own 0.04 to 1.75, median 0.50, against a median of 0.0005 to the nearest other text). On other rows: in the exploration a row's vector changed when all other rows changed (row 0 never did), while in the committed test row 1 keeps its vector when only rows 2 to 31 are exchanged; which rows a row depends on was not worked out. The same at batches of 2, 4 and 8. The pooling step is not the cause: rows 1 to 31 are wrong after the first convolution (with an all-zero input they lack the bias response; one input value set in row 1 moves no output value, in row 0 it moves 302). `opt_level` 1 to 3, `target` `X2-Benchmark` and `PSO`, `enable_preemption` 0 and uint8 input changed nothing. What the provider computes for those rows was not established.
- 2.12: The layout that is right, for 2.6 and 2.7. `l2d_encoder::onnx_edit::with_rows_as_height(model, rows)` rewrites the trained file without retraining: input `[1, 64, rows, 256]` (text `n` is row `n` of one image), `GlobalAveragePool` to `AveragePool` with kernel `[1, 32]` and strides written out, `Flatten` and its quantize pair removed, `Gemm` to a 1 x 1 `Conv` (the stored weight keeps its bytes, dimensions `[128, 256, 1, 1]`), output `[1, 128, rows, 1]`. On the NPU 33 of 35 nodes (the two on the CPU are the input's `QuantizeLinear` and the output's `DequantizeLinear`), and every row is bit-identical to the text alone for 1, 2, 8, 32, 64, 128 and 256 rows. Requirement 4.6 holds exactly in this layout: tolerance 0 output steps. The gate is `crates/l2d-encoder/tests/npu_rows.rs` (`every_row_is_right_when_the_texts_are_rows_of_an_image`: identical rows in give identical rows out, a text's vector depends neither on its row nor on the other texts, a last image that is not full; `in_the_batch_layout_only_the_first_row_is_right` records the defect and fails when a runtime release repairs it).
- 2.12: What 2.6 must do. (1) Open the package, take `read_model()`, rewrite the bytes in memory with `with_rows_as_height(.., 64)` and create the session with `commit_from_memory` (a session from memory works with the provider; this was not verified in 2.4). The package stays as it is: `model.int8.onnx` in the batch layout, `arch.input.batch` 32, revision unchanged; the rows per image are the session's choice, not the package's. (2) Write the input with `l2d_encoder::encode::ImageInput::float(codec)`: `write(codec, text, rows, row, image)` lays a text as row `row` (`image[c * rows * 256 + row * 256 + t]`), `clear_row` empties rows a shorter last image no longer uses; the flags are `ByteCodec`'s. `InputBatch` and `ByteCodec::fill` write the batch layout and are not what the session feeds any more. (3) Lend the image to the runtime with `TensorRef::from_array_view((vec![1, 64, rows, 256], &image[..]))` per run and drop it before the next write: no copy, 0.5 % of the time; IO binding (a runtime-owned input tensor written in place and bound again per run, the output bound once) works and gains nothing. (4) Read with `encode::read_rows(output, rows, used, vectors)`: `vector[d] = output[d * rows + n]`, L2-normalised. (5) Settings: one session, 64 rows per image, default threads (the intra-op setting changes nothing). The proto-session is `HeightEncoder` in `tests/npu_support/mod.rs`.
- 2.12: `error_on_failure` does not make a session NPU only. When the provider's compiler failed on a rewritten file (an `AveragePool` without strides), the session opened with 0 nodes on the NPU and ran every node on the CPU, with no error. 2.6 must read the placement report and refuse to encode unless the nodes on the CPU are exactly the two quantize nodes at the ends (33 and 2 of 35 for this architecture); requirement 4.4 depends on that check, not on the provider option.
- 2.12: The provider's cache key, by experiment: the provider looks at nothing but the key. The key of one compiled model reused (a) for the same model at 32 rows instead of 64: opened without compiling, the first run failed; (b) for another model of the same shape: opened without compiling and returned vectors that are neither model's, without an error; (c) with `opt_level=3`: not compiled again, option not applied. So the key must cover the revision, the runtime release, the provider options, and the model file actually handed to the provider (the rewritten bytes: layout, rows, the version of the rewrite). `encode::provider_cache_key(revision, release, model, options)` gives `<rev12>-<release>-<16 hex>`; `model` is the rewritten bytes handed to the provider (hashed inside, so it cannot be left out) and `options` every provider option with its value. A cache folder without `vitisai_ep_report.json` counts as not compiled (2.2). For 2.7: the runtime release belongs beside the encoder identity in the store, as the 2.4 notes say.
- 2.12: Throughput, end to end from log text to unit vector, CSIC's 25,608 distinct texts, release build, five passes, two runs (the second on a quieter machine; first in brackets). One session, float input: 1 row 1,311 logs/s; 8 rows 7,393; 32 rows 13,281; 64 rows 16,636 (13,928); 128 rows 17,003; 256 rows 20,091. 64 rows sustained over 102,432 texts: 17,161 (15,971). Requirement 4.2's 1,000 is met 14 to 17 times over. Where the time goes at 64 rows (59.6 us per log): writing the image 23 %, handing it over 0.5 %, the run 75.5 %, reading and normalising 1 %. 33 CPU seconds per million logs, 300 MiB working set. Two, three and four sessions on as many threads: 26,486, 35,678 and 37,613 logs/s (same vectors), the fastest setting measured, at 64 CPU seconds per million logs and 421 MiB. Start-up at 64 rows: cold 4.9 s, warm in a fresh process 1.0 s, warm in the same process 0.45 to 0.6 s (requirement 4.7: 30 s).
- 2.12: Feeding the quantized bytes directly (experiment, not adopted). `onnx_edit::with_quantized_ends` removes the input's `QuantizeLinear` and/or the output's `DequantizeLinear`; the provider accepts both (33 NPU nodes, 1 or 0 on the CPU). `encode::ImageInput::quantized(codec, scale, zero_point)` writes uint8 with the model's scale and zero point (float32 division, round half to even, saturate). The vectors are bit-identical to the float-input path over all 25,608 texts. Gain at 64 rows: 21,147 logs/s with uint8 input (+27 %), 21,572 with input and output (+30 %), CPU time 18 instead of 33 s per million logs. Adopting it needs no change of the package (the session can rewrite the verified file in memory and read scale and zero point from `package.model()`), but the tool's rounding rule then shapes every vector, so `REVISION_DOMAIN` would have to go to `/2` (2.4 notes) and the bit identity would have to stay a gate assertion. Proposal for the owner; 2.6 should start with the float input.
- 2.12: What the measurements point to next: the image writer costs 13.8 us per log against 3 to 4 us for `ByteCodec`'s batch layout (64 lines of 1 KiB, 64 KiB apart, per text); a writer that goes channel by channel over all texts of an image, or a second thread for it, was not tried. uint8 input with several sessions was not tried.
- 2.12: Quality of the vectors from the NPU, 15 fold models of 2.1, scored in Rust with the science gate's folds (checked against each record's path count, path hash and text count) and definitions; NPU against the record's 8-bit figures: linear head cross-endpoint ROC AUC 0.9583 against 0.9588 (largest difference in one run 0.0044; same penalty chosen in all 15), average precision 0.9778 against 0.9779; neighbour ratio k = 11 0.8990 against 0.8932 (three runs differ by 0.012 to 0.044, the rest within 0.006; ties among equally near 8-bit vectors are broken differently, and that was not told apart from single-step differences); k = 51 0.9211 against 0.9213. For the gate decision: the head passes 0.90 on every seed mean; the neighbour ratio at k = 11 misses it (0.899), as recorded. The fold that holds both twin endpoints out was not run: no such model exists.
- 2.12: For 4.4 and the trainer. (a) The writer should emit the image layout directly, or 4.4 keeps the batch layout and the session rewrites it; either way the hardware gate must run on the file that is fed. (b) The batch size of the exported file no longer matters to the NPU (the rewrite gives the same file from any batch). (c) The four table values the input range clips (2.5 notes) were left in: part 3's figures include them, and they match the recorded ones; calibrating over the table rows is still the clean fix and was not shown to matter. (d) For 2.8: with 8-bit vectors many stored vectors are equally near; the neighbour scorer needs a stated tie rule, because at k = 11 the rule moved the ROC AUC of single folds by up to 0.04.
- 2.12: Tests and how to run. Default: `tests/onnx_edit.rs` (6), unit tests in `src/encode.rs` (7), four definition tests in `tests/npu_quality.rs`. On request, release, one at a time, with `L2D_RUNTIME_DIR`, `L2D_NPU_WORK_DIR` (caches and outputs), `L2D_SCIENCE_DIR`, `L2D_SCIENCE_RUNS`, `L2D_CSIC_CSV`: `cargo test --release -p l2d-encoder --test npu_rows -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads=1` (30 s), `--test npu_throughput` (7 minutes; `L2D_NPU_SETTINGS` selects settings), `--test npu_quality` (3 minutes). They fail without the runtime directory, the NPU or the models and skip only with `L2D_GATE_ALLOW_SKIP=1`. `l2d-baseline` is a dev-dependency of `l2d-encoder` for the quality test (one line in `Cargo.lock`, no new package); `windows-sys` gets two more features on the dev-dependency (process times and memory).
- 2.12: Design lines that no longer match (design.md): 245 ("NPU registration uses `error_on_failure`; the CPU path is taken only with `--allow-cpu`": there is no CPU path, and `error_on_failure` does not keep a session off the CPU), 293 ("batch of 32, fixed shapes"), 636 ("The ONNX graph takes `[32, 64, 1, 256]` float input and returns `[32, 128]`": true of the file, not of what the NPU is fed), 637 (`OnnxWriter` operators), 677 ("cache key equal to the encoder revision"; "registered with `error_on_failure`" as the NPU-only guarantee), 680 ("The batch buffer of `ByteCodec` pads the last batch": the session writes an image and clears unused rows), 702 (`open(pkg, paths, allow_cpu)`), 726 ("keyed by ... the package's `cache_key()`, the runtime release and a digest of the provider options": also the model bytes handed to the provider, which carry layout and rows), 1233 ("fixed batch of 32 ... about 36,700 logs/s"). Task 2.6's text "Process batches with padding" means images of 64 rows. Not verified: another host, driver or runtime release; why the provider gets the batch layout wrong; provider options for speed; more than 256 rows.
- 2.12, review round 1 (2026-11-13). `with_rows_as_height` now refuses every file for which the rewrite is not known to be the same arithmetic, each with a reason that names the node: a `Conv` whose stored weight is not `[out, in, 1, width]`, or with a `kernel_shape`, `pads`, `strides` or `dilations` that reach along the height, or with an `auto_pad` other than `NOTSET`; a quantize node on the path of the texts with one scale per entry of an axis other than the channels; a `Flatten` that does not read the pool's output (directly or through one quantize pair); a quantize pair behind `Flatten` whose scale or zero point is not that of the pair before it (compared by value, not by name), or with no pair before it; a `Gemm` bias that is not a stored `[out]` tensor. The pool-shape walk is bounded by the number of nodes and nothing is indexed unchecked. `onnx_edit::node_ints_attribute(model, node, attribute)` reads a node's integer list, and the tests hold the emitted `AveragePool` to `kernel_shape [1, pooled length]` and `strides [1, 1]` on the fixture (32) and on hand-written encoders of other lengths (8, 20). The trained models pass the new checks; the gate and the quality run were repeated on hardware after the change. `tests/npu_quality.rs` now fails when the mean linear-head ROC AUC from the NPU differs from the recorded one by more than 0.01. Default tests of the task: `tests/onnx_edit.rs` 9, `src/encode.rs` 7, `tests/npu_quality.rs` 4. In the document the figures that rest on the uncommitted exploration carry the marks [E1] to [E6] with the log files named.
- 1.16: API as built, `l2d_core::zip_input` (re-exported from the crate root: `ZipInput`, `ZipInputSettings`, `ArchiveSummary`, `EntryReport`, `EntrySkip`, `ArchivePosition`). `ZipInput::open(&Path, &ZipInputSettings, &MemoryBudget) -> Result<ZipInput, L2dError>` (checks the settings, hashes the file with SHA-256 in one pass, reads the directory); `batches(&mut self, &mut MemoryBudget) -> ZipBatches<'_>`, an `Iterator<Item = Result<LogsFrame, L2dError>>` that ends after its first error; `occurrences(&self, text) -> u64`; `held_bytes(&self) -> u64`; `batch_plan(&self) -> Option<&BatchPlan>` (set once the batches are at their end); `finish(self) -> ArchiveSummary`. `ZipInputSettings { entries: Vec<String>, max_record_bytes: u64 }` (default 64 KiB, allowed 1 to 1 MiB). `ArchiveSummary { input: InputSummary, entries: Vec<EntryReport> }` with `locate(position) -> Option<(&str, u64)>`, `read()`, `damaged()`. `EntryReport { path, records, skipped: Option<EntrySkip> }`. `EntrySkip::{NotText, Encrypted, UnsupportedMethod(u16), NestedArchive, NoPatternMatch, Damaged(String), Directory, Link, NotReached}` with `Display` and serde in snake case. `ArchivePosition { entry: u32, line: u64 }::{pack, unpack}`. Constants `DEFAULT_MAX_RECORD_BYTES`, `MAX_RECORD_BYTES`, `MAX_ENTRIES` (2^24), `MAX_ENTRY_LINES` (2^40 - 1). Added to existing code: `Batcher::budget()`; `Accepted::rows`, `combine`, `own_strings`, `frame_error`, `hex`, `SKIPPED_RECORD_BYTES`, `memory::exact` and the lines adapter's `NO_SEPARATOR` became `pub(crate)`.
- 1.16: Deviation from the task text: the tool does not read archives with the `zip` crate. `zip` 2.6.1 is a dev-dependency of `l2d-core` and `l2d-cli` only (it writes the test archives and reads them as a second opinion); the directory, the zip64 records and the entry headers are read by own code in `l2d-core/src/zip_directory.rs`, and entries are inflated with `flate2` (pure-Rust backend). Reason, read in the crate's source (`zip-2.6.1/src/read.rs`, lines 647 to 711 and 60 to 86), not run: `ZipArchive::new` loads every directory entry before the caller sees one, allocates `Vec::with_capacity(number_of_files)` from the count the archive declares (bounded only by the directory offset the archive also declares), and on a directory that does not parse falls back to earlier end records in the file; none of that can be charged to a `MemoryBudget` or refused from outside. Measured part of the reason (debug, counting allocator, `the_directory_of_many_entries_is_held_as_counted`): for a directory of 30,000 entries the crate's reader keeps 10.27 MiB with a peak of 16.22 MiB; this reader keeps 3.39 MiB with a peak of 4.07 MiB while it opens, and counts 4.07 MiB for the directory (the peak is level with the count; the 1.0 MiB counted for the readers is not in use at that moment). What the own reader does not read: archives in several parts, and archives with data in front that shifts every offset (self-extracting); entry names that are not UTF-8 are shown with replacement characters, not decoded as code page 437. The owner may still prefer the crate; then the directory check has to be solved another way.
- 1.16: What a batch is. The batches are cut by the `Batcher` with `PARSE_COST`; the lines of a batch go through the same steps as the lines adapter (validation pass, Polars parse with the `0xFF` separator, agreement check, stable group-by). A batch is handed out as a `LogsFrame` of the texts first seen in that batch, in order of first occurrence, label `unknown`, group null; a batch without a new text is not handed out. The rows of all batches in order are the distinct texts of the archive, the same for any batch size. `occurrences` in a batch frame counts that batch only; the count over the archive is `ZipInput::occurrences(text)` after the last batch (saturating at `u32::MAX`). `first_position` and `SkippedRecord::position` are packed positions: entry index in the directory in the upper 24 bits, 1-based line in the lower 40; `ArchiveSummary::locate` gives path and line; the first entry has index 0, so its positions are its line numbers. `EntryReport::records` counts skipped records too, and the entries add up to `input.records`.
- 1.16: Rules as built. Entry patterns are `glob::Pattern`s matched on the entry name, case-sensitive, and `*` and `?` also match `/` (so `*.log` selects the logs of every directory). The text check accepts a full 8 KiB head that ends inside a UTF-8 character. A gzip entry is found by its first two bytes and read with `MultiGzDecoder` (members that follow one another are one file); other nested archives are found by their first bytes (zip, bzip2, xz, zstd, 7z, lz4, compress, lzip, tar at offset 257), also inside a gzip entry. An entry that inflates to more than its directory entry declares is cut off there; one that holds less or fails its CRC-32 is reported when its end is reached; the records read until then are kept and counted (`Damaged` with `records > 0`). An entry of at most 8 KiB is whole in the head, so its checksum fails before a record of it is read. A record longer than the maximum is passed over without being held and reported as `record of N bytes is longer than the maximum record length of M bytes`. Directories and symbolic links (Unix mode in the external attributes) are not read. Entry names are never used as paths; nothing is written anywhere.
- 1.16: Memory accounting. Held and reported to the batcher after every batch: the directory (`size_of` of the directory entry, the report and a `String`, 120 bytes, plus the name, per entry; charged before the lists are allocated and while the names are read), the readers of one entry (512 KiB plus 8 times the maximum record length plus 500 bytes: 1.0 MiB by default, 8.5 MiB at 1 MiB), the deduplication set (a `HashMap<[u8; 16], u32>` keyed by the first 128 bits of the text's SHA-256: 21 bytes per slot, slots a power of two at most 7/8 full, so 24 to 48 bytes per distinct text), the skipped records (as in `Dedup`), and the table the set must grow to for the next batch (old and new table exist together while it grows). When that plus the fixed 4 MiB of a batch exceeds what the limit leaves, the read is refused with every part named (`reading the archive needs ... before its next record: the deduplication set of N distinct texts ...`); refusals of the batcher get the same parts appended. So under 512 MiB a read ends at about 7.3 million distinct texts: 2^23 slots (168 MiB) hold 7,340,032, and the next table wants 336 MiB beside them.
- 1.16: Measured on `data/ait-lds-v2/russellmitchell.zip` (7,132,670,599 bytes, 14,911 directory entries), release, one run each, on-request test `the_ait_archive_is_read_within_the_memory_limit` in `crates/l2d-core/tests/zip_memory.rs` (counting allocator around the system allocator; heap peak counted from before the archive is opened). (a) Pattern `*.log`, limit 512 MB, run twice with the same counts (the second time on the final code): 272 entries read, 14,639 match no pattern; 22,880,272 records in 27.0 and 27.1 s (845,000 records per second) after 5.4 and 5.8 s for opening (hashing at 1,230 to 1,326 MB/s); 5,642,082 distinct texts (2,220,922,519 bytes of text) in 351 batches of at most 65,536 rows; 6 skipped (1 empty line, 5 over-long); heap peak 334.4 MiB both times, 0.70 of the 480 MiB the limit leaves; held at the end 171.8 MiB; SHA-256 `2da85d89764afa4ea3aa2b7565d246ccba25681866a516ddd151834089b5ab4c`. (b) All entries, limit 512 MB: refused after 23,043,185 records of 12,342 entries with 7,277,347 distinct texts (the message names 168.0 MiB for the set, 336.0 MiB for its growth, 31.7 MiB for 272,799 skipped records, 2.8 MiB for the directory). (c) All entries, limit 3 GB: 11,870 entries read; not read 2,696 directories, 218 not text, 127 nested archives, none damaged; 30,916,764 records in 58.1 s (532,000 per second) after 11.4 s for opening; 8,276,854 distinct texts (4,903,206,079 bytes); 357,123 skipped (357,060 empty lines, 6 not UTF-8, 57 over-long); heap peak 1,066.3 MiB, 0.35 of what the limit leaves; held at the end 381.4 MiB. Opening took between 5.4 and 11.4 s over the runs (same file; other work was running on the host, and the difference was not investigated). Runs (b) and (c) were made before three small changes to the code (a lint fix in the directory reader, no room asked for a batch after the last one, wording of refusals); (a) and the binary runs below are on the final code. The release binary after review round 1, timed from outside with its peak working set polled every 200 ms, one run each, logged in `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\task-1.16\cli-timing-round1.log` (output beside it): `l2d --memory-limit 512MB ingest --input ...russellmitchell.zip --entry *.log` exits 0 after 31.6 s with a peak working set of 343.3 MiB; without `--entry` it exits 4 after 41.4 s with a peak of 345.9 MiB and the refusal of (b). (An earlier unlogged run gave 33.8 s and 350.8 MiB; the reviewer measured 33.5 s plus 8.9 s for opening on a busy host.)
- 1.16: Measured on built archives (debug, counting allocator, `archives_stay_within_their_memory_limits`, 200,000-line shapes, limits that leave 6, 16, 48 and 256 MiB, and 512 MiB for records of 1 MB): in all 43 reads, completed or refused, the heap grew by less than the limit leaves; the largest shares were 0.54 (200,000 distinct short lines at 16 MiB: 8.7 MiB), 0.37 (200,000 distinct lines of about 110 bytes at 16 MiB) and 0.36 (30,000 entries at 16 MiB). Sixty records of 1 MB with the maximum record length at 1 MiB: 177.1 MiB under 512 MiB (0.35), refused at 64 MiB. A line of 64 MiB that is skipped, plain or inside a gzip entry, costs 0.3 to 0.5 MiB for the whole read, against 1.0 MiB counted for the readers. Per entry the reader takes about 15 to 20 microseconds in a debug build (30,000 one-line entries in 0.44 to 0.81 s); the line buffer is reused across entries (it was 170 microseconds per entry before that).
- 1.16: `l2d ingest` reads an archive when the input is named `.zip` (any case) or `--adapter zip` is given; new options of `ingest`: `--entry PATTERN` (repeatable) and `--max-record-bytes SIZE`. The text output lists entries read, entries not read with reasons (entries that match no pattern and directories are counted only) and skipped records as `entry line N: reason`, each list up to `--limit`; names go through `shown_text`, so control characters in a name are escaped. JSON: the `input` object has the fields of an ordinary input plus `entries_total`, `entries_read`, `entries_not_read` (a count per reason), `entries` (every entry) and `skipped` items of `{position, entry, line, reason}`. With a damaged entry the whole report is printed and the command then fails with exit code 2 (`InvalidInput`); with `--json` standard output then holds the report line and the error envelope on the last line. The other commands refuse an archive with exit code 4 (`this command does not read zip archives yet`); before this task a `.zip` was read as lines.
- 1.16: Dependencies. `Cargo.lock` grows from 253 to 263 packages. Linked into the tool: `flate2` 1.1.11 (`rust_backend` only) with `miniz_oxide` 0.9.1, `crc32fast` 1.5.5, `adler2` 2.0.1, `simd-adler32` 0.3.10, and `glob` 0.3.4 (in the lock file before). Tests only: `zip` 2.6.1 (default features off, `deflate`), `zopfli` 0.8.4, `log` 0.4.35. In the lock file but not compiled: `arbitrary` and `derive_arbitrary` 1.5.0 (a fuzzing dependency of `zip`). Checked with `cargo tree -p l2d-core -e normal`, `cargo tree -p l2d-cli -e normal` (no `zip`), `cargo tree -i zip -e all`; the guard test over the lock file is green. `zip` 9.5.1 is the newest release and was not evaluated.
- 1.16: Tests. Default run: `crates/l2d-core/tests/zip_input.rs` (25: equality with the unpacked files at six batch sizes; single entry against the lines adapter's frame; positions; hash; patterns; binary, encrypted, unsupported method, directory and link entries; damaged entries in five ways; declared against real sizes; gzip, cut gzip, nested archives; over-long records; zip64 fields and a zip64 end record with 66,000 entries; hostile names; damaged and lying directories; refusals), `crates/l2d-core/tests/zip_memory.rs` (2, about 35 s in a debug build, the slowest of the new tests), `crates/l2d-cli/tests/archive.rs` (7, against the built binary). On request: `cargo test --release -j 4 -p l2d-core --test zip_memory -- --ignored --nocapture`, with `L2D_AIT_ARCHIVE`, `L2D_AIT_ENTRY` (patterns separated by `;`, a lone `;` for all entries), `L2D_AIT_LIMIT` and `L2D_AIT_BATCH_ROWS`.
- 1.16: For 1.17. (a) Parts written per batch carry batch-local occurrence counts; the totals exist only in the set after the last batch, and the set has no iterator yet, so the parts need a fix-up step or a counts part keyed by the text. (b) The place where deduplication on disk takes over is `ZipInput::require_held` (and `grow_for`): today it refuses. (c) After `finish` the budget still holds what the archive held; a command that goes on must set it back. (d) `--entry` and `--max-record-bytes` are on `IngestArgs`; `read_input` refuses the zip adapter; `baseline score` needs its own path over `batches`. (e) Positions are packed: every output must print them through `ArchiveSummary::locate`. (f) `RunRecord` has no place for the entry reports; requirement 13.13 for a run record is open. (g) Skipped records are held as a list (about 116 bytes each on AIT: 357,123 take 43 MiB); an archive of blank lines is refused by that, not by the set. (h) The batcher wants a source of fewer rows than its smallest batch in one batch, and judges a row by the first 256: sixty records of 1 MB need 349 MiB. (i) No work directory, no `.gitignore` entry and no `--work-dir` were added. (j) Nothing limits the time an archive may take (compression ratio, many entries over the same data, a gzip entry that inflates without end): memory stays bounded, the analyst has to interrupt.
- 1.16: For 1.19. The directory is a `Vec` of entries with their offsets, and opening an entry is self-contained (`locate_data`, then a reader over a handle of its own), so entries can be opened side by side; `File::try_clone` shares the file position, so parallel readers need handles opened separately. The record order is directory order and a position already names entry and line, so "first seen" has to be decided in directory order when batches of several entries are merged. The sequential figures to beat are those of measurement (a): 27 s for 22.9 million records of 272 entries, plus 5 to 6 s for the hash pass.
- 1.16: Design lines that no longer match (design.md): 53 ("`zip` 2.x ... `flate2` (arrives with `zip`)": the tool links `flate2` and `glob`, `zip` is for tests only); 464 to 478 (the sketch: `open` takes the budget, `batches` returns `ZipBatches`, `EntrySkip` has `Directory`, `Link` and `NotReached` too); 449 does not say how patterns treat `/`; 457 does not say that the room for the set's next growth is held before the batch. Also 1126 (exit codes: a damaged entry of an archive ends `ingest` with 2 after its report, a failed read of the archive file with 5) and 1128 ("one document per run ... the last line of standard output is always one whole document": `ingest` on an archive with a damaged entry prints the whole report and then the error envelope, two documents).
- 1.16: Not verified. Only `russellmitchell.zip` of the eight AIT archives was read. No archive written by a real tool with encryption, with entries above 4 GB, or with names in code page 437 was read (the AIT archive is above 4 GB as a whole and all of its 11,870 text entries passed their CRC-32). The behaviour of the `zip` crate on a hostile directory was read in its source, not run. Free memory falling during an archive read (smaller batches) is the batcher's tested behaviour and was not exercised with an archive. Nothing was run on Linux. Except for measurement (a), all timings and peaks are single runs.
- 1.16, review round 1 (2026-11-13), B1. What U+FEFF means: at the very start of a file or of an entry (for a gzip entry, of its content) it is a byte-order mark and is dropped, once; anywhere else it is text, including a second one at the start. The fault was in the shared path: Polars drops one U+FEFF at the start of every buffer it parses, so a record that starts with one and is the first accepted record of a batch read differently from the validation pass, and the read failed with `Polars and the validation pass disagree` for some batch sizes only. It hit the zip input, the lines adapter (also a plain file that starts with two marks, at any batch size) and the CSV adapter (an unquoted first field that starts with the mark). Fix, in the one place all three share: `Accepted::push_raw` puts a second mark in front of the batch buffer when the buffer starts with one, for Polars to drop (three bytes, no copy of the batch; it looks at the buffer after the push, so it does not depend on how a record is cut into calls). `parse_csv_records` documents the rule for callers that do not go through `Accepted`. JSONL was checked and is not affected: a line with the mark before the object is not JSON and is skipped by the validation pass, and a mark inside a text is kept (tested at four batch sizes). CSIC goes through the same `Accepted` and is covered by the fix; no CSIC test with a mark was added. Tests, red before the fix (7 failed: 4 of 5 in `crates/l2d-core/tests/byte_order_mark.rs`, 3 of 27 in `tests/zip_input.rs`, output in `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\task-1.16\red-round1.txt`): a mark-led line at batch sizes 1, 2, 3 and default for the lines adapter and the zip input, after a full default batch of 65,536 lines for both, two marks at the start of a file and of an entry, a mark-led CSV field, a gzip entry, and the archive-against-unpacked equality test with a fourth file of marks. `crates/l2d-cli/tests/archive.rs` runs the reviewer's reproduction through the binary.
- 1.16, review round 1, advisories. A2: the deduplication key is 128 bits of SHA-256; two texts with one key are silently taken for one; finding such a pair on purpose takes about 2^64 hash computations, by accident the chance is about n^2 / 2^129; the comment at `text_key` says so. A6: a read of the archive file that fails is now `L2dError::Runtime` (exit 5), not a damaged entry: the file sits under the entry's readers behind `Watched`, which notes an error of the file itself, and `locate_data` returns `DataFault::{Damaged, Io}`. Tested at the seam with a reader that fails (unit test in `zip_input.rs`: a failure under a stored and under a deflated entry is noted, a short entry and a wrong checksum are not); not tested through a real failing disk or through the binary, for which there is no seam. A7: JSON `position` stays a number, as in the other commands; it exceeds 2^53 from entry index 8,192 on, and `entry` and `line` are the fields to use (said in `ingest.rs`). A8: the set now grows for the texts of a batch that are new, not for all its distinct rows (the keys are computed once into a list of 16 bytes a row); the room held before a batch is still for the largest batch that fits, which is an upper bound. A9: the text output counts entries that match no pattern and directories and lists the others, JSON lists every entry. A4: `data/README.md` corrected with the tool's own count (on-request unit test `the_directory_of_the_ait_archive_is_counted`): 14,911 directory entries, 2,696 directories, 12,215 files of which 11,992 are not empty, 14,975,678,744 bytes unpacked.
- 1.16, review round 1, A5: where this reader can differ from other zip readers (also in the module documentation). The directory is authoritative: an entry's own header is used only for the two lengths that say where its data starts; method, flags, sizes and checksum in it are not compared with the directory, and a difference surfaces as size or checksum damage when the entry is read. The end record is the last one whose comment ends at the end of the file, so an end-record look-alike inside the archive comment that reaches the end of the file wins. Directory entries beyond the end record's count are ignored, and so is data no directory entry points to. Entries whose data overlaps are each read. Added: `mutated_archives_are_read_or_refused_within_the_memory_limit` in `tests/zip_memory.rs` (default run, about 5 s): 1,500 archives with one to four seeded changes, three quarters of them in the directory and the end records; 801 were read (411 with a damaged entry), 699 were not an archive or had a damaged directory, none panicked, and the largest heap growth was 0.91 MiB under a limit that leaves 16 MiB. It found no fault in the reader. A unit test holds the table-size model to the capacities the standard map gives. Test counts after round 1, default run: `tests/zip_input.rs` 27, `tests/zip_memory.rs` 3 (about 40 s in a debug build), `tests/byte_order_mark.rs` 5, unit tests in `zip_input.rs` 2 (and one on request), `l2d-cli/tests/archive.rs` 8.
- 1.16: Owner decisions of 2026-11-13, which settle the open points above. (1) The own zip-directory reader stays; the `zip` crate is used in tests only. (2) Lines read from an entry before its damage is found are kept and counted, and the entry is reported as damaged. (3) A run with a damaged entry exits with code 2 after its report.
- 1.16, review round 2 (2026-11-14): approved. The reviewer probed the mark rule through the binary for lines, CSV, JSONL and archives (deflated and gzip entries) at batch sizes from 1 to 65,536 and with 200,000 distinct mark-led lines; the counts were the same at every batch size. Left open: exit code 5 for a failed read of the archive file is tested at the seam only, not through the binary. Seen and older than this task: a CSV file that starts with two marks still finds its header, so the second mark is dropped there too (for a CSV header the "dropped once" rule does not hold; it does not depend on the batch size). design.md was brought in line with the list above on 2026-11-14.
- 2.6: API as built (`crates/l2d-encoder/src/session.rs`, re-exported from the crate root). `EncoderSession::open(pkg: &EncoderPackage, paths: &ProjectPaths) -> Result<Self, EncoderError>` takes the runtime directory from `L2D_RUNTIME_DIR` or `runtime/ryzenai/`; `open_at(pkg, paths, runtime_dir)` takes it from the caller (a command with a directory option). There is no `allow_cpu` and no CPU provider anywhere in the crate's library. `encode(&mut self, texts: &[&str]) -> Result<Vec<Encoded>, EncoderError>` with `Encoded { vector: Vec<f32>, input: TextInput }`; `encode_into(texts, &mut Vec<f32>, &mut Vec<TextInput>)` appends `dimension` values per text and the flags (no allocation per text; on an error both buffers keep their length); `device()` is `Device::Npu`; `info() -> &SessionInfo` (identity, input mode, rows, runtime release, ONNX Runtime version, source, device, how it was matched, driver version, `PlacementReport`, `ExecutedNodes`, cache key and folder, whether the provider compiled now, open, load and session seconds, the buffers' bytes); `stats() -> &EncodeStats` (texts, images, seconds end to end, and of those writing, run, reading); `finish(self) -> RunRecord`.
- 2.6: The order of `open_at`, each step an error with the reason. (1) `RuntimeFiles::check(dir, Fast)`, before anything is loaded: `RuntimeMissing { file, expected_at }` or `RuntimeRefused { reason }`. (2) `NpuStatus::query()`: `NpuUnavailable` with the device listing when no AMD device of the class is present (which is also what a missing driver looks like); it is asked only when the files are right. (3) `session::provider_model(pkg, release)`: `read_model()` (size and hash checked again), `onnx_edit::with_rows_as_height(model, 64)`, the rewritten graph read back and held to one input `[1, channels, 64, input_bytes]` and one output `[1, dimension, 64, 1]`; a failure is `ModelRefused`. (4) The runtime is loaded once per process (`ort::init_from`), after `XLNX_ONNX_EP_REPORT_FILE` was set in-process; a second runtime directory in the same process is `Failed`. (5) The session is created from memory on the Vitis AI provider with `cache_dir` = `<project>/runtime/cache` (created, plain path), `cache_key` = `encode::provider_cache_key(revision, release, rewritten bytes, session::PROVIDER_OPTIONS)` and `PROVIDER_OPTIONS` = `[("enable_cache_file_io_in_mem", "0")]`; a provider that does not register or does not open the model is `NpuUnavailable`. (6) The model is run once on an image without a text with ONNX Runtime's profiling on; profiling ends with that run and the profile file (beside the cache folders, `<key>-first-run-<process>-<count>_<time>.json`) is removed, also when no session comes of it. (7) The report `vitisai_ep_report.json` in the cache folder must satisfy `PlacementReport::npu_only(nodes, input, output)` and the profile `ExecutedNodes::npu_only()`, else `NpuUnavailable` with the nodes named. `EncoderError` converts into `L2dError` (`Missing`, `Refused`, `Runtime`) for the exit codes.
- 2.6: RESULT, requirement 4.4: the provider's placement report is not enough to call a session NPU only, and the session does not rely on it alone. The report is written when the provider compiles; a start from the cache finds the report of that earlier compilation, and the provider does not write it again. On the reference host, with the bytes of `context.json` in the cache folder set to zero (or the file cut in half) and the report left as it was, the provider opened a session without an error that ran the model on the CPU provider: 50 images in 0.619 s against 0.087 s, other vectors, beside a report of 33 of 35 nodes on the NPU (`damage-1.log`; the first version of the session accepted it). ONNX Runtime's profile of a run tells the two apart: on the NPU three nodes run through `VitisAIExecutionProvider` (`QuantizeLinear`, `vitis_ai_ep_<n>`, `DequantizeLinear`) and none elsewhere; in the session that fell back the two quantize nodes run through it and nine nodes on `CPUExecutionProvider` (`Transpose`, `QLinearConv`, ...). `ExecutedNodes::npu_only` accepts, through `VitisAIExecutionProvider`, only nodes named `vitis_ai_ep_<n>` (the parts the provider compiled; at least one must have run) and the two ends (`QuantizeLinear`, `DequantizeLinear`); through `CPUExecutionProvider` at most one `QuantizeLinear` and at most one `DequantizeLinear`; every other node is refused and named in the error: another operator through the Vitis provider (a `Conv`), another or a further node on the CPU provider, any node on any other provider. With it the damaged folder gives no session, and the error names the folder to remove (`final3-damage.log`). The other damages tried: the compiled model (`compiled.*.xmodel`) removed, zeroed or cut gives a provider error at open (no session, the error now names the cache folder); `context.json` removed makes the provider compile again; the report removed gives no session (`final3-no-report.log`), and removing the folder as the message says makes the provider compile again. The session never deletes a cache folder itself (another process may be compiling into it).
- 2.6: The rule on the report, `PlacementReport::npu_only`: the report must list exactly the nodes of the model that was handed over (35 for architecture v1), at least one node must be on the NPU, and a node on the CPU must be the `QuantizeLinear` whose first input is the model input or the `DequantizeLinear` that produces the model output, matched by the tensor names of the rewritten graph and not by the operator alone (a quantize node inside the graph on the CPU is refused). Fewer nodes on the CPU than those two are accepted. On the reference host: 33 of 35 on the NPU, the two ends on the CPU (`VITIS_EP_CPU` in the report: they run through the provider, as the profile shows), for the spike fixture and for the trained model.
- 2.6: Two truncation flags (requirement 1.5 as amended): `TextInput::truncated` as before (the part the mode reads is longer than the input) and the new `TextInput::text_longer_than_input` (the whole text has more than `input_bytes` bytes, whatever the mode). Every way to an input reports both (`describe`, `byte_ids`, `encode_text`, `push`, `ImageInput::write`, `RowImage::fill`). `TextInput` stays 32 bytes, so `InputBatch::held_bytes` is unchanged. The flags describe a text and change no value of an input: nothing the `/1` of `REVISION_DOMAIN` stands for moved, the revisions are unchanged (the published model packages to `099570ec739c` as in 2.12), and the vectors of CSIC's 25,608 texts have the checksum 2.12 printed (1570811.2676). On CSIC in `payload` mode: 4,821 texts truncated, 9,681 longer than the input as a whole, 1,634 with an empty input.
- 2.6: The seam and what moved into `src`. `session::ImageModel` (one method: run the model on one image) with `ImageEncoder<M>` around it holds everything that is not the device: the images, the order, the flags, the last image that is not full, reading and normalising, the counters. The tool's one implementation is the session on the NPU; `tests/encoder_session.rs` puts a formula of the test file behind it (no model is run on any provider). `encode::RowImage` is the session's image buffer. The placement reader moved from `tests/npu_support` into `session::PlacementReport` (`npu_support::Placement` is now that type; `cpu_operators` became a method), and the profile reader of the 2.2 gate has a library form, `session::ExecutedNodes`. `HeightEncoder` and `Rig` stay in `tests/npu_support` for the 2.12 measurements of other settings. `ort` is now a normal dependency of `l2d-encoder` (it was a dev-dependency), same version and features; `Cargo.lock` is unchanged.
- 2.6: Run record (`SessionInfo::run_record`, `finish`): `kind` encode, `started_utc` (when `open` was called), `duration_ms` since then, `encoder`, `device` npu, `driver_version`, `logs_per_second` (texts over the seconds spent inside the encoding calls, from texts to unit vectors in the caller's buffers; none before the first text), `placement` (node counts of the report, source `provider_report`). `RunRecord` has no field for the rest, so it is in `settings` (an object, so `record_batch_plan` still works): `npu.matched_by` (`known_device_id` or `amd_vendor_only`), `npu.instance_id`, `npu.description`, `runtime.release`, `runtime.onnxruntime`, `runtime.source`, `session` (rows, cache key, compiled now, open seconds, buffers' bytes), `placement.all_nodes`, `placement.cpu_operators`, `placement.first_run_nodes_on_provider` and `placement.first_run_nodes_elsewhere` (the profile), `encoding` (texts, images, seconds by stage), `input_mode`. `inputs` is empty: the caller adds the sources.
- 2.6: Measured on the reference host, 2026-11-13, release build, AMD Ryzen AI 1.9.0 (NuGet files), NPU driver 32.0.20102.4480, logs under `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\task-2.6\logs\`; `final3-*.log` are the runs on the committed code. Fixture (spike model, science table): 70 texts, 128-dimensional vectors of length 1 within 1e-6, 33 of 35 nodes on the NPU, run record printed (`final3-fixture.log`). Rows (trained model, CSIC, session API only): 677 texts in one call against the same texts in four other orders (2,708 vectors), 128 of them alone, one text in each of the 64 rows and in images of 1 to 64 identical rows, an image of two after an image of 64: 0 vectors differ, tolerance zero (`final3-rows.log`). Start-up at 64 rows, the first-run check included: cold 3.06 s (`final3-startup-cold.log`; before that check 2.99 to 3.49 s for the trained model and 2.55 to 3.83 s for the fixture), warm in a fresh process 0.64 s (`final3-startup-warm.log`; 0.57 to 0.96 s before), warm in the same process 0.27 to 0.75 s; requirement 4.7 asks for 30 s.
- 2.6: Throughput, from log text to unit vector, CSIC's 25,608 distinct texts, five timed passes after one untimed. On the committed code, three runs (`final3-throughput-1.log` to `-3.log`): median 28,391, 32,582 and 34,236 logs/s (single passes 27,292 to 36,896); sustained over 102,432 texts in one call 28,112 to 32,821; `encode` (a vector allocated per text) 23,823 to 31,580; per log 29.4 to 35.4 us, of which the run 25.3 to 29.2 us (83 to 86 %), writing the image 3.8 to 5.8 us (13 to 16 %), reading and normalising 0.2 to 0.3 us; process CPU 11.1 to 15.5 s per million logs. Six runs before the first-run check was added (`throughput-1.log` to `-3.log`, `final-throughput-1.log` to `-3.log`): median 27,044 to 41,348. Whether the profile of the first run costs anything afterwards was not separated from that spread (the ranges overlap). These figures are above 2.12's (16,636 at 64 rows), and the session is not the reason: 2.12's own test, run again in the same hour (`compare-2.12-throughput-1.log`), gave 38,451 logs/s sustained for its `HeightEncoder` at 64 rows with float input (15,224 in its first setting, 26,480 to 29,553 in three others) and 38,792 for the run alone, against 17,173 and 21,693 in 2.12's own logs. The range to state for this session is therefore: medians of 27,044 to 42,217 logs/s over the ten runs up to the review (the reviewer's run included). The gap to 2.12's figure is not explained by what was measured. Host load does not explain it: 16,636 was the quieter of 2.12's two runs, and the process CPU fell from 33 s per million logs in 2.12 to 11.1 to 15.5 s here. Reading of the owner, 2026-11-13 (a reading, not a finding): the host is a laptop with power management, and the difference is related to the system itself; that was not measured and is not pursued. Timings on this host are stated as ranges.
- 2.6: The image writer. A writer that goes channel by channel over all texts of an image (`encode::RowImage::fill`) was built, held bit for bit to `ImageInput::write` and `clear_row` (unit test and `tests/byte_codec_speed.rs`), and measured: it gains nothing. Writers alone on one thread, CSIC, 15 alternating passes (`writers-1.log`): `payload` mode 3.14 us per log against 3.21 for the 2.12 writer and 3.00 for the codec's batch layout; `whole` mode 3.75 against 3.71 and 3.58. So the 13.8 us of 2.12 was not the layout: inside the session `RowImage` costs 3.4 to 6.4 us today, and the 2.12 writer in 2.12's test 3.7 us in its sustained setting. `RowImage` is kept as the session's buffer (it owns the image and the flags and allocates nothing after it is made, which a test counts), not as a speed-up. A second thread for the writer and uint8 input were not tried (uint8 is not adopted, owner decision).
- 2.6: Memory. The session's own buffers are fixed and stated from the code: `ImageEncoder::held_bytes()` = 4,409,856 bytes for architecture v1 at 64 rows and dimension 128 (image 4,194,304; the byte table twice, 131,072; the bytes of one image's texts 16,384; a length and the flags per row 2,560; the model's output 32,768; one image's vectors 32,768); a test asserts the sum, and another that filling images allocates nothing. Measured for the process (`final3-throughput-1.log` to `-3.log`): opening the session adds 201 to 203 MiB working set and 182 to 184 MiB private commit (runtime, provider and compiled model; 4.2 MiB of it are the session's buffers); with the session open the process holds 223 to 224 MiB working set; after encoding 249 to 250 MiB, peak 303 to 305 MiB, of which the test's own vector buffers are 12.5 MiB per pass and 50 MiB for the sustained call. The per-text cost of `encode` is the caller's `Vec<f32>` (512 bytes of values for dimension 128); `encode_into` adds nothing per text.
- 2.6: Tests. Default suite: `tests/encoder_session.rs` (18 since the remediation round, 15 before: order, partial images, both flags, the same vector whatever the row, rows after the last text are empty inputs, the callers' buffers on an error, a model output that is zero or not finite, the buffers' bytes, the placement reader and rule, the profile reader and rule, a `Conv` through the Vitis provider and a node on an unknown provider refused, the rewritten model and its cache key, a model that cannot be rewritten or changed after the package was opened, the three runtime refusals of `open_at` with nothing loaded or cached, the run record), 4 unit tests in `session.rs` (runtime files before the device, no NPU, the timestamp, the error envelope), 3 in `encode.rs` (`RowImage`), 1 in `byte_codec.rs` (the second flag), 1 in `byte_codec_speed.rs` (no allocation). - 2.6: Deviations from the design's contract. (a) `EncoderError` has five variants, not three: `RuntimeMissing`, `RuntimeRefused` (wrong files are not missing files), `NpuUnavailable`, `ModelRefused`, `Failed`. (b) `Encoded` carries `input: TextInput` (both flags, `empty`, the byte counts) instead of one `truncated` field. (c) `open_at`, `encode_into`, `info`, `stats`, `ImageModel`, `ImageEncoder` and `ExecutedNodes` are additions. (d) `open` runs the model once (an image without a text) to read the profile; it is not counted in the statistics. (e) The runtime files are checked at `CheckDepth::Fast`, so `runtime.source` in the run record is what the directory's record says; a full check (0.9 s of hashing per open) was not put into `open`. (f) A device whose driver version cannot be read still opens; the record then has no driver version. (g) The device query runs before the model is rewritten, so on a host without an NPU a bad model is not reported. (h) `.kiro/steering/` does not exist in this repository (neither in the worktree nor in the main checkout); the steering was taken from design.md and the task notes.
- 2.6: Design lines that are now stale (design.md, EncoderSession): the Intent "or on the CPU when allowed"; "Requirements ... 4.5" (withdrawn); `pub enum EncoderError { NpuUnavailable, RuntimeMissing, Failed }` (five variants); `pub struct Encoded { vector, truncated }` (`input: TextInput`); `TextInput { part_bytes: u64, read_bytes: u32, nul_bytes: u32, truncated, empty }` (the counts are `usize`, and there is `text_longer_than_input`); "`Placement` is read from that report" and "returns `NpuUnavailable` when the provider cannot be registered or no NPU nodes are placed" (the report and the profile of the first run must both hold; also refused: a node other than the two ends on the CPU, a missing report, a report of another model); "The session writes the texts of a run into an image (`encode::ImageInput` ...)" (`encode::RowImage`); the measured line "about 16,000 to 17,000 logs/s with one session at 64 rows ... ready in about 5 s cold and 1 s with a warm cache" (see the figures above); "`truncated` is true when that part has more than `input_bytes` bytes" (two flags); `finish` "identity, device, driver, logs per second, placement" (also the runtime release and source, how the device was matched and the first run's providers, in `settings`). Stale as well, outside the EncoderSession section (added in the remediation round): design.md line 15 "with a CPU fallback the analyst must allow" (there is none); line 295, the requirements table's row 4.4 "Fallback only when allowed" (NPU only, no fallback); line 296, its row 4.5 "Mark CPU runs" (requirement withdrawn); tasks.md line 267, task 2.6's "_Requirements: ... 4.5 ..._" (withdrawn); tasks.md line 419, "encoding batches for the NPU stay at the model's fixed 32" (the session runs images of 64 rows). In the code: the module documentation of `package.rs` gives the tensor layout as `x[n, c, 0, t]`; what the NPU is fed is `x[0, c, n, t]` of the rewritten graph, with the same values per text. For the owner: the hardware gate of task 2.2 (`tests/npu_gate.rs`, on request) still opens the spike model on the CPU provider for its comparison; that is older than the decision that no CPU provider runs anywhere and was not touched here.
- 2.6: For 2.7. (1) The store must record the runtime release beside the encoder identity and refuse vectors of another release: take it from `session.info().runtime_release` (also `settings.runtime.release` of the run record); the provider compiles per release and the vectors differ between releases (2.3 review). (2) Pass distinct texts; the session does not deduplicate. Use `encode_into` for many texts: one flat buffer of `dimension` values per text, in input order, and one `TextInput` per text; both flags are there for the output, and `empty` marks texts with an all-zero input (they all share one vector: 1,634 of CSIC's texts in `payload` mode). (3) Add the inputs and the batch plan to the record `finish()` returns. (4) One process, one runtime directory; several sessions in a process are possible (the runtime is loaded once), each holds about 200 MiB. (5) A hardware-free test of the integration can put an `ImageModel` of its own behind `ImageEncoder`; only `EncoderSession` touches the device. (6) The cache folder is `<project>/runtime/cache/<rev12>-<release>-<16 hex>`; nothing removes old folders (a new release or a new rewrite leaves the old one behind, about 1.7 MB each). (7) An `NpuUnavailable` that names a cache folder is repaired by removing that folder; a command could offer that.
- 2.6: Not tried or not verified. The refusal by the report's rule was not provoked on the device (no public way to make the provider's compiler fail; the rule is tested on reports written by the test); the refusal by the profile was (the damaged `context.json`), and so were the missing report and the damaged compiled model. A host without an NPU, without the driver or with another driver or runtime release did not run; those refusals are tested through the device list and the file check only. An interrupted compile (a cache folder half written by the provider itself) was not produced. Two processes opening the same encoder cold at the same time were not run. `ort::init_from` failing on a DLL that passed the file check was not provoked. The first run is the only run that is profiled: a session that would leave the NPU later (the device lost while encoding) is not detected by this check; what the provider does then is unknown. Whether a later release names the provider's nodes differently in the report or the profile is unknown. For the profile the rule then refuses: through the Vitis provider it accepts only the names `vitis_ai_ep_<n>`, `QuantizeLinear` and `DequantizeLinear`, so a compiled part under another name gives no session, with the name in the error (as first committed in 6b929bb it accepted any name that was not one of the two quantize operators; the remediation closed that). For the report, `PlacementReport::parse` reads only the devices `NPU` and `CPU`; a node under another device name is an error and gives no session.
- 2.6: Remediation round after the review of 6b929bb (2026-11-14). (1) `ExecutedNodes::npu_only` is as tight as the notes above now say (`session::CPU_PROVIDER`, `session::COMPILED_PART_PREFIX`); the tests were written first and seen failing on 6b929bb's rule (a `Conv` through the Vitis provider and a node on an unknown provider were accepted). On the reference host every session of the six hardware tests showed, in the profile of its first run, `QuantizeLinear`, `vitis_ai_ep_<n>` (n from 1 to 21 within one process), `DequantizeLinear` through the Vitis provider and nothing elsewhere, and the damaged `context.json` was refused by the tightened rule (`remediation-1.log`: 2 nodes through the provider, 0 of them compiled parts, 9 on the CPU provider). (2) The session is now created with ONNX Runtime's `session.disable_cpu_ep_fallback=1` (`SessionBuilder::with_disable_cpu_fallback`). With AMD Ryzen AI 1.9.0 the session on the NPU opens with it and the six hardware tests pass (`remediation-2-disable-cpu-fallback.log`); the damaged `context.json` is then refused earlier, by ONNX Runtime when the session is created ("This session contains graph nodes that are assigned to the default CPU EP, but fallback to CPU EP has been explicitly disabled by the user"), so on this release the profile rule is the second line behind it. It is a session option, not a provider option, and is not part of the cache key. (3) A model output that cannot be a unit vector is an error: `encode::read_rows` returns the first row that was read whose length is zero or not finite, and `ImageEncoder` turns it into `EncoderError::Failed` naming the text; no text of that image is handed over, `encode_into` leaves the callers' buffers as they were. Rows after the last text are not looked at, and the run at `open` (an image without a text) is not checked this way. CSIC's 25,608 texts pass it with the checksum unchanged. (4) Throughput in those two runs, for the record and inside the reading above (not looked into): medians 16,768 and 22,336 logs/s (single passes 14,436 to 25,585), process CPU 28.9 and 21.0 s per million logs; these are below the range of the ten earlier runs. (5) Left open from the review's advisory list: the device and the driver version are not in the cache key; `plain()` on a path of a network share; the `deviceStat` cross-check of the report that was dropped; the CPU provider comparison in `tests/npu_gate.rs` (task 2.2). The first setting of 2.12's test ran at half the speed of its later settings today and that was not explained.
- 2.6, review round 2 (2026-11-14): approved at bb1ac0a. The reviewer ran `npu_session` on the NPU (6 passed, CSIC checksum 1570811.2676 as in task 2.12, median 34,015 logs/s) and traced the profile rule by reading. Known and left as they are: a model whose output for a text is all zeros now fails the `encode` call for that image; with `session.disable_cpu_ep_fallback=1` a damaged `context.json` is refused at session creation, so the profile rule is no longer reached by that case on the device (its device evidence is `remediation-1.log`, and the default tests); `COMPILED_PART_PREFIX` matches the bare prefix without a number; `l2_normalize` divides by `max(norm, 1e-12)`, so a norm below that passes the check without giving a unit vector (not reachable with this model). design.md and tasks.md lines of the stale list above were brought in line on 2026-11-14.

- 2.15: How to rerun. Step 1, from the repository root, PATH reduced to `C:\Windows\System32;C:\Windows;<cargo bin>` (a Python on PATH fails the hardware tests): set `L2D_RUNTIME_DIR` (runtime\ryzenai), `L2D_SCIENCE_DIR` (models\science-2.1), `L2D_CSIC_CSV`, `L2D_NPU_WORK_DIR` and `L2D_EXPORT_DIR` (used: `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\task-2.15` and its `vectors`), then `cargo test --release -p l2d-encoder --test npu_embedding_export -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads=1` (about 7 s; log `export-1.log`). Step 2, fresh shell, from `tools\embedding-qualification`: `uv sync`, then `pwsh -NoProfile -File run_all.ps1 -Vectors <vectors dir> -Record D:\Source\log2decision\models\science-2.1\record.json -Logs <logs dir>` (about 6 minutes, most of it UMAP and k-means in `q3_structure.py`); pictures go to `docs/images/2026-11-14-embedding-qualification/`, figures to `q0.log` to `q4.log`. Seeds are fixed (`eq.SEED`); two full runs gave the same figures.
- 2.15: RESULT. Not noise and not the spike model's one-point collapse, but the vectors are close to one-dimensional: 12,079 distinct inputs give 11,052 distinct vectors, random pairs have median cosine 0.83 (9.0 % above 0.99, 0.36 % above 0.9998), the first principal component holds 87.9 % of the variance, two hold 95.1 %, participation ratio 1.3 of 128 (random unit vectors 126.7). Small edits stay at 0.995 or more and attacks move away from their benign request (median 0.38), endpoints group among good texts (neighbour agreement 0.80 against 0.20 by chance) although the path is not read; on the three held-out endpoints the linear head ranks at ROC AUC 0.939 (the first principal component alone 0.936), while every held-out good text has 9 or more bad texts among its 11 nearest fitting-side texts (ROC AUC 0.791 on distinct inputs, 0.551 with repeated inputs counted). Verdict in the document: head suitable with limits, neighbour scorer suitable only where the store holds texts of the same endpoint, outlier ranking not checked and doubtful. This is a finding for the owner before 2.8; whether a dimensional collapse counts as the task's stop condition was not decided here.
- 2.15: For 2.7 and 2.8, from the measurements. (a) In `payload` mode 25,608 distinct texts are only 12,079 distinct inputs (11,803 inputs occur once by GET and once by POST): neighbours must be taken over distinct inputs, or each text finds its own twin first and k = 11 covers about six inputs. (b) 1,634 CSIC texts (1,614 bad) have an empty input and share one vector; every log line without `?` or line feed does, so for plain log lines this encoder gives one point. (c) 381 vectors are shared by 1,408 inputs that differ in digits. (d) A linear head fitted on shuffled labels scored further than 0.2 from 0.5 on the held-out texts in 14 of 20 runs (0.052 to 0.914): a single shuffled-label control proves nothing on these vectors; repeat it. (e) Probes: upper case moves a benign payload to the attack side; `|ping` in a parameter stays on the benign side; nothing after byte 256 of the payload changes the vector.
