- 1.17: API as built, result parts: `l2d_core::parts` (re-exported from the crate root: `WorkDir`, `PartWriter`, `PartTable`, `PartInfo`). `WorkDir::create(&Path) -> Result<WorkDir, L2dError>` makes the directory with the directories above it and refuses a path that exists and is not an empty directory; `path()`; `require_free(&[(&str, u64)]) -> Result<u64, L2dError>` compares the free space of the volume with the sum of the named needs and returns the free bytes; `require_free_of(free, needed)` is the same check on a given figure. `free_disk_bytes(&Path)` is `GetDiskFreeSpaceExW` (the bytes this user may use) on Windows and `L2dError::Runtime` elsewhere. `PartWriter::new(&WorkDir, name)` (the name is lower-case letters, digits and `-`), `write(&mut DataFrame) -> Result<Option<PartInfo>, L2dError>` (one file `<name>-NNNNNN.parquet`, zstd, one row group, written on one thread; a frame without rows writes nothing; a file that exists is never replaced), `rows()`, `parts()`, `finish() -> PartTable { name, columns: Vec<PartColumn { name, dtype }>, rows, rows_sha256, parts: Vec<PartInfo { file, rows, bytes, sha256 }> }` with `bytes()`. `write_manifest(&WorkDir, &impl Serialize) -> Result<PathBuf, L2dError>` writes `manifest.json` under a temporary name and renames it; `read_part(&Path) -> Result<DataFrame, L2dError>`; `MANIFEST_FILE`. A part takes columns of type String, UInt64, UInt32, Float32 and Boolean.
- 1.17: API as built, deduplication on disk, on `ZipInput`: `spill_into(&Path, max_set_texts: Option<u64>)` names the directory for it (absent or empty; created; removed with its files when the archive is finished or dropped) and must be called before `batches`; `hold_beside(u64)` (what the caller holds for the whole read, counted as held); `selected_bytes() -> SelectedBytes { entries, packed, unpacked }` (what the entries that will be opened declare); `disk_dedup() -> Option<DiskDedup { set_texts, spilled_records, spilled_bytes, spilled_distinct, partitions }>` (`Some` once the set in memory was frozen); `occurrence_counts() -> OccurrenceCounts<'_>`, an `Iterator<Item = Result<Vec<u32>, L2dError>>` of the counts over the archive of every row handed out, in row order, in chunks of at most 65,536; `ZipBatches::entry_path(u32) -> Option<&str>`. Added to existing code: `Batcher::finish() -> Result<(BatchPlan, &mut MemoryBudget), L2dError>` and `Batcher::smallest_batch_bytes()`, `ProjectPaths::work_dir()` (`<project>/work`), `zip_input::table_bytes` became `pub(crate)`. The method is in `l2d-core/src/zip_spill.rs` (private module).
- 1.17: How deduplication on disk works. The set of 128-bit keys stays in memory as in 1.16. When its next growth, the fixed 4 MiB of a batch and the smallest batch no longer fit together (checked before a batch for the largest batch that fits, and again in the batch for its new texts), or when it holds `max_set_texts` texts, the set is frozen: it goes on counting the texts it holds and takes no new one. From then on every row of a batch whose text is not in the set is appended to one of 64 files chosen by the first byte of its key (key, position, count in its batch, text; raw, not compressed) and is not handed out. After the last entry each partition is read twice: once to add up the counts per key in a set of this partition's keys only, once to write the first row of every key with its count. The 64 results are in position order and are merged by position into frames, which `batches` hands out after the frames of the read. Every text of the frozen set was first seen before any text on disk, so the rows of all frames are the distinct texts in order of first occurrence, as in memory alone. A frame from disk is cut like a batch: at most the largest batch of rows, and no more text than a sixth of what the limit leaves.
- 1.17: Occurrence counts (note (a) of 1.16). A row handed out during the read still carries the count of its batch; a row from disk carries its count over the archive. For the counts of all rows, the key of every row handed out during the read is appended to `keys.bin` (16 bytes a row) and the count of every row from disk to `counts.bin`; `occurrence_counts` reads the first against the set and then the second. So the counts are a second table of parts (`occurrences`: `row`, `occurrences`), written after the last entry, not a column of the scores. `ZipInput::occurrences(text)` answers for the texts of the set in memory only once the read went on on disk.
- 1.17: `l2d baseline score` on an archive (`crates/l2d-cli/src/cmd/baseline_archive.rs`). New options: `--work-dir DIR`, `--entry PATTERN`, `--max-record-bytes SIZE` (the last two moved from `IngestArgs` into `ArchiveArgs` in `common.rs`, which both commands flatten); on an input that is not an archive each of them is an analyst error (exit 2). Without `--work-dir` the run writes to `<project>/work/baseline-score-<yyyymmddThhmmssZ>/`; `/work/` is in `.gitignore`. The directory must be empty or absent (exit 4 otherwise, nothing touched). Written: `scores-NNNNNN.parquet`, one part per batch that has new texts and one per frame from disk, with `row` (from 0, in order of first occurrence), `entry` (index in the archive's directory), `line`, `text`, `ratio`, `no_features`; `occurrences-NNNNNN.parquet`; `manifest.json` last. The manifest holds the scorer (kind, name, revision), the settings (patterns, maximum record length), the archive (path, SHA-256, records, distinct texts, skipped count, every entry with its records or the reason it was not read: requirement 13.13 for this run), and per table columns, rows, `rows_sha256` and the parts with rows, bytes and SHA-256. Standard output: the first `--limit` rows as they are scored (default 20, also with `--json`; JSON rows carry the entry's path and the line), the archive report of `ingest`, the `parts` object or lines (directory, manifest, tables, whether the deduplication went to disk, free and estimated disk space), the memory report. With a damaged entry the parts and the manifest are written, the report is printed and the command exits with 2, as `ingest` does. A run that fails leaves its parts without a manifest: a work directory without `manifest.json` is a run that did not end.
- 1.17: The free-disk check, before anything is read. Estimated for the parts: 1 MiB, the bytes the selected entries take in the archive (zstd in place of deflate) and a quarter of their unpacked bytes for the numbers of the rows. Estimated for deduplication on disk: their unpacked bytes (every text not in the set is written once per batch it occurs in, never more than the records read; plus 32 bytes a row). The command refuses with exit 4 when the free space of the volume is less than the sum and states the sum, both parts and the free space. It is an estimate, not a bound: a gzip entry declares its compressed size only. On the AIT archive it asked for 24.1 GiB; the run wrote 0.27 GiB of parts, and by its own counts 1.0 GiB of partition files and 0.1 GiB of keys; the peak use of the disk was not measured.
- 1.17: Determinism, as achieved. The same archive, scorer, options and batch size give the same manifest and the same part files byte for byte (tested through the binary: two runs, every file compared). Another batch size, or a memory limit that moves the deduplication to disk, cuts the same rows into other parts: the files and the manifest's part lists differ, the rows do not. To make that checkable, every table has `rows_sha256`: per column a SHA-256 over its values in row order, and over those the table's hash; it does not depend on the parts. Tested: equal at batch sizes 1, 7, 64 and the default, and equal between the memory path and the disk path, through the binary. Nothing in a part or in the manifest holds a time, a host figure or the work directory; the manifest holds the archive path as located. Polars writes `created_by: Polars` without a version; the bytes can change with the Polars or zstd release.
- 1.17: Memory accounting. Held from the moment `spill_into` is called: 2.1 MiB for the buffers of deduplication on disk (66 buffers of 32 KiB: 64 partitions written during the read or read during the merge, the key file, the count file). `baseline score` holds beside the archive, through `hold_beside`: the loaded scorer, hashing a text of the maximum record length and printing one (8.8 MiB in a test run, by a refusal message: a scorer of 65,536 buckets and records of up to 64 KiB). A batch's frame, its scores and the encoding of its part live between two batches and are not counted separately: they fit the memory the batch itself was allowed (6 times its text and 500 bytes a row), which is free by then; the measurements below include them. A partition's set is asked for before it is built (`table_bytes` of its row count, an upper bound of its distinct keys) and refused with the figures when it does not fit what is left. The frozen set stays in memory to the end. Not changed: the skipped records are still a list in memory (note (g) of 1.16).
- 1.17: Measured, `l2d baseline score` on `data/ait-lds-v2/russellmitchell.zip`, all entries, release binary with the feature `heap-report`, timed from outside with the working set polled every 200 ms, one run each, scorer fitted on CSIC (`baseline fit --adapter csic2010`, revision `7c2f260e3f52`); log `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\task-1.17\ait-measurement.log`, outputs `ait-all-512MB.out.txt`, `ait-all-3GB.out.txt` and their `.err.txt` beside it, work directories below `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\task-1.17\work\`. (a) `--memory-limit 512MB`: exit 0 after 487.8 s; 30,916,764 records of 11,870 entries, 357,123 skipped, 8,276,854 distinct texts (the figures of 1.16 under 3 GB); the set was frozen at 7,277,473 texts, 1,006,661 rows (1.0 GiB) went to disk and held 999,381 further distinct texts; heap peak 372,195,062 bytes (355.0 MiB, 0.74 of the 480 MiB the limit leaves), peak working set 359.1 MiB; on disk 386 score parts (277,133,027 bytes), 128 occurrence parts (9,577,139 bytes) and a manifest of 2.6 MB. This is the read that 1.16 refused. (b) `--memory-limit 3GB`: exit 0 after 395.6 s, deduplication in memory, heap peak 1,119,095,340 bytes (1,067.3 MiB), peak working set 849.3 MiB, 451 score parts (278,165,871 bytes) and 127 occurrence parts. `rows_sha256` of both tables is the same in (a) and (b): `d048ec6a...c6ec2959` for the scores, `b624cedd...818cfcde` for the occurrences. Reading alone took about 70 s in 1.16, so most of the time is scoring 4.9 GB of text and writing it; that was not broken down. Both runs were made before a lint fix in the reading of the counts (`chunks_exact` became `as_chunks`).
- 1.17: Checked on the parts of run (a). On-request test `the_parts_of_a_run_hold_one_row_per_distinct_log` in `crates/l2d-cli/tests/archive_parts.rs` (release, `L2D_PARTS_DIR`; output in `ait-parts-check-512MB.txt`): every part has the rows, bytes and SHA-256 the manifest states, the rows are numbered 0 to 8,276,853 without a gap, no text occurs twice (by 128 bits of SHA-256), the texts are 4,903,206,079 bytes (the figure of 1.16), and the 8,276,854 counts add up to 30,559,641, the records less the skipped ones. Read with pyarrow as a second reader (one-off, `uv run --with pyarrow`, `check_parts.py`, output `pyarrow-check.txt`): 386 and 128 parts of one row group each, codec ZSTD, the same row counts.
- 1.17: Measured on built archives (debug, counting allocator, `deduplication_on_disk_and_writing_parts_stay_within_the_memory_limit` in `crates/l2d-core/tests/zip_memory.rs`, output `memory-on-disk-debug.txt`): 300,000 distinct lines read twice, every frame written as a part. Short lines under limits that leave 8, 16 and 48 MiB: heap growth 4.8, 8.9 and 18.3 MiB (0.59, 0.56, 0.38), on disk at 8 and 16 MiB (set frozen at 5,709 and 224,988 texts). Lines of about 110 bytes under 12, 18 and 64 MiB: 6.3, 11.1 and 24.8 MiB (0.53, 0.62, 0.39), on disk at 12 and 18 MiB. With 8 MiB the long lines are refused before the first batch (the smallest batch of 1,024 rows at 1,100 bytes a row does not fit beside the buffers).
- 1.17: Dependencies. `polars-io` gets the features `parquet` and `zstd` (on `polars-io`, not through the facade); `windows-sys` gets `Win32_Storage_FileSystem`; `sha2` is a dev-dependency of `l2d-cli`. `Cargo.lock` grows from 263 to 284 packages, all linked into the tool: `polars-parquet` 0.52.0, `polars-parquet-format`, `polars-arrow-format` with `planus`, `array-init-cursor`, `foldhash`, `hashbrown` 0.15.5 (a fourth), `streaming-decompression`, `base64`, `zstd` 0.13.4 with `zstd-safe` and `zstd-sys` 2.1.2 (C source of zstd 1.5.7) and `jobserver`, `lz4` with `lz4-sys` 1.11.1 (C source of lz4 1.10.0), `brotli` with `brotli-decompressor`, `alloc-no-stdlib` and `alloc-stdlib`, `snap`, `zlib-rs` 0.6.8. `polars-io`'s `parquet` feature switches on all five codecs of `polars-parquet` at once; only zstd is used. No network crate: checked with `cargo tree -p l2d-core -e normal`, `cargo tree -p l2d-cli -e normal,build` (both saved in the task directory; neither names `tokio`, `reqwest`, `hyper`, `rustls`, `object_store`, `mio`, `socket2`, `futures` or an `async` crate), `cargo tree -i <crate> -e normal,build` for the new ones, and the guard test. The two C sources compile on this host in debug and release (MSVC through `cc`). Side effect to know: `polars-parquet` asks `flate2` for its `zlib-rs` backend and Cargo unifies features, so the tool's own inflating of zip and gzip entries now runs on `zlib-rs` (pure Rust) in place of `miniz_oxide` (`cargo tree -p l2d-core -e features -i flate2`, and `flate2-1.1.11/src/ffi/mod.rs`); the tests of 1.16 pass on it unchanged.
- 1.17: Tests. Written before the code they test, except `parts.rs`, which existed as a file but was not part of the crate when they were first run; the first run failed to compile because the module and the `ZipInput` methods did not exist (`red-core.txt`, `red-cli.txt` in the task directory): the evidence is a build failure, not failing assertions. Default run: `crates/l2d-core/tests/result_parts.rs` (11: parts, their hashes and the rows hash; columns and names that are refused; a work directory in use; the free-disk refusal with both figures; the manifest; equality of the disk path with the memory path at four batch sizes and six sizes of the set in memory, including none of the texts in memory; files that stay in the directory and are removed, also when a read is dropped; a set that genuinely outgrows a limit of 52 MiB; `selected_bytes`), one more test in `tests/zip_memory.rs` (now 4; the new one takes 76 s alone in a debug build, and the file took 205 and 236 s in two runs of the whole workspace, against about 40 s before), `crates/l2d-cli/tests/archive_parts.rs` (6 against the built binary: rows, counts and ratios against scoring the same texts from a file; the default directory; batch sizes and byte-for-byte repetition; 300,000 distinct lines under `--memory-limit 50MB`, which `ingest` refuses and `baseline score` completes on disk with the rows of a run under 2 GB; refusals; a damaged entry), and a unit test of the time stamp. `tests/result_parts.rs` took 47 s in the final run of the workspace. Final run (`test-final.txt`, `fmt-final.txt`, `clippy-final.txt` in the task directory): 627 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored in 62 test binaries; `cargo fmt --check` and clippy with `-D warnings` clean.
- 1.17: Deviations from the task text. (1) The deduplication set is not moved to disk: it is frozen in memory and only the texts beyond it are partitioned on disk; the partition files are raw records, not Parquet parts. (2) "Parquet parts per batch": a batch without a new text writes no part, the rows from disk are written after the last entry in frames of their own, and the occurrence counts are a second table. (3) `l2d ingest` was not given deduplication on disk: it writes nothing and still refuses when the set does not fit, which requirement 13.12 allows. (4) The check of the disk is against an estimate of parts and deduplication files together, not of the parts alone. (5) With an archive, `--limit` defaults to 20 in JSON too.
- 1.17: Design lines that no longer match (design.md): 53 says the dependency check "is repeated when task 1.17 adds its features" (done; and `flate2` now runs on `zlib-rs`); 459 ("writes records to parts partitioned by hash prefix": raw partition files, a frozen set, rows from disk after the read); 462 (one part per batch that has new texts, plus the `occurrences` table; the manifest also holds `rows_sha256`, the scorer and the entries); 463 (`<run>` is `baseline-score-<UTC time>`; the directory must be empty or absent); 464 (the estimate covers the deduplication files too); the sketch at 481 to 490 lacks `spill_into`, `hold_beside`, `selected_bytes`, `disk_dedup` and `occurrence_counts`, and `occurrences` answers for the set in memory only once the read is on disk; 345 names `PartWriter` and matches. The command table (1124) and the lines on exit codes and JSON (1138, 1140) do not name `baseline score` on an archive: its options `--work-dir`, `--entry` and `--max-record-bytes`, exit 2 after the report for a damaged entry, and two JSON documents in that case.
- 1.17: For 1.19. Deduplication on disk relies on the batches arriving in record order: a partition keeps the first row written for a key as its first occurrence, and the merge takes each partition's result as sorted by position. Parallel entries must hand their rows to `merge` in directory order, as 1.16 already asked for "first seen". The point where the set is frozen depends on the memory limit and the batch size and may depend on timing; the rows do not, as long as that order holds. The identity to compare sequential and parallel reading on is `rows_sha256` of both tables. The sequential figures to beat for scoring: 488 s under 512 MB and 396 s under 3 GB for all entries.
- 1.17: Not verified. Nothing was run on Linux, and there `free_disk_bytes` is an error, so `baseline score` on an archive stops at its disk check. A disk that fills during a run, a partition whose keys do not fit (the refusal exists and was not reached by a test), and free memory falling during a run on disk were not exercised. Only `russellmitchell.zip` was scored. The free-disk figure of the operating system was only checked to be above zero and was not compared with another reading. The estimate of the disk need was compared with one archive. `.kiro/steering/` does not exist in this repository, so no steering file was read. All timings and peaks are single runs.
- 1.17: Owner decisions of 2026-11-14. `flate2` on the `zlib-rs` backend: accepted. Five codecs linked by the Parquet feature for the one used: accepted. The disk estimate stays as it is until a second archive has been measured. The free-disk check for systems other than Windows (`statvfs`) is added later, as a follow-up outside this task; until then `baseline score` on an archive stops at the disk check there. `l2d ingest` gets the same path on disk as `baseline score`: task 1.20.
- 1.17, review (2026-11-14): approved at b2e180b, no blocking finding. The reviewer built its own archive (36 entries, 780,935 records, 584,011 distinct texts by an independent Python count) and got the same row hashes from eight runs, four in memory and four on disk, at limits from 50 MB to 2 GB and batch sizes from 300 to the default; pyarrow read the parts and matched the count row for row. A damaged entry gave exit 2 on both paths with the same rows. The AIT run under 512 MB was repeated: 604 s, heap peak 355.0 MiB, all 515 files byte-identical to the implementer's, although free memory fell during the run and the batch size changed 12 times. Findings, all advisory and taken into task 1.20 unless noted: the disk estimate is not a bound in either direction (too low for very short lines because the 32 bytes a spilled row are left out; too high for AIT, 24.1 GiB asked for 1.36 GB seen); the refusal for a partition that does not fit is reachable (a 3 MB archive of 1,000,000 lines of one partition, exit 4) and arrives only after the whole archive is read; a killed run leaves `dedup\` with raw log text and no manifest, recognisable as unfinished, and a rerun into that directory is refused (left as it is); small things listed in task 1.20. The slower test run came from `zlib-rs` unoptimized in debug builds, not from the new test alone: `archives_stay_within_their_memory_limits` took 68.5 s at 8ba2e95, 197.6 and 221.9 s at b2e180b, and 59.2 s with `[profile.dev.package.zlib-rs] opt-level = 3`, which is now in `Cargo.toml`. Not verified by the review: a disk that fills during a run, exit 5 from a failing partition write, anything on Linux, key collisions (by reading only). design.md was brought in line with the list above on 2026-11-14.
- 1.20: What `l2d ingest` does now on a zip archive. It takes the path on disk of `baseline score`, with the same code (`crates/l2d-cli/src/cmd/archive_run.rs`, new; `baseline_archive.rs` and `ingest.rs` both call it), but only when the set no longer fits (remediation below; at d03b1db the directory was made and the disk checked before every read): until the set is frozen nothing is made and no disk is looked at; at the freeze the work directory is made, the free disk space is checked against the estimate, `dedup/` below it takes the rows, and the batches are read to their end, the frames from disk included. An archive whose distinct texts outgrow the memory limit is therefore read to its end instead of being refused, when the buffers of deduplication on disk fit beside the frozen set. New option `--work-dir DIR` (on an input that is not an archive an analyst error, exit 2, as for `baseline score`); without it the directory is `<project>/work/ingest-<UTC time>-<process id>/` (the process id because nothing is kept there and two runs in one second must not meet; `baseline score` keeps `baseline-score-<UTC time>`). A directory given with `--work-dir` must be empty or absent, which is checked before the read (exit 4 otherwise, nothing touched; below a file exit 2). The estimate is the one for deduplication on disk alone, since `ingest` writes no parts. Exit codes as before: 0, 2 after the report for a damaged entry, 3, 4 for a refusal, 5.
- 1.20: What `ingest` leaves. Nothing: the files of the deduplication go when the archive is finished or dropped, and then every directory the run made is removed as far as it is empty, the directory itself and the absent ones above it (`<project>/work/` too when the run made it). A directory that was there before and empty stays, empty. The same on a refusal and on an error, because it is done when the values are dropped. Tested through the binary on success (default directory, a named absent one with an absent parent, a named empty one), on the partition refusal and on a directory in use. Not covered: a killed process leaves `dedup\` with raw log text, as a killed `baseline score` does.
- 1.20: What `ingest` prints. The `input` object is unchanged and is equal, field for field, in memory and on disk, at the default batch size and at 3,000 rows, and equal to the `input` object of `baseline score` in memory and on disk (tests, 100,000 and 300,000 distinct lines read twice). New in JSON, only when the read went to disk (remediation; at d03b1db always): `work` with `directory` (where the temporary files were), `deduplication` (`on_disk`, `set_texts`, `spilled_records`, `spilled_bytes`, `spilled_distinct`, as under `parts` of `baseline score`), `disk_free_bytes` and `disk_estimated_bytes` (the check at the freeze). The text output gains the lines `deduplication: on disk after ...` and `disk: ...` only when the read went to disk; a read that stays in memory prints what it printed before. `ingest` prints no occurrence counts, so none are read back.
- 1.20: What it cost `ingest` at d03b1db, and no longer costs after the remediation. At d03b1db every `ingest` of an archive named a directory: 3.4 MiB more were held from the first batch, the key of every row handed out was written to `keys.bin` (16 bytes a row) although `ingest` does not read the counts, and a run on a disk with less free space than the estimate was refused even when the set would have fitted the memory. Now a read that stays in memory does none of this; a read that goes to disk holds 2.1 MiB from the freeze on and writes no `keys.bin` and no `counts.bin`. Still: the frames from disk are read back although only their number is wanted.
- 1.20: API as built. `l2d-core`: `SelectedBytes::dedup_disk_bytes() -> u64` (the estimate for deduplication on disk); `WorkDir::remove_created(&self)` (removes the directories `WorkDir::create` made for this value, the deepest first, as far as they are empty); `WorkDir::create` returns `L2dError::InvalidInput` when a file is where a directory above the work directory would have to be. Private: `Spill::deduplicate(room)` no longer takes `table_bytes` as an argument; `zip_spill::{SPILL_HELD, SPILLED_ROW_BYTES, ESTIMATED_RECORD_BYTES, estimated_disk_bytes}`. `l2d-cli`: `cmd::archive_run::{ArchiveRun, RunDir, DedupReport}`: `ArchiveRun::prepare(context, input, name, given_work_dir, parts_estimate)`, `work() -> &WorkDir`, `finish() -> (ArchiveSummary, RunDir)`; changed by the remediation (note below); the archive is a field before the directory, so it is dropped first and its files are gone when the directory is removed. `JsonOut::cut_off()`. `utc_stamp` and its test moved from `baseline_archive.rs` to `archive_run.rs`.
- 1.20: The disk estimate as it is now. For deduplication on disk: the unpacked bytes of the selected entries (as before) plus 32 bytes (the header of a row on disk: key, position, count, length) for every 6 unpacked bytes. For the parts nothing changed. The number of rows is not known before an archive is read, so the 32 bytes can only be counted for an assumed record length; 6 bytes with the line end is the assumption (`ESTIMATED_RECORD_BYTES`), chosen because the archive the review found the estimate too low for has records of 7.3 bytes. It is still an estimate and not a bound: records of 2 bytes would write 16 times their unpacked bytes, the estimate allows 6.3 times. The only bound (2 bytes a record) would ask 17 times the unpacked bytes: about 248 GiB for the AIT archive, where 161 GiB were free on this host on 2026-11-14, so it was not taken. This is the owner's to confirm (report of the task). The estimate was not lowered anywhere: for the AIT archive it rises from 24.1 GiB to about 98.6 GiB (13.97 GiB unpacked by the figure of 1.17: 6.6 GiB packed and 3.5 GiB for the parts as before, 88.5 GiB for deduplication).
- 1.20: The short-line measurement (debug binary, `--memory-limit 50MB`, the reviewer's archives and scorer, one run each, `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\task-1.20\repro\base\summary.txt` before the change and `repro\head\summary.txt` after, script `scripts\repro.ps1`). `short.zip` (1,500,000 lines, 10,888,890 bytes unpacked, 7.3 bytes a record): `baseline score` wrote 57,148,908 bytes of partitions and 14,689,291 bytes of parts, 71.8 MB together, against an estimate of 75,953,175 (before: 56,428,602 and 9,139,425 against 17,879,095); `ingest` wrote 48,986,670 bytes against 68,962,970. `mix.zip` (lines of about 18 bytes): 39,418,597 and 5,816,488 against 110,803,483 (before: 38,352,882 and 3,681,481 against 23,685,435). Seen there and not changed, because the task leaves the rest of the estimate alone: the estimate for the parts by itself (6,990,321 bytes for `short.zip`) is below the parts written (14.7 MB); the sum holds because the deduplication part has room. Tests: `the_disk_estimate_of_deduplication_holds_for_short_lines` (`l2d-core`, 400,000 lines of 6.7 bytes, the bytes in the deduplication directory looked at after every batch and the partitions by their own count) and `the_disk_estimate_holds_for_an_archive_of_short_lines` (binary, both commands).
- 1.20: Early partition refusal (as built at d03b1db; the remediation below decides it by distinct keys, not rows, and checks between batches). `Spill::spill` is given the room a partition's set may take (what the limit leaves beside what the archive holds and the fixed 4 MiB of a batch, the same figure the deduplication after the read uses, which can only have shrunk by then) and refuses the row that would make the partition's table larger than that. The message is the one of 1.17 (partition, rows, memory needed, memory left) with `; stopped after N records of M entries` added. The check after the last entry stays, for a room that shrank. On the reviewer's `hostile.zip` (1,000,000 lines, all in partition 0) under 50 MB: before, exit 4 after 19.7 s and the whole archive read, 972,653 rows in the partition; after, exit 4 after 0.9 s, at row 28,673 of the partition, 36,345 records read; `ingest` on it: exit 4 after 4.6 s at row 114,689. Tests: `a_partition_whose_keys_do_not_fit_is_refused_while_the_archive_is_read` (`l2d-core`, 70,000 lines chosen by the first byte of their SHA-256, no text in memory: `Refused`, the figures in the message, fewer records read than the archive holds, the directory gone) and `a_partition_whose_keys_do_not_fit_is_refused_and_leaves_whole_documents` (binary, 200,000 such lines: `ingest` exit 4 with nothing left of a named and of the default directory; `baseline score --json` exit 4 with two whole documents and only `scores-*.parquet` left). The limits in the binary test (44MB for `ingest`, 53MB for `baseline score`; 41MB and 51MB after the remediation) are chosen so that the set is frozen and the partition then outgrows the room; they depend on the constants of the memory model and need a new choice when those change (the message is asserted, so a wrong choice fails visibly).
- 1.20: Test shapes on request. `deduplication_on_disk_and_writing_parts_stay_within_the_memory_limit` (`crates/l2d-core/tests/zip_memory.rs`; the task text calls it `..._their_limits`) keeps one shape in the default run: 300,000 lines of about 110 bytes read twice under a limit that leaves 18 MiB. It stayed because it is the shape that goes to disk with lines as long as a log's and the one whose heap came nearest to its limit (0.62 of it in 1.17, 0.65 now). The five others are `the_other_shapes_of_deduplication_on_disk_stay_within_the_memory_limit`, `#[ignore]`: `cargo test -j 4 -p l2d-core --test zip_memory -- --ignored --nocapture the_other_shapes`. Run once after the change (debug, `memory-on-disk-others-debug.txt` in the task directory, 58 s): short lines under 9, 16 and 48 MiB: heap growth 4.5, 6.5 and 18.5 MiB (0.50, 0.41, 0.39 of the limit), on disk at 9 and 16; long lines under 12 and 64 MiB: 5.3 and 24.8 MiB (0.44, 0.39), on disk at 12; the default shape: 11.8 MiB (0.65). The smallest limit of the short lines moved from 8 to 9 MiB: with the 1.3 MiB now charged for the counts the read is refused at 8 MiB before its first batch (8.4 MiB needed).
- 1.20: Small things from the review. (a) The directory given with `--work-dir` is removed again when the run made it and was refused, with the directories it made above it; done for both commands by `RunDir` in `archive_run.rs`, which calls `WorkDir::remove_created` when it is dropped. A directory that holds parts is not empty and stays, so a run of `baseline score` that fails after its first part still leaves its parts without a manifest; one that fails before its first part now leaves no directory. (b) `--work-dir` below an existing file: exit 2, `the work directory ... cannot be made: ... is a file, not a directory (give another --work-dir)`; before: exit 5 with `os error 183`. A work directory that is itself a file is still the refusal of 1.17 (exit 4, "not an empty directory"). (c) What `occurrence_counts` holds is charged: `SPILL_HELD` is the 66 buffers of 1.17 (2.1 MiB) and 1.3 MiB for a chunk of 65,536 keys, its 65,536 counts and the buffers of the two files read; 3,538,944 bytes, held from `spill_into` to the end, and named in the refusals as "the buffers of deduplication on disk and of its occurrence counts". (Remediation: `ingest`, which reads no counts, holds the 66 buffers alone, 2,162,688 bytes, and only from the freeze.) A consequence: under the same limit the set is frozen earlier than before (`mix.zip` under 50 MB: at 5,886 texts, before at 26,856; 422 score parts, before 204); the rows are the same. (d) A refusal after the first row with `--json`: `baseline score` on an archive ends the document it began (`]` for the list, then `"incomplete":true`, then `}`) and the error envelope follows on the next line, so standard output holds whole documents only. `incomplete` is a new field, there so that the cut document is not taken for a finished one. Only this command does it; `baseline score` on other inputs and `triage` still cut their document off as `output.rs` describes.

- 1.20: Deviations from the task text. (1) The estimate counts the 32 bytes for an assumed record of 6 bytes, not for the rows of the archive, which are not known before the read: it holds for the two archives of the review and for the tests, and is not a bound (above). (2) The test the task names `..._stay_within_their_limits` is `..._stay_within_the_memory_limit`; its smallest shape on request moved from 8 to 9 MiB. - 1.20: Design lines that no longer match (design.md, not edited). 459: deduplication on disk is taken by `l2d ingest` too, and "A partition whose keys do not fit in memory is a refusal" now happens during the read, decided by the partition's distinct keys (remediation); `ingest` makes its directory and checks the disk only at the freeze, and `baseline score` checks the disk for deduplication on disk at the freeze. 464: `<run>` is also `ingest-<UTC time>-<process id>`; what the run made of the directory is removed again when it is left empty (`ingest` always, `baseline score` when it wrote no part). 465: the estimate for the deduplication files is the unpacked bytes plus 32 bytes per 6 of them; "which task 1.20 corrects" is done for the archives measured, under the assumption above. The sketch from 482 on lacks `SelectedBytes::dedup_disk_bytes` and, for `WorkDir`, `remove_created` and the analyst error below a file. The command table (1142): `l2d ingest` has `--work-dir`, `--entry` and `--max-record-bytes` on an archive and prints `work` when the read went to disk. 1159: a run of `baseline score` on an archive that fails after output has begun ends its document with `"incomplete":true` before the error envelope; and `ingest` on an archive that is refused during the read prints the envelope alone.
- 1.20: For 1.19. (a) Both commands prepare the read in `archive_run.rs` (`ArchiveRun::prepare` for `baseline score`, `spill_when_needed` for `ingest`) and read through `ZipInput::batches`; parallel entries belong below that, in `ZipInput`. (b) The rows of a partition are written as `merge` takes them, and its distinct keys are counted between batches (remediation); `merge` must stay on one thread and take the batches in directory order (as 1.17 asked for the first occurrence). The refusal itself then depends on the distinct keys of a partition and the room only; the `stopped after N records` in its message depends on when it is counted. (c) `SPILL_HELD` (3.4 MiB; 2.1 MiB for `ingest`, from the freeze) is held once per archive, not per reader. The freeze of `ingest` happens between batches, or in a batch only into the room the batch kept for the set's growth: a parallel reader must keep that. (d) The identities to compare a parallel read on: `rows_sha256` of both tables for `baseline score`, the `input` object for `ingest`. (e) Sequential figures for `ingest` under 512 MB: 74.7 s for all entries, 39.5 s for `*.log`.

The free-disk refusal was not reached through the binary (a disk cannot be made to look full there): the refusal is tested in `l2d-core` with a given figure, the removal of the directory through other refusals. A disk that fills during a run, exit 5 from a failing partition write, a killed process, and free memory falling during a read on disk were not exercised. The most bytes in `dedup\` at one time were not measured reliably: a directory listing lags for files that are open (the poll saw 1,073,661,462 bytes on the AIT archive, the tool counts 1.0 GiB of partitions and writes 116 MB of keys beside them); in `the_disk_estimate_of_deduplication_holds_for_short_lines` the figure from the listing has the same weakness, the figure from the tool's count does not. With all rows in one partition the directory holds the partition and its deduplicated copy together, up to twice the text; the estimate does not count that, and such an archive is refused early unless the memory is large. The estimate was compared with the reviewer's two archives, the test archives and the AIT archive (88.3 GiB asked, about 1.2 GiB used), not with a second real archive. `incomplete` was only seen on the partition refusal. `.kiro/steering/` does not exist in this repository. All timings and peaks are single runs.

- 1.20, remediation after the review of d03b1db (2026-11-14): The review approved d03b1db with advisory findings; the owner decided: (1) `ingest` uses the disk only when it is needed; (2) the disk estimate keeps its 6 bytes a record and is measured later; (3) the refusal of a partition counts distinct keys, not rows, in this round. Extending `"incomplete"` to `triage` and to `baseline score` on plain inputs is a later task and was not touched; nothing of `statvfs` or of 1.19. Work, scripts and outputs: `D:\BuildArtifacts\log2decision\task-1.20\remediation\` (`scripts\repro.ps1`, `scripts\run.ps1`, `repro-before.txt`, `repro-before-parts.txt`, `repro-after.txt`, `repro-after-big.txt`, `out\`; the before binary is d03b1db in debug, the after binary this commit in debug, big.zip and AIT in release).
- 1.20, remediation, item 1 (`ingest` and the disk). `ZipInput::spill_when_needed(work_dir, max_set_texts)` (`zip_input.rs`) names the work directory without making it: until the set is frozen the read is the read without a directory (nothing made, no disk looked at, nothing more held, the batches cut as at e7cbbce). At the freeze, decided by the same rule as with a directory (the set's next growth no longer fits beside the smallest batch; with no buffers held before it the set gets further than with a directory), the directory is made (`WorkDir::create`), the free disk is checked (`ZipInput::check_disk_at_freeze`), and `Spill::create_in` takes `dedup/` below it; the read goes on on disk, and the files and what was made are removed when the archive is finished or dropped (`Spill` owns the `WorkDir` and calls `remove_created`). Such a read keeps no occurrence counts (`ingest` reads none): no `keys.bin`, no `counts.bin`, and it holds the 66 file buffers alone from the freeze on, sized then to what is left beside the smallest batch, 32 KiB each down to 4 KiB (`zip_spill::buffer_for`; 2.1 MiB at most, 264 KiB at least). The sizing was added after the first after-run: with 32 KiB buffers taken at the freeze, `ingest` of the review's `dup.zip` under 42 MB completed at batch sizes 10 and 64 and was refused at 1,024 and the default ("the smallest batch of 1024 rows needs 728.0 KiB ... only 319.4 KiB is available"), because the set had grown into the memory the buffers of d03b1db held from the start; reserving that memory before the freeze would have brought back finding (c). A freeze inside a batch takes only the room the batch kept for the set's growth (when even the smallest buffers do not fit there, the growth is refused as without a directory); the usual freeze is between batches. A refusal at the freeze (the directory cannot be made, too little disk, the buffers do not fit) is a clean exit with the records read in the message, nothing left, and with `--json` the error envelope alone (`ingest` prints nothing before its end). `--work-dir` the analyst gives is checked before the read (`WorkDir::check`: below a file exit 2, not empty exit 4); the default directory is not, so that a project whose `work` cannot be made reads as at e7cbbce until the freeze. `ingest` prints `work` (JSON) and the two lines of the text output only when the read went to disk, with the disk figures of the check at the freeze. CLI: `archive_run::spill_when_needed`; `ingest.rs` no longer uses `ArchiveRun`.
- 1.20, remediation, item 1 for `baseline score` (decision). Its parts need the work directory from the first batch, and its occurrence counts are read out through `keys.bin`, which must hold the key of every row from the first batch on; so the directory, `keys.bin` and the 3.4 MiB stay from the start, and the memory model of the command is unchanged (its parts are byte-identical to d03b1db, below). Only the deduplication part of the disk check moved: before the read the disk is checked for the parts alone; at the freeze for deduplication on disk with the whole parts estimate again beside it (`FreezeDiskCheck::beside`). Why: the same rule as `ingest` (the disk for deduplication is asked for when deduplication goes to disk), and a run that stays in memory is no longer refused for the disk deduplication would need (big.zip-like archives of few distinct texts). The check at the freeze asks the parts estimate whole although some parts are written by then, so it is never weaker than the check of d03b1db, only later; the cost is that a run refused there has written parts until then (no manifest, `incomplete` with `--json`, as for the partition refusal). The `disk_free_bytes` and `disk_estimated_bytes` of `parts` are those of the last check: for `mix.zip` scored in memory (2 GB) 7,350,799 bytes estimated after, 110,803,483 before.
- 1.20, remediation, the review's findings (a) to (d), before (d03b1db) and after, single runs. (a) Project directory in which no directory can be made (deny of "add subdirectory" on the project, `ingest` of `mix.zip`, default limit): before exit 5 ("creating the work directory ... Access is denied. (os error 5)"); after exit 0, the text report equal to the reviewer's e7cbbce output but for the memory lines, nothing made. (b) `big.zip` (21.5 GB unpacked, one distinct text): the review saw exit 4 at once at d03b1db (126.7 GiB asked, 109.2 GiB free); on this host 142.1 GiB were free, so d03b1db did not refuse and read on (stopped by hand after 281 s, which left its directory, as a killed run does); after (release, `--memory-limit 512MB` as the review): exit 0 after 96 s, 358,144 records, 1 distinct text, no `work`, no directory seen, heap peak 207,479,357 bytes (e7cbbce in the review: 207,479,150). (c) `--entry *.gz` on `mix.zip` under 40 MB: before exit 4 ("needs 8.4 MiB ... leaves 8.0 MiB"); after exit 0, 25,000 records, the `input` object equal to e7cbbce's and the batch plan equal but for the free-memory figures. (d) `keys.bin` for archives that fit (the default directory polled every 40 ms): before 9,338,880 bytes seen for `mix.zip` and 23,986,176 for `short.zip`; after no directory at all, for both.
- 1.20, remediation, item 3 (partition room by distinct keys). `Spill::spill` no longer refuses. Between batches (`Spill::check_partitions`, called at the end of `merge` on a frozen set, after the batch's frame is let go, so that its memory is the room) every partition has an upper bound of its distinct keys: those counted at its last count plus the rows written since. When the bound is above what the room holds (`keys_fitting`: the most keys whose table fits the room) and the partition's rows have at least doubled since its last count, its file is read and its keys counted with a set made for at most that many keys and never grown (`distinct_keys`); more keys than that is the refusal ("partition N of 64 holds more than K distinct keys (in R rows on disk), and the set of their keys needs ..., but ... is left ...; stopped after N records of M entries"). The post-read deduplication reserves the smaller of the rows and that many keys and refuses on the first key beyond it. The refusal therefore depends on the distinct keys of a partition and the room, not on the rows; the count is exact when taken, the bound between counts is sound (a key is counted once at most per row), and the doubling bounds all counts of a partition to about twice its final file. Memory of a count: the set takes at most the room, which nothing uses between batches; its file is read through a buffer nothing uses during the read (a buffer of the occurrence counts, or of the key file a read without counts does not have). What still depends on the batch size: the point of the freeze (which texts the set holds) moves by at most one batch, as in 1.17; the refusal for rows is gone.
